# Linear archive — closed issues

This file is the archive of every **closed** issue (state `Done` or `Canceled`)
from the `Reely` project on the `Side projects` Linear team, captured on
2026-08-07 when the tracker moved to GitHub Issues.

Closed issues were not recreated as GitHub issues. They carry no operational
value to the Factory loop — nothing picks them up, nothing blocks on them — but
their cross-references are load-bearing: open issues cite them by identifier for
the decisions and the reasoning recorded here. So they are archived whole, in
one committed file, and every closed identifier keeps a stable anchor.

**Linking into this file.** Each issue heading is preceded by a raw HTML anchor
whose id is the issue identifier in lower case, so any closed issue is
addressable by fragment — for example
`docs/archive/linear-issues.md#sidepro-135`.

**Reading the cross-references.** Every `SIDEPRO-nnn` reference to an issue in
this project is resolved. One that points at another closed issue links to its
anchor here. One that points at an issue still **open** at capture time links to
the GitHub issue it was recreated as, under `pedrosousa13/reely`. References
inside code blocks, inside existing links, and inside the per-issue Linear URLs
are left verbatim.

One reference resolves to nothing and is left bare: `SIDEPRO-56`, a stale
relation pointing into a different Linear project, which was never part of this
export and has no counterpart to link to.

Issue count: **55** — 52 Done,
3 Canceled. Ordered by issue number.
Descriptions and comments are reproduced verbatim, with Linear's inline issue
markup flattened to plain identifiers.

## SIDEPRO-134 — Restore the scheduled @real run against live YouTube/Vimeo SDKs to green

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Completed:** 2026-07-28
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-134/restore-the-scheduled-real-run-against-live-youtubevimeo-sdks-to-green

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*
> Migrated from GitHub issue [pedrosousa13/reely#116](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/116>) on 2026-07-28.

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug
**Summary:** The scheduled `@real` e2e run against live YouTube/Vimeo SDKs failed; determine cause and restore green.

**Current behavior:**
[Run 30339715094](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/actions/runs/30339715094>) failed. Unknown whether it's an adapter regression or a third-party outage / rate limit / consent interstitial. The run page carries a Playwright HTML report and a trace for the retried attempt.

**Desired behavior:**
The `@real` suite is green again. Transient failures are confirmed by rerun; genuine regressions are diagnosed to a provider adapter and fixed.

**Key interfaces:**

* The `@real`-tagged Playwright suite; reproduce locally with:

  ```sh
  REELY_REAL_PROVIDERS=1 pnpm test:e2e --project=chromium --grep @real
  ```

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Rerun performed first, before investigating.
- [ ] If green after rerun → close with the rerun link (transient).
- [ ] If still red → root cause identified per provider; fix lands through the normal gate; suite green after.

**Out of scope:**

* Restructuring the provider adapters (tracked separately as the `create*Provider` closures issue).
* Changing the workflow's failure-reporting mechanism (tracked separately).

## Original report

The scheduled `@real` run failed: [https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/actions/runs/30339715094](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/actions/runs/30339715094>)

These tests talk to third parties, so the first question is whether this is a genuine adapter regression or an outage, a rate limit, or a consent interstitial on their end. Re-run the job before investigating — the run page carries a Playwright HTML report and a trace for the retried attempt.

Close this issue once the run is green again: the workflow comments on an open issue rather than filing a new one, so leaving a stale one open silences the next report. (Note: the GitHub original is closed by this migration — see the follow-up issue about repointing the workflow's reporting at Linear.)

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-134-restore-the-scheduled-real-run-against-live-youtubevimeo`, branched from `main` at `204bcc3`.

First step per the brief: rerun the failed scheduled `@real` run before investigating.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/119 (squash-merged, all required checks green).

**Root cause — neither branch of the brief applied.** The rerun was performed first, as asked, and failed identically (`11 passed / 1 failed`, three attempts). So not transient. Comparing the CI and local Playwright network traces:

| | `/youtubei/v1/player` | `googlevideo.com/videoplayback` | Outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Local | 200 | 3 requests | plays |
| CI | 200 | **zero** | never starts |

On a GitHub-hosted runner YouTube answers the player API and then serves no stream at all, so the player never requests media and playback never starts. The adapter reports a recoverable `blocked`, which is the honest answer to being refused. Last green run `bb2cc40`, first red `204bcc3`, with no `provider-youtube` change between them — so not an adapter regression either. YouTube declines to serve video to datacenter IPs, and nothing on our side can move that.

**Maintainer decision**: drop the `@real` provider tests from CI rather than simulate a video or runtime-skip, for **both YouTube and Vimeo** even though Vimeo currently passes. The specs stay in-tree and runnable locally behind `REELY_REAL_PROVIDERS=1`; the scheduled workflow is retired.

**What shipped**: deleted `.github/workflows/real-providers.yml`. Since `playwright.config.ts` already inverts `@real` out of every run unless `REELY_REAL_PROVIDERS` is set, and this workflow was the only thing that set it, deleting it was sufficient — `ci.yml` and `visual-baselines.yml` neither set it nor mention `@real`. The `buffered-real` and `youtube-real` comments now record why, with the measurement.

**Verification**: typecheck, lint, format clean; 818 unit tests pass; all 12 `@real` tests pass locally, including the one that cannot pass in CI.

**Two follow-ups, neither blocking:**

1. The `real-providers` GitHub label is now orphaned — its only consumer was the deleted workflow. Left in place rather than removed unasked.
2. Rerunning the old workflow caused it to file GitHub issue #118 rather than comment on the original #116, because the Linear migration had closed #116. PR #119 closes #118. That fallback is the behaviour the "repoint the workflow's reporting at Linear" follow-up covers — and it is now moot, since the workflow is gone.

## SIDEPRO-135 — Provider adapters: the create*Provider closures are 574-783 lines each

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Completed:** 2026-08-02
- **Assignee:** unassigned
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-135/provider-adapters-the-createprovider-closures-are-574-783-lines-each

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-196](#sidepro-196) — Decide how provider-specific seams fit the glossary's seam enumeration
  - [SIDEPRO-186](#sidepro-186) — Split the react package's 2,780-line entry module into primitive-family modules
  - [SIDEPRO-187](#sidepro-187) — Split the core package's entry module into vocabulary, detection, and store modules

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*
> Migrated from GitHub issue [pedrosousa13/reely#108](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/108>) on 2026-07-28. Original body below, with `#N` references rewritten to GitHub links. Labeled `needs-triage`: the issue's own status note says this is now a decision, not a wait — including `wontfix` as a legitimate outcome — and that decision belongs to the maintainer.

> **Status, 2026-07-27 — the stated gate has opened; this is now a decision, not****
****a wait.** "When to pick this up" below says *after the release candidate ships**
**([#18](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/18>))*. **#18 closed on 2026-07-27**, so that condition is met. The issue keeps
> its `post-mvp` label because nothing here is user-visible and its own scope
> note still stands: if the adapters are comfortable to work in, closing this
> `wontfix` is a legitimate outcome. The prerequisites below — `@real` green
> before and after per provider, one provider per PR, extract by lifecycle seam —
> are unchanged and are what make it safe.

Recorded from the [#101](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/101>) security and simplification review ([review comment](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/101#issuecomment-5091953174>)), which found this and deliberately did not fix it. Filing so the measurement survives past that comment.

## The finding

Each provider package puts effectively its whole implementation inside one `create*Provider` closure, running from its opening line to the end of the file:

| Package | File | Closure | Lines |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| `provider-hls` | `src/index.ts` | `createHlsProvider` | 783 (270-1052) |
| `provider-vimeo` | `src/index.ts` | `createVimeoProvider` | 724 (459-1182) |
| `provider-native` | `src/index.ts` | `createNativeProvider` | 627 (200-826) |
| `provider-youtube` | `src/index.ts` | `createYouTubeProvider` | 574 (296-869) |

Everything inside shares one lexical scope, so any state is reachable from any handler and the units inside are not separately testable. The cost is navigability and test granularity, not correctness.

## Why it was not fixed in #101

Restructuring four working adapters immediately before a release candidate is a bad trade. All four currently pass the `@real` provider suite 12/12 against the live SDKs, and that suite is the only thing that can catch a regression in the parts of these files that unit tests do not reach.

## When to pick this up

After the release candidate ships ([#18](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/18>)). Prerequisites for doing it safely:

* The `@real` suite green before and after, per provider, not just at the end.
* One provider per PR. There is no shared abstraction to extract first — the four adapters differ enough that a common base would be speculative.
* Extract by lifecycle seam (load / playback / tracks / teardown), not by line count.

## Scope note

This is a structural observation, not a bug report. If the adapters are still comfortable to work in when the RC is out, closing this as `wontfix` is a legitimate outcome.

**Comments** (4)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Closed as `wontfix`, 2026-07-29 — maintainer decision

The release-candidate gate this issue was waiting behind has opened, and the decision it asked for is: leave the adapters as they are. The issue named this a legitimate outcome itself.

The reasoning is recorded in **`.out-of-scope/provider-adapter-decomposition.md`** (PR #127), so it survives this issue being closed and a future triage pass surfaces it instead of re-litigating the same measurement. In short:

- The cost is navigability and test granularity, not correctness.
- There is no non-speculative target to extract *to* — the four adapters differ enough that a shared base would be invented to justify the split.
- The `@real` suite that would guard such a refactor **no longer runs on a schedule** (retired in `8985e60`, because a hosted runner's IP reputation rather than our code decided the result). Its safety net is now manual, which raises the price of the change.

**What would reopen it:** concrete friction — a provider bug that takes an unreasonable time to locate because of the scope sharing, or a behaviour that genuinely cannot be tested without splitting the closure. Line count alone will not; that measurement has already been taken and deliberately not acted on.

The prerequisites recorded here (`@real` green before and after per provider, one provider per PR, extraction by lifecycle seam) still stand and still apply if it is ever picked up.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

## Reopened, 2026-07-30 — maintainer decision

The `wontfix` recorded here on 2026-07-29 is reversed. As part of a codebase-wide maintainability pass (the entry-module splits in [SIDEPRO-186](#sidepro-186) and [SIDEPRO-187](#sidepro-187)), the maintainer has decided the provider adapters should be decomposed after all, accepting the cost the closing comment weighed: the `@real` suite runs only manually now, and that manual run is a hard gate on this work, not a formality.

Nothing changed in the measurement — what changed is the decision. The prerequisites recorded on this issue remain binding:

- `@real` green before and after, per provider, run locally. A provider whose baseline cannot be made green stops there and reports.
- One provider per PR.
- Extraction by lifecycle seam, with explicit dependencies — never by line count, and still no shared cross-provider abstraction.

This issue is now the umbrella; the work lives in one child issue per provider:

- [SIDEPRO-188](#sidepro-188) — native (first: simplest adapter, proves the pattern; also rewrites the repo's out-of-scope record so repo and tracker agree)
- [SIDEPRO-189](#sidepro-189) — HLS
- [SIDEPRO-190](#sidepro-190) — YouTube
- [SIDEPRO-191](#sidepro-191) — Vimeo (last: most SDK-quirk surface)

**What would re-close it:** the first child failing its `@real` baseline gate with no reasonable path to green — that is the closing comment's risk argument proven out, and it should be weighed again rather than overridden.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

All four children have landed, one provider per PR, each on its own `@real` gate:

- [SIDEPRO-188](#sidepro-188) — native → PR #144 (627 → 123 lines)
- [SIDEPRO-189](#sidepro-189) — HLS → PR #146 (1,062 → 427)
- [SIDEPRO-190](#sidepro-190) — YouTube → PR #147 (869 → 135)
- [SIDEPRO-191](#sidepro-191) — Vimeo → PR #148 (1,234 → 154)

Every adapter is now split by seam with explicit dependencies, no cross-provider abstraction was introduced, and every package's boundary test file is byte-identical to what it was before its decomposition. PR #145 settled the vocabulary (**Seam** and **Attachment** in CONTEXT.md) partway through.

Two things this issue did not settle, left deliberately open:

- **The seam enumeration.** HLS, YouTube and Vimeo each needed provider-specific seams beyond CONTEXT.md's four (`error-recovery`, `quality-levels`, `time-updates`, `chromeless-availability`). That is [SIDEPRO-196](#sidepro-196)'s question.
- **The YouTube half of the `@real` gate is currently unrunnable from this machine** — YouTube answers every embed from its egress IP with iframe-API error 150, proven independent of any Reely code ([SIDEPRO-191](#sidepro-191)'s landing comment carries the evidence). The Vimeo PR landed on a per-provider reading of the gate, which is how this issue words it.

Leaving the state of this umbrella to the maintainer rather than closing it from a loop session.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-02*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Closing as done. Every child of this umbrella has landed, and the decomposition is verifiable in the code rather than only in the issue history:

| Package | Closure was | `index.ts` now | Modules |
| -- | -- | -- | -- |
| `provider-hls` | 783 lines | 427 | 7 |
| `provider-vimeo` | 724 lines | 154 | 9 |
| `provider-native` | 627 lines | 123 | 6 |
| `provider-youtube` | 574 lines | 135 | 8 |

Landed as [SIDEPRO-188](#sidepro-188) (native), [SIDEPRO-189](#sidepro-189) (HLS), [SIDEPRO-190](#sidepro-190) (YouTube) and [SIDEPRO-191](#sidepro-191) (Vimeo) — one provider per PR, extracted by lifecycle seam, exactly as the prerequisites here required.

The scope note allowed `wontfix` as a legitimate outcome if the adapters turned out comfortable to work in. That is not what happened: the work was done, so this closes as completed rather than cancelled.

One follow-up outlives this issue: [SIDEPRO-196](#sidepro-196) asks how the provider-specific seams the decompositions introduced fit the glossary's seam enumeration. That question is tracked there, not here.

## SIDEPRO-136 — Adaptive classic preset (DefaultPlayer)

- **State:** Canceled
- **Labels:** `wontfix`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Assignee:** unassigned
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-136/adaptive-classic-preset-defaultplayer

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) — Reely MVP: product contract and delivery tracker

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*
> Migrated from GitHub issue [pedrosousa13/reely#9](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/9>) on 2026-07-28. Original body below, with `#N` references rewritten to GitHub links. Labeled `needs-triage`: deferred out of the MVP by owner decision; whether and when to pick it up post-RC is a maintainer call.

> **Status, 2026-07-27 — out of the MVP; every listed blocker is closed.**
> `DefaultPlayer` was
> [deferred out of the MVP](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/1#issuecomment-5078544716>):
> the MVP ships headless primitives only, and [#32](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/32>) was retargeted at the
> primitives plus the composed reference example ([#67](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/67>)) rather than at this
> preset. The "Blocked by" list below ([#8](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/8>), [#6](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/6>), [#7](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/7>)) is stale — all three are
> closed — so nothing technical is holding this; it is out of scope by choice.
> The narrow-layout behaviour described below overlaps what the reference example
> now does with container queries (see [#114](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/114>) / [#115](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/115>)), which is worth reading before
> building the preset's own version.

## Parent

[#1](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/1>)

## Context for the implementer

`DefaultPlayer` is the batteries-included player, and it must be built **from the same public primitives consumers get** — if the preset needs a private hook, the primitives API has a gap that must be fixed first. That constraint is the point of the preset: it proves the public API is sufficient.

Layout is the "adaptive classic" design (locked):

* Viewport contains poster/media, a centered play affordance when paused, status overlays, and a bottom control surface.
* Desktop row: play, time, seek slider, mute/volume, captions, settings, PiP, fullscreen.
* Narrow layouts: play, current time, seek slider, mute, overflow menu, fullscreen. Lower-priority controls move into an accessible menu driven by **container queries** — the player adapts to its container, never user-agent sniffing.
* Controls auto-hide only while playing, and never while keyboard focus, an open menu, scrubbing, an error, or a blocked-autoplay action is active.

## What to build

* `DefaultPlayer` in the separate `@reely/react/preset` entry (JS only — theme CSS is the next issue and stays a separate import).
* Wire the poster input forms from the poster issue into the preset `poster` prop: `string | ResponsivePoster | React.ReactElement`, plus `nativePoster`. Extend the Next.js fixture to cover the preset path.
* Container-query responsive behavior + overflow menu.
* Auto-hide with every hold condition above.
* Centered play affordance; blocked-autoplay state shows it prominently.
* Icon replacement at two levels: per-control children and a preset-wide icon map via `Player.IconProvider`.
* Preset accepts the activation props (`loading`, `loadMargin`, `preload`) and autoplay values, all pass-through to the primitives.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] Failing-first responsive browser tests: wide layout, narrow layout, coarse-pointer sizing, safe-area insets, auto-hide, menu-open hold, scrubbing hold, error hold, blocked-autoplay hold.
- [ ] Preset imports only public primitives (lint/test guard, no private imports).
- [ ] All three poster forms + `nativePoster` work through the preset; Next fixture covers the preset `poster` prop with a server-rendered Next Image.
- [ ] Overflow menu is keyboard- and screen-reader-accessible; focus returns predictably on close.
- [ ] Icon override works per-control and preset-wide.
- [ ] `@reely/react/preset` entry stays within the 10 KB gzip budget.
- [ ] Docs: preset quick start, prop reference, responsive behavior notes.
- [ ] Stories (per [#19](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/19>) conventions) for wide, narrow, menu-open, auto-hide, and blocked-autoplay preset states using the mock controller.

## Out of scope

Theme CSS and the full a11y/state matrix hardening (next issue), captions rendering (captions issue).

## Verification

```sh
pnpm --filter @reely/react test && pnpm test:e2e -- --grep preset && pnpm test:integrations && pnpm test:bundle
```

## Blocked by

* [#8](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/8>), [#6](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/6>), [#7](<https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/7>) (all since closed — see status note above)

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*
> Two comments carried over from [GitHub #9](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/9), originally posted by pedrosousa13.

Deferred: DefaultPlayer is the capstone that composes the primitives, so it should land after the primitive set is complete. It needs an icon set + accessible settings/overflow menu ([#31](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/31)), an error primitive ([#34](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/34)), and captions ([#15](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/15)) — building those inside the preset would duplicate/pre-empt that primitive work. Re-blocking behind #31, #34, #15. Design notes captured for when it's picked up.

The label was contradicted by three independent records: sessions 12 and 13 both recorded this as out of MVP, and [#101](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/101)'s body listed it as out of scope. The label is what a fresh agent reads first, so the contradiction kept getting carried forward — four consecutive session handoffs flagged it without anyone taking the five-second action.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Closed as `wontfix` — a deferral, not a rejection, 2026-07-29

`DefaultPlayer` was deferred out of the MVP by owner decision: the MVP ships headless primitives only, and the WCAG verification pass was retargeted at the primitives plus the composed reference example rather than at this preset. Nothing technical blocks it — the three issues in the stale "Blocked by" list all closed — so this is out of scope by choice, not by dependency.

Closing it clears the triage bucket, which was the point: an issue that cannot be evaluated until an unshipped milestone lands does not belong in `needs-triage` indefinitely.

**Deliberately no `.out-of-scope/` record.** That knowledge base is for durable rejections; its own rules say a temporary circumstance is a deferral rather than a rejection, and filing one here would poison the deduplication checks with a rejection that was never made. This issue is the record.

**To revisit:** reopen once the MVP ships. Two things are worth reading first —

- The narrow-layout behaviour described in the body overlaps what the reference example now does with container queries, so the preset should not re-derive it.
- The constraint that makes the preset worth building is that it must compose only **public** primitives; if it needs a private hook, that is a gap in the primitives API to fix first, not a licence to reach inside.

<a id="sidepro-140"></a>

## SIDEPRO-140 — Repoint the real-providers workflow's failure reporting at Linear

- **State:** Canceled
- **Labels:** `wontfix`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Assignee:** unassigned
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-140/repoint-the-real-providers-workflows-failure-reporting-at-linear

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed during the GitHub → Linear issue migration (2026-07-28).

## The gap

`.github/workflows/real-providers.yml` reports scheduled `@real` suite failures by commenting on an **open GitHub issue**, filing a new GitHub issue if none is open. With the tracker moved to Linear, the next scheduled failure will file a fresh GitHub issue that nothing watches.

## Options to evaluate

* Repoint the workflow at Linear (e.g. create/comment on a Linear issue via Linear's API with a workspace API key stored as a repo secret).
* Keep GitHub as the automated-report surface and accept a manual migration step when a report appears.
* A hybrid: keep filing on GitHub but have the workflow title/label make the cross-tracker hop obvious.

The maintainer should decide the direction before this becomes implementable.

**Comments** (1)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Closed — the premise no longer holds, 2026-07-29

This issue asks where `.github/workflows/real-providers.yml` should report scheduled `@real` failures now that the tracker is Linear. **That workflow no longer exists.** It was deleted in `8985e60` ("Retire the scheduled real-provider run"), which is on `main`; the file is gone and no scheduled job files anything anywhere.

The retirement was decided for the same reason this issue was filed. From that commit:

> A check that cannot pass, and whose failure says nothing about the code, is worse than no check: it files an issue every night that someone has to read and dismiss.

The `@real` specs themselves are untouched — still tagged `@real`, still gated behind `REELY_REAL_PROVIDERS=1` in `playwright.config.ts`, which is what makes deleting the workflow enough to leave them local-only. They remain the only guard against YouTube and Vimeo changing their APIs, and `buffered-real` and `youtube-real` now say plainly that the guard runs only when someone runs it, and to run it when touching a provider adapter.

So there is nothing to repoint. No `.out-of-scope/` record: this is resolved work, not a rejected request, and recording it there would register a false rejection.

**If a scheduled real-provider run is ever wanted again**, reporting into Linear is the right target — but that is a new issue about reinstating the schedule on infrastructure where it can actually pass, not this one.

<a id="sidepro-150"></a>

## SIDEPRO-150 — Media's provider branches disagree on geometry and on the style prop

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Completed:** 2026-07-28
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-150/medias-provider-branches-disagree-on-geometry-and-on-the-style-prop

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug

**Summary:** `Player.Media` renders three different branches (native `<video>`, YouTube mount, Vimeo mount) and they disagree on two things: whether the element fills the viewport, and whether the consumer's `style` prop is honoured.

**Current behavior:**

In `packages/react/src/index.tsx`, the native `<video>` branch sets:

```js
style={{ position: 'relative', zIndex: 0, ...style }}
```

while the YouTube and Vimeo mounts both set:

```js
style={{ position: 'relative', zIndex: 0, width: '100%', height: '100%' }}
```

Two consequences:

1. **Native video does not fill the viewport.** With no `width`/`height` it falls back to the `<video>` element's intrinsic size, laid out top-left in normal flow. A headless consumer therefore gets a filling player on YouTube/Vimeo and a small top-left video on MP4/HLS — same API, different geometry depending on the source. Visible today in the unthemed `Fixtures/PlayerFixture` stories: a 768x432 viewport with the video in the corner.
2. **The iframe branches silently drop the consumer's** `style`**.** Neither spreads `...style`, so `<Player.Media style={{ ... }} />` is ignored for YouTube and Vimeo.

Point 2 contradicts the rule stated in this same file (the `viewportStyle` comment, ~line 849):

> #89: geometry a primitive sets for itself is a default the consumer's `style` prop overrides, so it is spread *before* `...style` everywhere.

`packages/react/theme.css` masks issue 1 for themed consumers:

```css
:where([data-reely-part='media']) { display: block; width: 100%; height: 100%; }
```

so this only bites headless usage — which is the documented default.

**Desired behavior:**

All three branches follow the same rule: the primitive sets its geometry defaults, spread *before* `...style`, so the consumer can override them.

* Native `<video>` gets `width: '100%'` and `height: '100%'` defaults, plus an `objectFit` default so the frame scales rather than distorting.
* YouTube and Vimeo mounts spread `...style` after their defaults.

**Key interfaces:**

* `Media` in `packages/react/src/index.tsx` — all three return branches.
* `viewportStyle` comment in the same file states the rule to follow.
* `packages/react/theme.css` media block — check the theme still composes correctly and does not double up.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Native `<video>` fills its viewport by default, with an `objectFit` default chosen and justified in a comment.
- [ ] All three branches spread `...style` after their own defaults; a consumer `style` overrides geometry on every provider.
- [ ] Unthemed `Fixtures/PlayerFixture` renders the video filling the viewport.
- [ ] Themed rendering is unchanged.
- [ ] A test covers the native branch's default geometry and consumer override, per provider branch.
- [ ] Changeset added: this changes public default geometry.

**Out of scope:**

* Restyling the control row or the theme (tracked separately).
* Any change to `Viewport`.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-150-medias-provider-branches-disagree-on-geometry-and-on-the`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/120 (squash-merged, required checks green).

All three `Player.Media` branches now share `mediaStyle` (`position: relative; z-index: 0; width: 100%; height: 100%`), spread before `...style` per the #89 rule. The native `<video>` adds `display: block` (it is inline-level, so it otherwise hangs a descender gap below the frame) and `object-fit: contain` — the frame is content, so a mismatched box letterboxes rather than crops; cropping stays available through `style`.

Two things beyond the literal brief, both flagged by `/review`:

- `theme.css` no longer restates the media layer's `display`, `width` and `height`. The inline defaults set identical values and already outranked them, so nothing renders differently — and the file's own header calls restating inline geometry there dead CSS. This is the "does not double up" check the brief asked for, not a restyle; only `background-color` remains.
- The changeset is `minor`, not `patch`: this changes a public default geometry, matching how the #89 change of the same class was bumped.

Tests follow the split `style-precedence.test.tsx` documents — that file pins that a consumer's `style` wins per branch; the `index`/`youtube`/`vimeo` suites pin what the defaults are.

`.changeset/first-prerelease.md` still enumerates six primitives under #89 and was left untouched; `Media`'s own changeset ships in the same release and states its coverage separately.

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** `Overview/Theme` renders the control row with the buttons' default text children inside icon-sized boxes, so the labels wrap and overflow. The theme looks broken exactly where it is meant to look its best.

**Current behavior:**

`packages/react/theme.css` gives every button-shaped control a fixed square box:

```css
inline-size: var(--reely-control-size, 2.75rem);
block-size: var(--reely-control-size, 2.75rem);
flex: 0 0 auto;
```

That is deliberate — the comment explains `flex: 0 0 auto` protects the 44px touch target on narrow players. But the primitives' default children are **text**: `Enter picture-in-picture`, `Enter fullscreen`, `AirPlay`, `Mute`, `Play`. Text in a 44px square wraps to three lines and spills out of the row.

`apps/storybook/stories/reference/reference-player.tsx` passes **icon** children (see `Player.PlayButton`, `Player.PipButton`, `Player.AirPlayButton` around lines 422-446) and looks correct. The Theme story does not.

**Desired behavior:**

The Theme page demonstrates the theme the way it is meant to be consumed — with icon children — so the control row reads as a real player. The dependency is documented: `theme.css` sizes controls for icons, and a consumer passing default text labels needs to size them itself.

**Key interfaces:**

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Themed stories render with icon children; no label wraps or overflows the viewport box at the sizes those stories demonstrate.
- [ ] `Theme.mdx` states that controls are sized for icons and what a consumer must do otherwise.
- [ ] Existing computed-style assertions in `theme.stories.tsx` still pass.
- [ ] No change to `theme.css` token values or to the 44px minimum.

**Out of scope:**

* Shipping icons from `@reely/react` — the library stays headless.
* Changing `--reely-control-size` or the touch-target rule.

Three things worth recording:

## SIDEPRO-152 — Add a theme toggle to the Storybook toolbar

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-152/add-a-theme-toggle-to-the-storybook-toolbar

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** Every per-part story renders unstyled, because `theme.css` is deliberately not mounted globally. A toolbar toggle would let any story be viewed themed on demand, without changing the default.

**Current behavior:**

The primitives are headless and import no CSS, so `Player/*` stories render raw unstyled DOM. That is honest — it is what the library ships — but it makes the workbench hard to read, and it hides the optional stylesheet everywhere except `Overview/Theme`.

`apps/storybook/stories/theme.stories.tsx` already solves the mounting problem per story:

```js
import themeCss from '../../../packages/react/theme.css?inline';
const withTheme = (Story) => (<><style>{themeCss}</style><Story /></>);
```

with a comment explaining why a global `import '@reely/react/theme.css'` is wrong: every story renders in the same document and other stories assert unthemed computed styles.

**Desired behavior:**

A `globalTypes` toolbar toggle (Headless / Themed), default **Headless**, with a decorator in `.storybook/preview.tsx` that mounts the stylesheet per story when the toggle is on and tears it down with the story. Flipping it restyles the whole workbench; leaving it alone changes nothing.

This also demonstrates the library's central claim from `Theme.mdx` — "Add one import. That is the whole difference." — interactively.

**Key interfaces:**

* `apps/storybook/.storybook/preview.tsx` — `globalTypes` and the decorator.
* `apps/storybook/stories/theme.stories.tsx` — reuse its `?inline` import approach and its reasoning; do not duplicate the mechanism if it can be shared.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Toolbar toggle switches every story between headless and themed.
- [ ] Default is headless; the Vitest run pins the toggle off so existing assertions are unaffected.
- [ ] The two files that assert computed styles still pass — `captions.stories.tsx` and `theme.stories.tsx` are the only ones using `getComputedStyle`.
- [ ] No story leaks theme CSS into another; verify by toggling on, navigating away, and asserting an unthemed computed style.

**Out of scope:**

* Changing `theme.css` itself.
* Making themed the default.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-28*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-152-add-a-theme-toggle-to-the-storybook-toolbar`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/122 (squash-merged by auto-merge, required checks green — `e2e (webkit)` took 42m but passed).

The Theme toggle (Headless / Themed) lives in `.storybook/preview.tsx`'s `globalTypes`; the mounting itself is `.storybook/theme.tsx`, the single place `theme.css` is read. It renders as a `<style>` inside the story tree, so it tears down with the story instead of leaking into the shared preview document. `theme.stories.tsx` dropped its own decorator and pins itself with meta-level `globals: { theme: 'themed' }` — one mechanism, and that file is it turned on.

Notes worth keeping:

- **The toggle is pinned off for Vitest by `initialGlobals`, not by luck.** `globalTypes.theme` has no `defaultValue` (the deprecated SB≤7 spelling), and `@storybook/addon-vitest` composes `initialGlobals` into the portable-story path, so it is the only source of `headless` in the test run.
- **`stories/theme.contract.test.ts` covers both decorator branches** in the fast node suite, mirroring `mock-player.contract.test.ts` and for the same stated reason — a typo in the `'themed'` string would otherwise slip past everything and silently leave every story headless. Proven non-vacuous.
- **`TearsDownWithTheStory` is order-dependent by construction** and its comment now says so: it passes vacuously under a `-t` filter or if the exports are reordered, and on the autodocs page every story co-mounts so it renders themed there. It stays because it is the only test that exercises real DOM teardown; the contract test is the unconditional cover.
- `vitest.config.ts` now processes `theme.css` — Vitest replaces CSS imports with empty strings by default, so the "the `<style>` carries the stylesheet" assertion would have asserted against nothing.
- **`Reference.mdx` was corrected by this change**: it said the reference example could not be themed because the only per-story mount reads `theme.css` by path out of `packages/`, which its import rule forbids. The import now lives in `.storybook/`, outside that rule's scope, so those stories are themable like any other. The import rule itself is untouched.

One pre-existing problem found but deliberately not touched: `ConsumerCssWins`'s comment claims its consumer style is "declared BEFORE the theme in document order — so if this wins it is the cascade layer doing the work, not source order." That was false before this change and still is — Storybook renders the project decorator's `<style>` first either way, and `.consumer-tint` (0-1-0) outranks `:where(…)` (0-0-0) on specificity regardless, so the story would pass with `@layer` deleted. Fixing it means mounting the consumer style ahead of the theme's, which needs a mechanism outside this issue. Flagging for triage rather than filing it myself.

**Current behavior:**

`Overview/Reference example` is described in `Introduction.mdx` as "one composed player built only from public exports — the runnable proof that the primitives are sufficient". It is the page that best answers "what is this library". It is not where the sidebar opens.

The practical effect: the workbench reads as a pile of broken buttons on first contact, because the per-part stories are unstyled by design and inert by design (mock provider, no media).

**Desired behavior:**

Storybook opens on the Reference example, and the sidebar order puts the Overview pages before `Player/*`.

**Key interfaces:**

* `apps/storybook/.storybook/main.ts` and/or `preview.tsx` — sidebar sort order / default story.
* `apps/storybook/stories/reference/` — the target page.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Opening Storybook with no URL parameters lands on the Reference example.
- [ ] Overview pages sort above `Player/*` in the sidebar.
- [ ] No story ids change — the e2e suite drives stories by id and must keep passing.

**Out of scope:**

* Restyling any story.
* Changing what the Reference example renders.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-153-open-storybook-on-the-reference-example-instead-of-a-bare`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/123 (squash-merged, required checks green).

`parameters.options.storySort` in `.storybook/preview.tsx` sets the order: `Overview` (Reference example first, then Introduction, Contract, Captions, Capabilities matrix, Theme), then `Player`, `Reference`, `Theme`, and last `Real playback` and `Fixtures` — both hit the network or exist to be driven by e2e, so they sit below what a visitor reads.

- **No ids changed.** Sorting only; ids come from each story's `title` and none was touched. Proven by building `storybook-static/index.json` before and after: identical set of 136 entry ids, same sha256 over the sorted list.
- **The landing was previously `Overview/Capabilities matrix`** — a table. Worth recording, since the brief said "whichever story sorts first" without naming it.
- **`storySort` is applied at index time, not just in the manager.** Storybook statically parses `preview.tsx` via `getStorySortParameter` and sorts `index.json` server-side, so the manager never sees a wrong first leaf even transiently.
- **Unlisted entries sort last at every depth** (no `"*"` in the order array), so a new `Overview/*` page or even a whole new top-level section added later cannot displace the landing. Only editing the order array can.

`e2e/workbench.spec.ts` is new and deliberately unlike its neighbours: every other spec drives `/iframe.html?id=…`, but this asserts what a visitor with no URL sees, which only exists in the manager. Nothing else would catch the regression, and it is a silent one. Proven to fail for the right reason — with `storySort` removed it reports `Received: "/docs/overview-capabilities-matrix--docs"`. Chromium-only via `test.skip`, the mechanism `live.spec.ts` / `hls.spec.ts` / `poster.spec.ts` already use; the e2e matrix's chromium leg runs it.

One thing for future reference: local `pnpm test:e2e` shows ~20 `[webkit]` failures where media never reaches `currentTime > 0`. They reproduce on a clean tree and are Playwright's Linux WebKit lacking H.264 on this machine — CI's webkit leg passes in ~2m. Not a repo problem, but it will mislead anyone running the full e2e suite locally.

## SIDEPRO-154 — Show ActivationButton as a poster overlay with custom children

- **State:** Done

**Description**

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** `Player/ActivationButton` stories render the button alone, so it reads as a stray text button. It is actually a full-bleed overlay meant to sit on top of a poster, and it accepts arbitrary children — neither is discoverable from the stories.

**Current behavior:**

`ActivationButton` renders with `position: absolute; inset: 0; z-index: 30` — a full-bleed click target over the viewport — and falls back to a text child only when none is given:

```jsx
{children ?? (isError ? 'Retry' : 'Play')}
```

Its accessible name comes from `aria-label` (default `Play video` / `Retry loading video`), so a decorative child image is safe.

With nothing underneath it in the stories, it looks like a small stray button, and a reader cannot tell that an image or icon can go inside, or that a poster belongs beneath it. This came up directly from a maintainer reading the workbench: *"Why do we have a button as an activation button, what happens if I want to have an image in there?"*

**Desired behavior:**

At least one story composes the realistic shape — `Player.PosterImage` (or `Player.Poster`) beneath, `ActivationButton` overlaying it with a custom icon child — so the overlay nature and the children slot are both obvious. The docs text says what the default child is and where the accessible name comes from.

**Key interfaces:**

* `apps/storybook/stories/activation.stories.tsx`
* `ActivationButton`, `Poster`, `PosterImage` in `packages/react/src/index.tsx`
* Stories must issue zero external requests — use a local/data-URI poster, as other stories do.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A story shows a poster with the activation overlay on top and a custom (non-text) child.
- [ ] Story docs state that `children` overrides the default text and that the accessible name comes from `aria-label`.
- [ ] Zero external requests; the axe check passes.
- [ ] Existing activation stories and the `ActivatesOnClick` play function still pass.

**Out of scope:**

* Any change to `ActivationButton`'s implementation or API — `asChild` is tracked separately.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-154-show-activationbutton-as-a-poster-overlay-with-custom`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/124 (squash-merged, required checks green).

`OverlayOnPoster` composes the realistic shape — `Player.Poster` + `Player.PosterImage` beneath, `ActivationButton` full-bleed on top with a `Player.PlayIcon` child. The meta docs gained a `**Layout**` bullet and a `**Children**` bullet, and `**Accessibility**` now says the name comes from `aria-label`, never from `children`.

**The brief's geometry premise was backwards, and the measurements are worth keeping.** Rendered in a 480×270 viewport:

| | button rect | icon rect |
| --- | --- | --- |
| headless | 480×270 at (0, 0) | 48×48 at (216, 109.5) — centred |
| themed | 64×64 at (0, 0) | 20×20 at (22, 22) |

- Headless already centres the icon: a `<button>` centres its own content, so the full-bleed overlay does it unaided. No consumer styling needed — which is why the reference example gets away with a bare `<Player.PlayIcon>`. The ~1.5px vertical offset is the UA button stylesheet's asymmetric padding.
- There is no inline-vs-theme conflict: `activationOverlayStyle` sets only `position`/`inset`/`zIndex`, never a size.

So the story is headless like its four siblings; the only consumer styling is `color: #fff` against a dark poster. Pinning it themed would have shipped a story whose headline image is a 64px circle in the corner.

---

**Found and deliberately not fixed — worth a separate issue.** `theme.css`'s `[data-reely-part='activation']` sets `inline-size/block-size: 4rem` while the primitive pins `inset: 0`. The box is over-constrained, so CSS drops `right`/`bottom` and **the themed activation button renders flush in the viewport's top-left corner instead of centred** — `place-items: center` only centres the icon inside the circle. `margin: auto` on that rule is the standard fix. The theme has never been exercised against `ActivationButton`: `theme.stories.tsx` only composes `Controls`, and the reference example is headless with its own CSS. Changing the theme was out of scope here.

The play function asserts what the story teaches, including that the icon is centred within 3px — proven non-vacuous by nudging it 40px. Axe is proven live too: injecting a bare `<img>` fails the run on `image-alt`.

<a id="sidepro-155"></a>

## SIDEPRO-155 — Document that primitives render fixed elements, with children as the extension point

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-28
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-155/document-that-primitives-render-fixed-elements-with-children-as-the

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166) — Describe parts as parts in Contract.mdx, not as primitive names

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Needs a maintainer decision before any implementation.** `wontfix` **is a legitimate outcome.**

## The question

Reely's primitives render fixed elements. `ActivationButton` is typed `ComponentPropsWithRef<'button'>` and always renders a `<button>`; the other controls follow the same shape. `grep -rn "asChild" packages/react/src` returns nothing.

So a consumer can pass **children** into a control, but cannot substitute the **element**. There is no way to render the activation affordance as a different component, or to merge the primitive's behaviour and attributes onto a consumer's own element — the pattern Radix popularised as `asChild`, and the one most headless React libraries now expose.

This surfaced from a maintainer reading the workbench and asking why the activation affordance is a `<button>` and what happens when they want something else in there. The children slot covers the common case (an icon or image inside the button); it does not cover wanting a different element or an existing design-system component to *be* the control.

## Why it is a real question for this library

The README positions Reely as headless — "nothing is rendered you did not compose". A fixed element type is a limit on exactly that claim. Against that:

* `asChild` costs a real amount of complexity — ref merging, prop merging, and a documented rule for what wins.
* It interacts with the accessibility guarantees. `ActivationButton` sets `aria-label`, `aria-disabled`, `type="button"` and `data-state`; if a consumer supplies the element, those have to be merged onto something that may not be a button at all, and the a11y story gets harder to hold.
* The capability-gating model means controls sometimes render `null`. That has to compose sanely with a consumer-supplied element.

## What a decision looks like

* **Yes, broadly** — `asChild` across the control primitives, with a merging contract and a11y rules written down.
* **Yes, narrowly** — only where there is a demonstrated need, e.g. `ActivationButton`.
* **No** — document that children are the extension point and the element type is fixed on purpose, and say why in `Contract.mdx`.

## If the answer is yes

Scope it into its own issue with the merging contract specified. Do not fold it into story or theme work.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Decision: no `asChild`, 2026-07-29 — and what remains

The maintainer's answer is the third option in the body: **no**. Primitives keep fixed element types; children stay the extension point. The reasoning is recorded in **`.out-of-scope/as-child.md`** (PR #127) — the accessibility guarantees are merged onto elements the primitives know are buttons, the merging contract would be permanent public surface, and capability-gated `null` renders complicate the pattern. It is explicitly reversible: `asChild` is an additive prop, so a concrete composition that children genuinely cannot express would reopen it.

That leaves the half of the decision that *is* work — the body's own "No" branch says to document it. Retitled and re-scoped to that, and moved to `ready-for-agent`. Category changed `Feature` → `Improvement`: what ships is documentation of existing behaviour, not a new capability.

---

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** State in `Contract.mdx` that primitives render fixed element types on purpose, that children and the part attributes are the extension points, and why — so a consumer learns the boundary by reading rather than by trying.

**Current behavior:**

Nothing says it. The README positions the library as headless — "nothing is rendered you did not compose" — which a reader can reasonably take to include choosing the element. `ActivationButton` is typed `ComponentPropsWithRef<'button'>` and always renders a `<button>`; the other controls follow the same shape; searching the source for `asChild` returns nothing. So a consumer discovers the limit by writing code that does not work, and has no way to tell whether it is an omission or a decision.

**Desired behavior:**

`Contract.mdx` covers the element-type boundary alongside the data-attribute contract it already documents. A reader should come away knowing three things:

1. Each primitive renders a fixed element type, and this is deliberate.
2. The extension points are children (put what you like *inside* a control), the `style` prop, and the `data-reely-part` / `data-state` attributes for CSS — which together cover styling and content, just not substitution.
3. Why: the primitives set `aria-*`, `type` and `data-state` on elements they know the shape of, and that guarantee cannot survive an arbitrary substituted element. WCAG 2.2 AA is a release gate here, not an aspiration.

Keep it proportionate — a short section, in the page's own register. It should not read as an apology or as a roadmap promise, and it must not imply `asChild` is coming.

**Key interfaces:**

- `apps/storybook/stories/Contract.mdx` — where this belongs, near the existing attribute contract.
- `CONTEXT.md`'s `Primitive` and `Part` entries — the vocabulary to use. Note [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166) is separately correcting this same page's part-vs-primitive language; if that has landed first, match what it settled rather than reintroducing the older phrasing.
- `.out-of-scope/as-child.md` — the full reasoning, for the writer's context. Do not link to it from the page; it is an internal triage record, not consumer documentation.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `Contract.mdx` states that primitive element types are fixed and deliberate.
- [ ] It names children, `style`, and the part attributes as the extension points, with at least one concrete example.
- [ ] It gives the accessibility reason, briefly.
- [ ] It does not promise `asChild`, present or future.
- [ ] No source change to any primitive, and no change to the data-attribute contract.
- [ ] No changeset — documentation only, no package behaviour changes.

**Out of scope:**

- Implementing `asChild` in any form, narrow or broad.
- Changing any primitive's props, types or rendered element.
- Restructuring `Contract.mdx` beyond adding this section.
- The part-vs-primitive wording fix, which is [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166)'s job.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-155-document-that-primitives-render-fixed-elements-with-children`, cut from `main` at `7231e84`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/128 (squash-merged to `main`).

`Contract.mdx` gains an `Element types are fixed` section. Documentation only — no primitive source changes, no data-attribute contract change, no changeset.

Three claims the brief's phrasing would have made turned out to be false against the source, so the section is scoped more narrowly than the brief assumed:

- `VolumeSlider` renders a bare `<input type="range">` and takes no children, so "every control accepts children" does not hold.
- `ErrorDisplay` takes render-prop children called with `{ error, retry }`, and `Captions` takes `renderCue`. The library *does* hand over inner rendering — through explicit typed props rather than a general substitution mechanism. The section says so rather than claiming nothing merges primitive behaviour into consumer markup.
- `MenuItem` and `MenuRadioItem` set no `aria-label` (their accessible name comes from children), so the accessibility claim is scoped to the button primitives this page already documents.

Also recategorised `data-reely-part` / `data-state` as the primitive's output rather than an extension point, since both are spread after the consumer's props and cannot be overridden.

Landing gate: typecheck green, 826 tests green, format/lint/`docs:check` green, `/review` (Standards + Spec) findings all resolved.

<a id="sidepro-159"></a>

## SIDEPRO-159 — Show per-part CSS examples in the workbench, gated against drift

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-159/show-per-part-css-examples-in-the-workbench-gated-against-drift

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-161](#sidepro-161) — ConsumerCssWins does not prove the cascade layer does anything

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** `Contract.mdx` documents every `data-reely-part` and `data-state`, but the workbench contains no CSS that uses them. A reader gets raw unstyled DOM or the whole of `theme.css` — nothing in between. Radix's primitives docs show a small stylesheet per primitive, written against the same data attributes they tell you to target, so the demo doubles as proof the contract is sufficient. That middle rung is what is missing here.

**Current behavior:**

Three things exist and do not meet:

* `apps/storybook/stories/Contract.mdx` lists the full data-attribute contract in prose. No CSS.
* `packages/react/theme.css` is an all-or-nothing stylesheet, toggled globally by the Headless/Themed toolbar control (#152).
* `scripts/docs-examples.mjs` gates `ts`/`tsx` fences in the READMEs and two MDX pages against real files in `examples/`, so a shown example cannot drift from a compiling one. It does not handle `css`.

The nearest thing to a per-part CSS example is `ConsumerCssWins` in `theme.stories.tsx`, which mounts a one-rule `<style>` — a working instance of the mechanism, used for a different purpose (and see #161 for what it actually proves).

**Desired behavior:**

A reader looking at `Player/PlayButton` can see the CSS that styles it, written against `[data-reely-part='play-button']` and its `data-state`, and see the story rendered with exactly that CSS applied. The snippet is generated from a real file, so it cannot drift from what ran.

**Plain CSS. Not Tailwind, not anything else.**

The examples are vanilla CSS, full stop. The data attributes *are* CSS selectors, so CSS is the one form every consumer can translate from — a Tailwind user reading `[data-state='playing'] { … }` knows to write `data-[state=playing]:…`, while a plain-CSS, CSS-modules or vanilla-extract user reading utility classes learns nothing about the contract. Tailwind is also not a dependency of this repo, so its classes would render inert in a story while appearing to do the work — the opposite of what gating is for.

**Key interfaces:**

* `examples/css/<part>.css` — the new fixtures. `examples/` is already the fixture root for `docs-examples.mjs`.
* `scripts/docs-examples.mjs` — extend the fence languages it gates to include `css`. Its pure functions are covered by `scripts/docs-examples.test.mjs`; extend those too.
* `apps/storybook/.storybook/theme.tsx` — the existing decorator already mounts a stylesheet inside the story tree and tears it down with the story. Reuse that mechanism rather than inventing a second one.
* The per-story `docs.description.component` blocks, or `parameters.docs.source`, for surfacing the CSS beside the story. **Open question for the maintainer:** which of the two, or something else. Storybook has no native "CSS file tab" beside a story, so whatever is chosen is an approximation — decide it before marking this ready.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `docs-examples.mjs` gates `css` fences the same way it gates `ts`/`tsx`, with `pnpm docs:check` failing when a shown snippet diverges from its fixture.
- [ ] Three or four exemplary parts carry a CSS example — pick a button, a slider, an overlay, and one whose `data-state` is worth styling. Not all twenty.
- [ ] Each such story renders with its own CSS applied, mounted and torn down per story.
- [ ] The CSS is visible to a reader of that story's docs page, not only in the repo.
- [ ] `theme.css` is unchanged, and the Headless/Themed toggle still behaves as it does today.

**Out of scope:**

* Covering every part. Three or four carry the lesson; twenty is maintenance surface with no extra teaching.
* Tailwind, in any form, including adding it as a dependency of `apps/storybook`.
* Building a separate documentation site. Deliberately deferred — these fixtures are the content layer either path needs, so this work is not wasted if a site is built later.
* Changing `theme.css` or the data-attribute contract.

**Notes:**

The gating is the point, not the snippets. A headless library's docs are only credible if the CSS in them demonstrably works, and this repo already has the machinery to guarantee that.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Open question resolved, 2026-07-29

The brief's one blocker — which surface shows the CSS beside the story — is decided by the maintainer:

**`docs.description.component`.** The CSS goes in a fenced `css` block in each story's docs description.

Two reasons it wins over `parameters.docs.source`:

- **Additive.** The existing "Show code" panel keeps showing the JSX composition, which is the other half of what a reader needs. `docs.source` would displace it.
- **No new machinery.** A description block is plain markdown, so `docs-examples.mjs`'s fence gating applies to it directly — which is the point of the issue, since the gating is what makes the shown CSS provably real.

Everything else in the brief stands unchanged. This issue is now `ready-for-agent`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-159-show-per-part-css-examples-in-the-workbench-gated-against`, cut from `main` at `290c8ee`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/129 (squash-merged to `main`).

Four vanilla-CSS fixtures under `examples/`, `css` gated as a peer of `ts`/`tsx` in `docs-examples.mjs`, each stylesheet shown both on its story's docs page and in a new `Styling a part` section of `Contract.mdx`. `theme.css` untouched; the Headless/Themed toggle behaves as before.

Three things worth recording, because they were not visible from the brief:

**The fixtures could not live at `examples/css/<part>.css`.** Marker names are `[a-z0-9-]+`, so they cannot hold a path separator, and `readFixtures` was a flat non-recursive read keyed on basename. They are `examples/css-<part>.css` instead, and `indexFixtures` now throws when two files would answer to one marker name rather than silently letting the last one win.

**A production build would have made the examples unreadable.** Vite's `?inline` runs CSS through `cssMinify`, so every printed snippet on the built docs would have been one line with all teaching comments stripped — while `Contract.mdx` kept its formatting, because MDX does not go through `?inline`. No test in the repo would have caught it: they run against dev, and CI only checks the Storybook build *succeeds*. Stories now import `?raw`, and the built bundles were checked byte-for-byte against the fixtures on both surfaces.

**Three stylesheets asserted things the library does not do.** `css-activation.css` had `dormant` and `eligible` inverted (`use-activation.ts:295` — `dormant` is the clickable resting state) and withheld the hover affordance from `error`, which is also pressable (`:275-294`, bailing only on a configuration error). `css-poster.css` labelled `[data-state='error']` as "no poster configured" (that is `idle`) and carried a `display: none` rule that could never fire against the inline `display: block`. `css-seek-slider.css` reached for `input[type='range']` where the part name `seek-slider-input` exists — breaking the rule stated on the same page. Also swept out four declarations that restated inline values, per ADR-0001.

The story descriptions' cascade claim was corrected too: the examples are unlayered, so they *beat* `@layer reely` — the theme goes underneath, which is the guarantee `theme.css` advertises. Related to [SIDEPRO-161](#sidepro-161).

`Contract.mdx`'s part list gained `seek-slider-input` and `seek-buffered-range`, which already existed in source but were undocumented. The contract itself is unchanged.

Landing gate: 23 docs tests, `docs:check`, typecheck, 826 unit tests, 86 Storybook tests, format, lint, and the Storybook build all green. `/review` (Standards + Spec) findings cleared over three rounds.

<a id="sidepro-160"></a>

## SIDEPRO-160 — The themed ActivationButton renders in the corner instead of centred

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-160/the-themed-activationbutton-renders-in-the-corner-instead-of-centred

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-138](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/178) — WCAG 2.2 AA verification pass — owner visual review

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug

**Summary:** `theme.css` gives `[data-reely-part='activation']` a fixed 4rem square while the primitive pins `inset: 0` inline. The box is over-constrained, so CSS drops the `right`/`bottom` offsets and the themed activation button renders flush in the viewport's **top-left corner** rather than centred.

**Current behavior:**

`ActivationButton` applies `activationOverlayStyle` inline (`packages/react/src/index.tsx`, ~line 1075):

```js
{ position: 'absolute', inset: 0, zIndex: 30 }
```

`packages/react/theme.css` (~lines 82-94) then sets:

```css
:where([data-reely-part='activation']) {
  display: grid;
  place-items: center;
  inline-size: 4rem;
  block-size: 4rem;
  border-radius: 50%;
  /* … */
}
```

`top`, `bottom` and `height` are then all non-`auto` with no `auto` margin, which over-constrains the box. Per CSS 2.1 §10.3.7 the `right` and `bottom` offsets are ignored for LTR, so the 64px circle lands at (0, 0). `place-items: center` only centres the icon *within* the circle — it does nothing for the circle itself.

Measured in a 480×270 viewport while working #154:

|  | button rect | icon rect |
| -- | -- | -- |
| headless | 480×270 at (0, 0) | 48×48 at (216, 109.5) — centred |
| themed | 64×64 at (0, 0) | 20×20 at (22, 22) |

Headless is correct: a `<button>` centres its own content, so the full-bleed overlay puts the icon dead centre unaided. It is only the themed path that misplaces it.

**Why it was never noticed:** the theme has never been exercised against this part. `theme.stories.tsx` composes only `Controls`, and the reference example is headless with its own CSS.

**Desired behavior:**

The themed activation button is centred in the viewport, at the same 4rem size and with the same appearance it has today. Headless rendering is unchanged.

`margin: auto` on that rule is the standard idiom — `inset: 0` plus `auto` margins is how a fixed-size absolutely-positioned box centres — but confirm against the actual rendering rather than assuming, and consider whether `place-self: center` reads better alongside the existing `place-items: center`.

**Key interfaces:**

* `packages/react/theme.css` — the `[data-reely-part='activation']` block.
* `activationOverlayStyle` in `packages/react/src/index.tsx` — for reference only; the inline geometry is correct and must not change (the primitive is a full-bleed click target when unstyled).
* `apps/storybook/stories/activation.stories.tsx` — `OverlayOnPoster` composes poster + overlay and already asserts centring within 3px in the headless case.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] The themed activation button is centred in its viewport, at unchanged size and appearance.
- [ ] Headless rendering is unchanged — `OverlayOnPoster`'s existing centring assertion still passes.
- [ ] A story or test covers the themed case, since nothing does today; it must fail without the fix.
- [ ] Changeset added if the fix changes `theme.css`, which ships in the package.

**Out of scope:**

* Changing `ActivationButton`'s inline geometry or its API.
* Restyling the activation button's appearance — size, colour and radius stay as they are.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-160-the-themed-activationbutton-renders-in-the-corner-instead-of`, cut from `main` at `8c798da`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/130 (squash-merged to `main`).

**The fix went on the primitive, not in `theme.css` — a deliberate override of this brief's out-of-scope line, decided by the maintainer mid-issue.**

The brief scoped the fix to `theme.css` and put `ActivationButton`'s inline geometry out of bounds. Review surfaced that ADR-0001 named this bug as proof of its own rule, which raised the question of where the fix belonged. The maintainer's call: state `margin: auto` on the primitive, because the bundled theme is not the only stylesheet that will ever size that part — for a headless library, a consumer stylesheet doing it is the *primary* case, and a theme-only fix leaves all of them broken.

`packages/react/theme.css` ends up byte-identical to `main`. Nothing in it was the defect.

`margin: auto` is inert on the default path: with `inset: 0` and auto sizing, CSS 2.1 §10.3.7 (inline axis) and §10.6.4 (block axis) both resolve auto margins to zero, so the unstyled full-bleed click target is unchanged. `OverlayOnPoster` asserts the overlay's rect *equals* the poster's exactly, and passes with the fix reverted — direct evidence rather than an argument from spec.

Two stories, both verified failing without the fix:

- `SizedByConsumerCss` (`activation.stories.tsx`) — an unlayered consumer stylesheet sizing the overlay to 96px. Fails at `expected 192 to be less than or equal to 1`. This is the case the redirect was made for; nothing covered it before.
- `ActivationIsCentred` (`theme.stories.tsx`) — the bundled theme's 4rem circle. Fails at `208`.

**ADR-0001 was corrected.** It cited this bug as proof that `theme.css` must not set a structural property the primitive already sets. That was wrong twice: the two sets never overlapped (`inline-size`/`block-size` versus `position`/`inset`/`z-index`), and duplication cannot produce a fight anyway, since an inline value simply wins — which the following bullet already said. What collides is *interaction*, not duplication: two individually legitimate declarations, a fixed size against four zero offsets. The bullet now records the old reading as mistaken so it is not re-derived, and generalises the lesson: an inline structural value must degrade gracefully when a stylesheet sets something that interacts with it.

One degenerate behaviour change is documented in the changeset: a box a stylesheet makes *larger* than the viewport now overflows equally above and below rather than only below, because §10.6.4 has no negative-margin clamp where §10.3.7 does.

Landing gate: 826 unit tests, 88 Storybook tests, typecheck, `docs:check`, format, lint — all green.

<a id="sidepro-161"></a>

## SIDEPRO-161 — ConsumerCssWins does not prove the cascade layer does anything

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-161/consumercsswins-does-not-prove-the-cascade-layer-does-anything

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-159](#sidepro-159) — Show per-part CSS examples in the workbench, gated against drift

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug

**Summary:** The `ConsumerCssWins` story in `apps/storybook/stories/theme.stories.tsx` claims to isolate `@layer reely` as the reason a consumer's rule beats the theme. Both halves of that claim are false, and the story would pass with `@layer` deleted from `theme.css` — so the guarantee it exists to protect is unguarded.

**Current behavior:**

The story carries this comment:

```jsx
{/* Unlayered, single class, declared BEFORE the theme in document
    order -- so if this wins it is the cascade layer doing the work,
    not source order. */}
```

Two problems, both pre-existing and both verified:

1. **It is not declared before the theme.** Storybook's `defaultDecorateStory` reduces the decorator array so that later entries render *outermost*, and project decorators are outermost of all. The theme's `<style>` therefore lands in the document ahead of the consumer's, both before and after the toggle work in #152. Measured: the theme style sits at document index 2, the consumer's at 4.
2. **Source order is not what is being tested anyway.** `.consumer-tint` has specificity 0-1-0; the theme's rules are all `:where(…)` at 0-0-0. The consumer rule wins on specificity alone, regardless of layers or order.

So the story is green, and would stay green if the `@layer reely` wrapper were removed from `theme.css` entirely. `theme.css`'s own header promises "Unlayered consumer CSS beats a cascade layer… your own rules win by default" — that promise currently has no test.

**Desired behavior:**

Either the story genuinely isolates the layer, or it stops claiming to and something else covers the promise.

To isolate the layer, the consumer's rule has to lose on every axis *except* the layer — i.e. equal-or-lower specificity than the theme's `:where(…)` (so, an unlayered `:where()` rule of its own), and declared *after* the theme in document order, which needs the consumer style mounted outside the story tree or after the theme's. Whoever picks this up should confirm the mechanism empirically before settling on it; the decorator ordering above is the trap.

**Key interfaces:**

* `apps/storybook/stories/theme.stories.tsx` — the `ConsumerCssWins` story and its decorator.
* `apps/storybook/.storybook/theme.tsx` — the project decorator that mounts the theme, and the reason the ordering is what it is.
* `packages/react/theme.css` — the `@layer reely` wrapper and the header comment stating the guarantee.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] The story's comment describes what is actually true of the DOM it produces.
- [ ] A test fails when `@layer reely` is removed from `theme.css` — demonstrate the red, do not assume it.
- [ ] The existing `ConsumerCssWins` assertions still pass, or are replaced by stronger ones.
- [ ] No change to `theme.css`'s rules or to the layer name.

**Out of scope:**

* Restyling the theme.
* Changing how the theme is mounted in Storybook (#152's decorator stays as it is) unless isolating the layer genuinely requires it — in which case say so explicitly rather than changing it quietly.

**Notes:**

Found while working #152. Left alone there because fixing it needs a mounting mechanism outside that issue's scope.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-161-consumercsswins-does-not-prove-the-cascade-layer-does`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/131 (squash-merged, all nine required checks green).

**What shipped.** The consumer rule is now rigged to lose on every cascade axis except the layer: dropped to `:where(.consumer-tint)` so it ties `:where([data-reely-part='controls'])` at 0-0-0, and portalled into `document.head` so it is the *earlier* declaration and would lose the tie on source order. What remains is the cascade layer, which is resolved before specificity is ever consulted.

The red was demonstrated, not assumed — with `@layer reely` removed from `theme.css`, the story fails with `expected 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)' to be 'rgb(1, 2, 3)'`. (The received transparent value is itself confirmation the theme won the tie: its `controls` rule uses the `background` shorthand, which resets `background-color`.)

**The brief's mechanism was wrong.** It asks for the consumer rule "declared *after* the theme in document order". A later declaration *wins* on source order, so that configuration isolates nothing — it would stay green with the layer deleted. It is implemented as *before*, deliberately. The brief also contradicts itself here: "Current behavior" #1 measures the consumer style as already being after the theme (index 2 vs 4) and correctly calls that the bug, then prescribes it as the fix.

**One thing this issue assumed that is not true.** The old story was described as proving the consumer's single class outranks the theme. It never did. While `@layer reely` exists, an unlayered consumer rule beats a layered theme rule at *any* specificity, so the layer settles the contest before specificity is consulted — measured across all four combinations of layer present/absent and `:where()` present/stripped, the old rule won every time, for a different reason each time. No cascade-outcome test can isolate `:where()` while the consumer rule is unlayered. That promise is covered structurally instead, over the whole stylesheet text, in `packages/react/test/theme.test.ts` ("every selector is specificity-zero via `:where()`"), which names the offending selector. So nothing was lost by lowering the story's selector to 0-0-0.

**Also fixed, found by review.** The probe that locates the theme's `<style>` originally matched the substring `@layer reely`, which appears in `theme.css`'s *header comment* as well as the at-rule — so with the real wrapper deleted the probe still resolved the stylesheet via the comment, and the intended failure survived by luck. It now matches the at-rule with its brace, spaced optionally, since the production Storybook build minifies `?inline` CSS to `@layer reely{`.

`theme.css` is unchanged: no rule edits, no layer rename. The theme's mounting (`withTheme`, `withCss`, `preview.tsx`) is untouched.
