<a id="sidepro-166"></a>

## SIDEPRO-166 — Describe parts as parts in Contract.mdx, not as primitive names

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-166/describe-parts-as-parts-in-contractmdx-not-as-primitive-names

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-178](#sidepro-178) — Complete the data-reely-part list in Contract.mdx and gate it against drift
  - [SIDEPRO-155](#sidepro-155) — Document that primitives render fixed elements, with children as the extension point

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** `Contract.mdx` introduces `data-reely-part` as "the primitive's stable name". That is wrong for most of the values it then lists — a primitive can render several parts, so a part name is not a primitive's name. `CONTEXT.md` now defines both terms, and this page contradicts it.

**Current behavior:**

`apps/storybook/stories/Contract.mdx` opens its list with:

> `data-reely-part` — the primitive's stable name (`play-button`, `mute-button`, `volume-slider`, `seek-slider` / `seek-buffered`, …)

Several of the values in that same list are not primitives and never were:

* `ErrorDisplay` renders `error`, `error-message` and `error-retry`.
* `Captions` renders `captions`, `captions-button` and `captions-announcer`.
* `SeekSlider` renders `seek-slider` and `seek-buffered`.
* `Poster` and `PosterImage` are two primitives, but `poster` / `poster-image` read as one primitive's two parts.

So the relationship is one primitive to many parts, and the sentence asserts one to one.

`CONTEXT.md` (added after this page was written) settles the vocabulary:

> **Primitive**: An exported React component a consumer composes into a player.
>
> **Part**: A named element in the rendered DOM, identified by `data-reely-part`. One primitive may render several — `ErrorDisplay` renders `error`, `error-message` and `error-retry` — so a part name is not a primitive's name.

`docs/agents/domain.md` requires output to use the glossary's vocabulary, which makes this a live contradiction rather than a stylistic quibble.

**Desired behavior:**

The page describes what `data-reely-part` actually names, in the glossary's terms, and a reader can tell that one primitive may expose several parts — which matters, because it is exactly the case where they cannot reach the element with a `style` prop and have to use the attribute.

**Key interfaces:**

* `apps/storybook/stories/Contract.mdx` — the `data-reely-part` bullet, and the surrounding prose if it repeats the assumption.
* `CONTEXT.md` — the definitions to match. Do not restate them at length; the page should read naturally, not like a glossary quotation.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `Contract.mdx` no longer describes a part as a primitive's name.
- [ ] A reader can tell that one primitive may render several parts, with at least one concrete example.
- [ ] Terminology matches `CONTEXT.md`'s `Primitive` and `Part` entries.
- [ ] No change to any `data-reely-part` value, and no change to the list of them.

**Out of scope:**

* Renaming any part.
* Restructuring the page or its other bullets (`data-state`, `data-provider`, `data-buffering`).
* Changing `CONTEXT.md`.

**Notes:**

Surfaced while writing `CONTEXT.md`. Prose-only; the attribute values themselves are correct and stable.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-166-describe-parts-as-parts-in-contractmdx-not-as-primitive`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/132 (squash-merged, all required checks green).

**What shipped.** The `data-reely-part` bullet now says what the attribute names — a part, one named element in the rendered DOM — and that several parts can come from one primitive, with `ErrorDisplay` as the worked example. Prose only; the same 24 names in the same order, reflowed but not edited.

The captions sentence is load-bearing rather than decorative: the list joins `captions` / `captions-button` / `captions-announcer` with slashes, and those three come from **two** primitives, so the punctuation cannot be read as a grouping by primitive. The page now says so instead of leaving the trap in place.

Also recast "Four parts carry a worked example below" as a count of examples — the same assumption in a second place, since the four examples style eight parts between them.

**Two factual errors in this brief.** Neither reached the page.

1. "`Captions` renders `captions`, `captions-button` and `captions-announcer`" is false. `Captions` (`packages/react/src/index.tsx:1418`) renders `captions`; it is `CaptionsButton` (`:1988`) that renders `captions-button` (`:2027`) and `captions-announcer` (`:2048`).
2. `Captions` is also not the single-part primitive it looks like — it renders `caption-cue` (`:1434`) and `caption-line` (`:1413`, via `defaultCueRenderer`). An early draft used it as the clean one-part foil, which would have argued against the passage's own point.

**A scope call, stated rather than made quietly.** Acceptance criterion 1 is unconditional — "`Contract.mdx` no longer describes a part as a primitive's name" — and two bullets listed as out of scope did exactly that: `data-provider` called `play-button`, `error` and `captions-button` "primitives" and then correctly said "parts" later in the same sentence; `data-state` said "Absent where the primitive has no observable state: `airplay-button`" and described the attribute as "the component's derived state", `component` being a term `CONTEXT.md` lists under *Avoid*. Read as *restructuring* those bullets are out of scope, but a noun swap is not restructuring, and leaving them would have left criterion 1 unmet. The fix is three noun swaps and nothing else.

`part` rather than `primitive` for `data-state` is the accurate word: the attribute is emitted per part. `SeekSlider` renders four parts and only `seek-slider` carries a `data-state` (`:1754`); same shape in `CaptionsButton` and `ErrorDisplay`.

**Worth triaging separately.** This page's part list is materially incomplete: source defines 34 distinct `data-reely-part` values, the list names 24. Missing are `caption-cue`, `caption-line`, `settings-menu`, `settings-menu-root`, `settings-menu-trigger`, `menu-item`, `menu-radio-group`, `menu-radio-item`, `menu-radio-indicator` and `gestures`. Acceptance criterion 4 forbade touching the list, so it is exactly as it was found — but "the full set" is not what it is.

## SIDEPRO-167 — Rename mediaEligible to match the glossary's committed source

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-167/rename-mediaeligible-to-match-the-glossarys-committed-source

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-185](#sidepro-185) — "Output" names two different things once CONTEXT.md defines it
  - [SIDEPRO-183](#sidepro-183) — Define activation identity in CONTEXT.md
  - [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) — The activation identity names in use-activation point at each other's meaning

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** improvement

**Summary:** `mediaEligible` reuses the word `eligible`, which already names an activation phase, for a different concept — a match on source identity that gates whether the media element may mount. `CONTEXT.md` now calls that second concept a **committed source** and records the rename as pending.

**Current behavior:**

Two unrelated things share a word:

* `activation: 'eligible'` — one of the phases in `PreProviderActivation` (`packages/core/src/index.ts`), meaning loading has been permitted.
* `mediaEligible` — in `packages/react/src/use-activation.ts:179`, computed as `eligibleIdentity === currentActivationIdentity`, and used at `packages/react/src/index.tsx:968` to return `null` from `Media` until it is true.

The second is not a phase. It answers "is the source we are rendering the one activation committed to?", which is why it is an identity comparison rather than a state read. `CONTEXT.md`:

> **Committed source**: The source whose media element may mount, because its identity matches the one activation committed to. Named `mediaEligible` in code, pending a rename.

**Desired behavior:**

The identifier says what the glossary says. `sourceCommitted` is the proposed name — it reads correctly at the use site (`if (!sourceCommitted) return null`) and keeps the glossary's noun. Pick a different one if it reads better, but not one containing `eligible`.

**This is an internal rename, not an API change.** Verified: `usePlayer` is declared `const usePlayer` at `packages/react/src/index.tsx:192` and never exported, and the public type re-export block (`index.tsx:157-162`) exports only `PlayerLoadingStrategy`, `PlayerMediaMount` and `PlayerPreload` — not `ActivationBindings`. So no consumer can reach `mediaEligible`, and no changeset is needed. Confirm this still holds before starting rather than trusting it.

**Key interfaces:**

* `packages/react/src/use-activation.ts` — the `ActivationBindings` field (line 27), the computed value (179), the effect dependency (576), and the two returns (592, 599).
* `packages/react/src/index.tsx` — the destructure (945) and the guard (968).
* `packages/react/test/activation.test.tsx` — lines 113 and 139.
* `CONTEXT.md` — drop "Named `mediaEligible` in code, pending a rename." from the **Committed source** entry once the rename lands; leaving it would make the glossary wrong in the other direction.

**Do not touch** `.planning/plans/2026-07-23-issue-7-lazy-activation.md`. It mentions `mediaEligible` several times, but it is a record of what was planned at the time, not documentation of current behaviour. Rewriting it would falsify a historical document.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] No identifier in `packages/react/src` or its tests contains `mediaEligible`.
- [ ] The new name matches the glossary's **Committed source** entry, and `CONTEXT.md`'s pending-rename note is removed.
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm test:storybook` all pass, with no behaviour change — this is a rename, so the existing activation tests passing unchanged is the proof.
- [ ] No changeset, unless the public-surface check above turns out to be wrong — in which case stop and say so rather than shipping a breaking rename quietly.

**Out of scope:**

* Renaming `eligibleIdentity` / `currentActivationIdentity` or anything else in `use-activation.ts` — one rename, tightly scoped.
* Any change to the activation state machine or to `PreProviderActivation`.
* The historical planning document.

**Notes:**

Decided while writing `CONTEXT.md`. The risk of leaving it is the ordinary one — the glossary says one thing, the code says another, and the mismatch is invisible to every check.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-167-rename-mediaeligible-to-match-the-glossarys-committed-source`.

Re-confirmed the public-surface check the brief asks for, and it still holds: `usePlayer` is `const usePlayer` at `index.tsx:192` and unexported; the type re-export block (`:157-162`) exports only `PlayerLoadingStrategy`, `PlayerMediaMount` and `PlayerPreload` from `./use-activation.js`, not `ActivationBindings`; `ActivationBindings` reaches `index.tsx` only as a type import feeding the unexported `PlayerContextValue` (`:53`); and the package exports just `./dist/index.js`. No consumer can reach `mediaEligible`, so no changeset.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/133 (squash-merged, all required checks green).

**What shipped.** `mediaEligible` → `sourceCommitted` across `use-activation.ts` (5 sites), `index.tsx` (2), `activation.test.tsx` (2), plus dropping `CONTEXT.md`'s pending-rename note.

**On the name.** `sourceCommitted` over the glossary's literal `committedSource`: a `committedSource: boolean` misnames a predicate as a noun, reading as though it holds a source. `sourceCommitted` inflects the glossary's noun into a predicate that reads correctly at the gate — `if (!sourceCommitted || source.status === 'failure') return null` — and matches the codebase's other unprefixed adjectival booleans (`buffering`, `seeking`, `muted`).

**Two things beyond the identifier itself.** Both disclosed in the commit message rather than made quietly:

2. `sourceCommitted` sorts differently from `mediaEligible`, so six hand-maintained alphabetical lists move it back into position. `source` sorts before `sourceCommitted`, so the `Media` destructure is a genuine re-sort rather than an append.

**No behaviour change, proven rather than asserted.** The reorders touch four dependency arrays and object literals, where a silently added or dropped dep would be a real behaviour change wearing a rename's clothes. Review normalised both revisions to a common token and diffed the token multisets per file: `activation.test.tsx` is token-identical, `use-activation.ts` differs only in which token carries the trailing `]);`. No dep or property added or removed anywhere. 826 unit and 88 storybook tests pass, identical counts before and after.

**No changeset, confirmed decisively.** Beyond the source-level check, the committed `packages/react/dist/index.d.ts` built from `main` contains zero occurrences of `ActivationBindings` or `mediaEligible` — nothing consumer-reachable ever carried the old name.

**Two pieces of vocabulary drift left alone, worth triaging separately.**

1. `eligibleIdentity` / `setEligibleIdentity` (`use-activation.ts:176,208,237,285`) still back `sourceCommitted`, so line 179 now reads `const sourceCommitted = eligibleIdentity === currentActivationIdentity` — both vocabularies in one line. This issue's out-of-scope list names `eligibleIdentity` explicitly, so it was not touched. Note `committedIdentity` is already taken by a different thing; `committedSourceIdentity` would clear both.
2. `activation.test.tsx:362` — the test title "interaction eligibility is dormant during the first commit of a new source" and its local `renderedSecondSourceAsEligible` describe the committed-source concept in the old vocabulary. Pre-existing prose this change did not author, so it was left as found, same as `style-precedence.test.tsx:258` ("make the media eligible").

<a id="sidepro-174"></a>

## SIDEPRO-174 — Report the media's intrinsic aspect ratio as a CSS custom property

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-174/report-the-medias-intrinsic-aspect-ratio-as-a-css-custom-property

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) — Give published outputs a CONTEXT.md entry and link ADR-0002 from the Styling section
  - [SIDEPRO-178](#sidepro-178) — Complete the data-reely-part list in Contract.mdx and gate it against drift
  - [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) — Record how the library publishes CSS custom properties outward (ADR-0002)

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** Publish the media's measured aspect ratio on the viewport as a CSS custom property, so a consumer can size the player to its content — vertical video, YouTube Shorts, anything not 16:9 — without knowing the ratio in advance.

### Current behavior

Nothing in the library reports the shape of the media it is playing.

* `PlayerState` carries no intrinsic width, height, or aspect ratio. `PlayerQuality` has `width`/`height`, but those describe an encoded rung of an adaptive ladder, and the ladder is empty on native MP4 and on YouTube — so it cannot answer "what shape is this video".
* Consumers size the box themselves, and every example in the repo hardcodes `aspect-ratio: 16 / 9`. `Media` fills its viewport with an `objectFit` default, so non-16:9 content letterboxes rather than distorts. Correct, but never content-shaped.
* YouTube **Shorts URLs already resolve** — source detection accepts a `/shorts/<id>` path alongside `/embed/<id>`, with test coverage. The source half of "render a Short" works today. Only the geometry doesn't.

The result: a consumer rendering a mixed feed of landscape videos and Shorts must hardcode a ratio per item or letterbox everything.

### Desired behavior

When a provider can measure its media's intrinsic dimensions, it publishes them onto the viewport element as a custom property:

```
--reely-media-aspect-ratio: 1080 / 1920;
```

Consumers opt in from their own stylesheet, and the `var()` fallback covers every case where no ratio is known:

```css
[data-reely-part='viewport'] {
  aspect-ratio: var(--reely-media-aspect-ratio, 16 / 9);
}
```

Rules that matter:

* **Written to the DOM, never through React state.** This is a layout value; routing it through `PlayerState` would re-render every consumer of that state once per source, and again on every dimension change. Set it imperatively on the node. A re-render is a defect here, not an implementation detail.
* **It updates.** A rung switch to a differently-shaped level, or a source change, republishes the value.
* **It clears.** Changing to a source whose ratio is not known must remove the property, not leave the previous video's ratio behind. A stale ratio is worse than no ratio, because the fallback stops applying.
* **Unmeasurable providers never set it.** The `var()` fallback is the entire story for them.

Per provider:

| Provider | Source of truth |
| -- | -- |
| native | the media element's own `videoWidth`/`videoHeight` — first at `loadedmetadata`, then on the `resize` event |
| hls | inherits from native, which it already delegates to |
| vimeo | the SDK's `getVideoWidth()` / `getVideoHeight()` (promise-based) |
| youtube | **not reported** — the IFrame API exposes no dimensions |

**Do not infer the ratio from the URL.** A `/shorts/` path is a tempting signal and it was considered and rejected by the maintainer on 2026-07-29: this codebase states the opposite principle for live detection ("derive from the media, never from the source URL or filename"), and the exception is not worth carving. YouTube reports nothing; consumers who know they are rendering a Short can set the ratio themselves, since the `/shorts/` form is already parsed and available to them.

### Key interfaces

* **The token.** A new `--reely-*` custom property, named consistently with the existing ones. Per ADR-0001 (`docs/adr/0001-structural-css-ships-inline.md`), this is the "token with an inline `var()` default" category — a structural value consumers plausibly restyle from CSS — so it belongs in the documented token table even though the reader here is the consumer rather than a primitive. `PosterImage`'s `object-fit: var(--reely-poster-fit, cover)` is the existing shape to follow.
* **The viewport node.** `Player.Viewport` already registers its DOM node with the player context; that registration is the existing seam for reaching the element the property goes on.
* **The provider adapter surface.** Adapters own their media element or mount, so each knows its own dimensions. Choose whatever channel reaches the viewport node without adding a `PlayerState` field — the no-re-render constraint above is the requirement; the mechanism is the implementer's call.

### Acceptance criteria

- [ ] A native source with a non-16:9 frame publishes the custom property on the viewport once metadata has loaded, carrying that frame's ratio.
- [ ] A consumer rule reading `aspect-ratio: var(--reely-media-aspect-ratio, 16 / 9)` sizes the box to the content, and falls back to 16/9 wherever no ratio is published.
- [ ] HLS inherits the behaviour through the native provider it delegates to.
- [ ] Vimeo publishes the ratio from the SDK's reported dimensions.
- [ ] YouTube never publishes it — a test asserts the property is **absent**, not defaulted.
- [ ] Switching to a differently-shaped source republishes the value; switching to a source whose ratio is unknown clears it rather than leaving the previous one.
- [ ] Publishing the value triggers no React re-render — a test asserts the render count is unchanged across the metadata event.
- [ ] A fixture story in the workbench renders a vertical source (a small local asset is fine) so the behaviour is visible, and the box takes the video's shape rather than letterboxing inside 16:9.
- [ ] The token is documented alongside the other `--reely-*` tokens.
- [ ] Changeset added: this adds a public token.

### Out of scope

* **A** `Player.Viewport` **prop that applies the ratio itself** (e.g. `aspectRatio="auto"`). Deliberately deferred by the maintainer on 2026-07-29 — consumers write the CSS for now. It raises layout-shift-on-metadata, first-paint and theme-interaction questions that deserve their own issue.
* Adding intrinsic dimensions to `PlayerState`.
* Inferring geometry from a `/shorts/` path or any other URL shape.
* Changing `Media`'s `objectFit` default or any cropping behaviour — settled separately.
* Restyling the reference example or the theme beyond what demonstrating the token requires.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-174-report-the-medias-intrinsic-aspect-ratio-as-a-css-custom`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/134 (squash-merged, all required checks green). All ten acceptance criteria met.

**How it travels.** An optional `subscribeDimensions` adapter member, modelled on `subscribeCues`, relayed by `PlayerController` and consumed by `Viewport` in a `useEffect` that writes the property imperatively. Nothing was added to `PlayerState` or `PlayerEventDetailMap`. The channel carries plain numbers by necessity as well as by choice: `packages/core/tsconfig.no-dom.json` compiles core's public types with `lib: ["ES2022"], types: []`, and that gate was proven to cover the new surface by adding an `HTMLVideoElement` field and watching `tsc` reject it.

**HLS is deliberately not engine-gated,** unlike `subscribeCues`. Captions are gated because hls.js owns them; both engines play into the same `<video>`, so neither owns its intrinsic size. Tested across both engine paths.

**A bug found in review, not in the brief.** Vimeo's `teardown()` cleared cues — under a comment stating the principle, "a retry must not inherit them" — but not dimensions, which were cleared only in `destroy()`. Since `retry()` calls `teardown()` then `start()`, the previous embed's ratio survived a retry until the new one answered. That is exactly the failure this issue names, and it made the changeset's own claim untrue. Fixed by mirroring the cue machinery; `destroy()`'s explicit clear then became a redundant second publish and was removed, leaving the same division of labour cues use. The test was written first and reproduced the leak (`expected { width: 1080, height: 1920 } to be undefined`).

**Verification worth recording.**
- The no-re-render criterion was mutation-tested two ways — a real `PlayerState` field in the controller's dimension setter, and a forced render in the `Viewport` subscription. Both turn the test red, so it is load-bearing rather than decorative.
- The fallback resolving to `16 / 9` was the one criterion originally only *demonstrated*, in a story tagged `!test`. It is now asserted in real Chromium. The insight that made this cheap: testing the fallback needs no media, so it belongs in the deterministic Storybook suite rather than behind the real-playback exemption. A second story drives the genuine write path so the first cannot pass by coincidence.
- Two rounds of false-greens were caught during implementation, where optional chaining (`subscribeDimensions?.()`) let tests pass against a missing member.

**Deviation from the brief, endorsed by both review axes.** The brief asks for this in `theme.css`'s token table, citing ADR-0001's "token with an inline `var()` default" category. That category is constituted by *a primitive reading the value* — the ADR's own wording, with `PosterImage`'s `object-fit: var(--reely-poster-fit, cover)` as its exemplar. Nothing reads this one back. `CONTEXT.md:72` defines a token the same way, and `Theme.mdx`'s table lists values a consumer may *set*, which this is not — setting it only gets it overwritten at the next `loadedmetadata`. It is documented in `Contract.mdx` beside the other library outputs, with a pointer from `theme.css`'s header for discoverability. **Suggest correcting the issue text rather than the code.**

**Two things left for triage.**

1. **ADR-0001 may need a fourth category.** Its boundary list has three entries and none covers "written outward by the library". This feature is the repo's first CSS custom property the library writes — there were previously zero `setProperty` calls anywhere. Amending an ADR is a maintainer decision, so it was not touched.
2. **`apps/storybook/README.md` says stories must "never render `Player.Media`"** with no carve-out for the `real-playback` / `!test` exemption, which three story files now rely on. Pre-existing inaccuracy; this change makes it the third.

**The fixture.** `tracer.mp4` is 320×180 and cannot show a portrait ratio, and the repo had no fixture-generation script and no recoverable provenance for it. `tracer-portrait.mp4` is 8,373 bytes at 360×640 (H.264, 1s, no audio, faststart), and `apps/storybook/README.md` now records the exact `ffmpeg` command that produced it.

<a id="sidepro-177"></a>

## SIDEPRO-177 — Record how the library publishes CSS custom properties outward (ADR-0002)

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-177/record-how-the-library-publishes-css-custom-properties-outward-adr

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) — Report the media's intrinsic aspect ratio as a CSS custom property
  - [SIDEPRO-185](#sidepro-185) — "Output" names two different things once CONTEXT.md defines it
  - [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) — Give published outputs a CONTEXT.md entry and link ADR-0002 from the Styling section

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** Reely now writes a CSS custom property outward for consumers to read, and no architecture record covers that direction. Write ADR-0002 for it, leaving ADR-0001's subject intact.

**Current behavior:**

Every CSS decision on record runs *inward* — how a value the primitive needs reaches the DOM. ADR-0001 ("Structural CSS ships inline, not as a stylesheet") answers that with a three-entry boundary: inline on the primitive; a token with an inline `var()` default; or `theme.css`. All three are delivery mechanisms for values the library consumes.

[SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) introduced the opposite direction. The library measures the media's intrinsic size and publishes `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` on the viewport part, imperatively, for the consumer's own stylesheet to read. Before it there were **zero** `setProperty` calls anywhere in the packages — verified at filing time, and still exactly the two that shipped with it.

That leaves a contributor with a real question and no answer: a new CSS custom property gets three documented options, none of which fit one the library writes. The wrong answer is already attractive — [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174)'s own brief called it a token and sent it to `theme.css`'s token table, and that was wrong for a reason worth recording rather than re-deriving.

**Desired behavior:**

A new ADR records outbound publication as its own decision, and ADR-0001 keeps its subject.

The material is already settled and does not need re-litigating — it needs writing down:

* **The rule.** The library may publish a measurement as a CSS custom property, written imperatively on the part it describes. These are *outputs*, in the same family as `data-state`, not tokens.
* **Why not a token.** `CONTEXT.md` defines a **Token** as a property *a primitive reads* inline with a fallback, so a consumer can change a value from a stylesheet. Direction is part of the definition, not incidental to it. `Theme.mdx`'s table lists values a consumer may *set*; setting a published one only gets it overwritten at the next measurement.
* **Where they are documented.** `Contract.mdx`, beside the other library outputs — with a pointer from `theme.css`'s header so the name is still discoverable from where a reader looks for `--reely-*`.

Consequences worth recording, because each is a way to get the category wrong:

* A consumer must not set one. The library overwrites it.
* Unknown must **remove** the property rather than publish a zero or empty value, or the consumer's `var()` fallback stops applying. A stale or zeroed value is worse than an absent one.
* The value must not travel through `PlayerState`. Publication is a layout concern; routing it through state re-renders every consumer of that state on every change.
* `@reely/core`'s public types compile with no DOM lib at all, so the value travels as plain data and the React layer does the DOM write. That constraint shapes any future instance too.

**Key interfaces:**

* `docs/adr/` — a new ADR numbered 0002. Match ADR-0001's filename convention: a claim in kebab-case, not a topic (`0001-structural-css-ships-inline.md`).
* ADR-0001 — gains a cross-reference only. Its **subject, title and three-entry boundary list stay exactly as they are.** Adding a fourth bullet there was considered and rejected by the maintainer on 2026-07-29: that list answers "where does a structural value the primitive needs live?", which is a different question from "how does the library publish a measurement outward?".
* `CONTEXT.md` — the **Token** entry is the authority for the direction argument. Quote or paraphrase it; do not change it.
* `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` — the first and only instance. Record it as such, with a pointer to [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) / PR #134.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `docs/adr/` contains a new ADR numbered 0002, its filename a claim in ADR-0001's style.
- [ ] It states the rule: the library may publish measurements as CSS custom properties written on the part they describe, and these are outputs rather than tokens.
- [ ] It argues why such a property is not a token, resting on `CONTEXT.md`'s directional definition rather than on assertion.
- [ ] It names `Contract.mdx` as the documentation home and says why `theme.css`'s token table is not.
- [ ] It records all four consequences above.
- [ ] It records `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` as the first instance.
- [ ] ADR-0001 gains a cross-reference to ADR-0002 and is otherwise byte-identical — same title, same three boundary entries, same Consequences.
- [ ] Both ADRs read as one another's neighbour: 0002 references 0001 where the inbound/outbound split matters.
- [ ] Matches ADR-0001's voice — prose that argues and records what was learned, including where a previous reading was wrong. Not a bullet skeleton.

**Out of scope:**

* Adding a fourth bullet to ADR-0001's boundary list, or otherwise changing its subject. Explicitly decided against.
* Changing `CONTEXT.md`, `Contract.mdx`, `theme.css` or `Theme.mdx`. [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) already documented the property itself; this issue documents the *category*.
* Any code change whatsoever. Documentation only.
* Generalising to properties nobody has asked for, or inventing a second instance.
* `apps/storybook/README.md`'s "never render `Player.Media`" rule, which lacks a carve-out for the `real-playback` / `!test` exemption three story files now rely on. Real, but a separate finding and a separate issue.

**Notes:**

Surfaced during the Factory session that landed [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174). Both `/review` axes independently endorsed documenting the property as an output rather than a token, and the Spec axis recommended correcting [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174)'s issue text rather than the code — so the reasoning this ADR records has already been tested twice.

There is no prior rejection: `.out-of-scope/` holds only `as-child.md` and `provider-adapter-decomposition.md`, neither related. `docs/adr/` currently holds only `0001`, so there is no numbering precedent beyond it.

## SIDEPRO-178 — Complete the data-reely-part list in Contract.mdx and gate it against drift

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-178/complete-the-data-reely-part-list-in-contractmdx-and-gate-it-against

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166) — Describe parts as parts in Contract.mdx, not as primitive names
  - [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) — Report the media's intrinsic aspect ratio as a CSS custom property

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** `Contract.mdx` presents its `data-reely-part` list as the styling contract, but it names roughly 24 of the ~34 values the library actually emits — and nothing checks it, so it drifted silently and will again.

**Current behavior:**

The page's `data-reely-part` bullet lists part names, and reads as a catalogue: the section around it tells you to "style and query against these attributes rather than internal class names." A reader has no way to tell the list is partial.

At filing time, deriving the set from the React package's source gave ~34 distinct values against ~24 documented. Absent from the page: `caption-cue`, `caption-line`, `settings-menu`, `settings-menu-root`, `settings-menu-trigger`, `menu-item`, `menu-radio-group`, `menu-radio-item`, `menu-radio-indicator`, `gestures`.

**Re-derive both sets yourself rather than trusting those figures.** They came from one file and the codebase moves; the whole point of this issue is that a hand-maintained list goes stale.

The pattern in what's missing is worth noting: it is the captions-rendering, settings-menu and gestures families more or less entirely. That suggests the list was accurate when written and was never revisited as primitives landed — which is exactly the failure a check prevents and prose cannot.

Nothing gates it today. Four contract tests exist and none covers parts; the repo does already have the shape this needs, in tests that parse a source artifact and assert a documented set against it.

**Desired behavior:**

The page names every part the library emits, and a check fails when the page and the source disagree — in **both** directions. A part added to a primitive without being documented should fail. A part named on the page that no longer exists should also fail, since a documented selector that matches nothing is worse than an undocumented one: a consumer writes CSS against it and gets silence.

The failure must name the offending part. "The lists differ" sends someone diffing 34 strings by hand.

**Key interfaces:**

* `Contract.mdx`'s `data-reely-part` bullet — the documented set.
* Part values are string literals on the primitives in the React package. Derive the set from source; do not hand-maintain a second copy of it in the test, or the test becomes the thing that drifts.
* The contract-test convention — tests that read a source artifact's text and assert a property of it, naming what failed. `theme.contract.test.ts` and the React package's theme test (which parses the stylesheet and reports the offending selector) are the closest precedents. `scripts/docs-examples.mjs`'s drift gate for `example:` fences is the other candidate home. Judge which fits and say why.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] The `data-reely-part` bullet names every distinct part value the library emits.
- [ ] A check fails when a part exists in source but not on the page. **Demonstrate the red** — add a part to a primitive, run it, restore.
- [ ] A check fails when the page names a part that no source emits. **Demonstrate that red too** — they are separate directions and one passing does not imply the other.
- [ ] The failure output names the specific part(s), not just that the sets differ.
- [ ] The test derives both sets programmatically; neither is a hand-written list inside the test.
- [ ] Prose stays true. [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166) established that a part name is not a primitive's name and that the list's slash-grouping does not group by primitive — adding ten values must not reintroduce either error.
- [ ] The existing entries' order and grouping are preserved unless adding forces a change; if it does, say what changed and why.

**Out of scope:**

* Renaming any part, or changing what any primitive emits. This documents what exists.
* Restructuring the page or its other bullets (`data-state`, `data-provider`, `data-buffering`).
* Documenting each newly listed part's `data-state` values, or giving any of them a worked styling example — the page deliberately carries four examples, "four, not twenty".
* The `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` section added by [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174).

**Notes:**

Surfaced while working [SIDEPRO-166](#sidepro-166), whose acceptance criteria forbade touching the list ("No change to any `data-reely-part` value, and no change to the list of them"), so the incompleteness shipped as found and was recorded on that issue instead.

No prior rejection: `.out-of-scope/` holds only `as-child.md` and `provider-adapter-decomposition.md`, neither related.

The gate is the durable half of this issue. Adding ten names fixes today; the check is what stops the eleventh from going missing.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-178-complete-the-data-reely-part-list-in-contractmdx-and-gate-it`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/136 (squash-merged, branch deleted).

Re-derived both sets rather than trusting the brief's figures: 34 emitted, 24 documented, and the ten absent were exactly the ones the brief named — no discrepancy. Added them to the bullet, preserving existing order; the additions extend the captions group at its tail and add two new slash groups rather than reflowing what was there.

The gate is `apps/storybook/stories/parts.contract.test.ts`, a vitest contract test beside the page rather than an addition to `scripts/docs-examples.mjs` — that script rewrites generated `example:` fences from a fixture, and this bullet is inline code in a sentence with no fixture that could generate it. The rationale is recorded in the file header, since the brief asked for the judgement rather than a default. Both sets are derived; neither is written down. Both reds were demonstrated, then demonstrated again after review fixes rewrote the assertions.

Landing gate: prettier, eslint, `tsc -b`, 860 tests across 46 files, and `/review` on both axes. Seven findings were applied before landing, the substantive one being that the original guard against non-literal emissions overclaimed — it missed the object-spread form entirely, and a template literal both passed it and registered `${x}` as a phantom part. It is now an occurrence count: every mention of `data-reely-part` in the sources must be claimed by a string-literal attribute. Also fixed: `test(` → `it(` to match the four sibling contract tests, "library emits" → "primitive emits" per `CONTEXT.md`, a Linear identifier where the repo cites `#178`, a missing vacuity guard on both derivations, and a non-recursive source scan that would have thrown `EISDIR` on a future subdirectory.

Known limit, recorded in the PR rather than papered over: the occurrence count reads raw text, so a comment mentioning `data-reely-part` in the React source would red spuriously. It fails loudly and is one reword away. What the count genuinely cannot see is an attribute assembled from fragments, and the comment says so.

Also verified: no `packages/` change, and the `data-provider` bullet's claim about landing on exactly 11 named parts still holds — none of the ten new parts emits it.

## SIDEPRO-179 — The activation identity names in use-activation point at each other's meaning

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-179/the-activation-identity-names-in-use-activation-point-at-each-others

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) — Rename mediaEligible to match the glossary's committed source
  - [SIDEPRO-183](#sidepro-183) — Define activation identity in CONTEXT.md

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** `eligibleIdentity` holds the activation identity that has been committed to. `committedIdentity` holds a latest-values snapshot of the current configuration and records no commitment at all. Each name describes the other one's job, and the collision is why [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) could not finish the vocabulary it started.

**Current behavior:**

Three identity-shaped names live in the activation hook, and only one of them is accurate.

* `currentActivationIdentity` — the string key from `activationIdentityKey(sourceKey, loading, configuration)`, computed for the current render. Says what it holds. Fine.
* `eligibleIdentity` — state, `string | undefined`. It stores a previously-computed activation identity, and the derived value reads `sourceCommitted = eligibleIdentity === currentActivationIdentity`. So it holds *the identity activation committed to* — the exact concept `CONTEXT.md` names **Committed source**. [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) renamed the derived value to `sourceCommitted` but was scoped to that one identifier, so its input still says "eligible" and a single line now carries both vocabularies.
* `committedIdentity` — a ref holding `{ configuration, loading, nativeOptions, sourceKey }`, reassigned in a layout effect on every render and read from inside callbacks. That is the latest-values ref pattern: it exists so a closure can read fresh configuration without re-subscribing. It records no commitment. Its type is `CommittedIdentity`.

So the name `eligibleIdentity` wants is taken, by something that isn't a commitment. That is why the obvious fix from [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167)'s review — "rename it to `committedIdentity`" — doesn't work, and why the suggested `committedSourceIdentity` would only have parked a second "committed" thing beside the first.

**Verify the reading of** `committedIdentity` **before acting on it.** Check every site that reads the ref. If it turns out to record a genuine commitment rather than acting as a latest-values snapshot, **stop and report that** — the whole shape of this issue depends on it, and a rename built on a wrong reading is worse than the current names.

**Desired behavior:**

Each of the three names says what it holds, in one vocabulary:

* the state that stores the committed activation identity is named for commitment;
* the ref is named for what it actually is — the latest configuration values, captured for callbacks;
* `sourceCommitted`'s definition reads in a single vocabulary rather than two.

Pick names by reading them at their use sites, as [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) did — `sourceCommitted` won there over the glossary's literal `committedSource` because a boolean predicate should not be named like a noun. Same judgment applies here.

**Key interfaces:**

* The activation hook's `eligibleIdentity` state and its setter.
* Its `committedIdentity` ref and the `CommittedIdentity` type.
* `currentActivationIdentity` and `activationIdentityKey` — accurate today; leave them unless a rename genuinely forces a change, and say so if it does.
* `CONTEXT.md`'s **Committed source** entry — the authority for what the committed concept is called.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] No identifier uses "eligible" for the committed-activation-identity concept.
- [ ] The ref's name describes what it holds; its type name follows it.
- [ ] `sourceCommitted`'s definition reads in one vocabulary end to end.
- [ ] **No behaviour change.** This is a rename, so the existing activation tests passing unchanged is the proof — if you find yourself editing test logic or assertions, something has gone wrong.
- [ ] No public API change. Verify it rather than trusting this brief: confirm the hook and these types are not reachable from the package's published entry, and add no changeset if they are not. If any is reachable, stop and say so rather than shipping a breaking rename quietly.
- [ ] Any comment reasoning in the old vocabulary moves with the identifier.

**Out of scope:**

* `activation: 'eligible'` — the activation phase, meaning loading has been permitted. A legitimate, glossary-backed name for a genuinely different concept. Do not sweep it, and do not touch the state machine or `PreProviderActivation`.
* Adding or changing `CONTEXT.md` entries. If this work suggests the glossary needs an "activation identity" entry, say so and it will be triaged as domain modelling — that is a maintainer decision, not a side effect of a rename.
* Any behavioural change to activation, the observer wiring, or the latest-values pattern itself. The ref stays a ref; only its name changes.
* Historical planning documents, which record what was planned at the time.
* Pre-existing "eligible" prose elsewhere in the test suite that this change does not author.

**Notes:**

Deliberate follow-up to [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167), which out-scoped these identifiers by name ("one rename, tightly scoped") and recorded the leftover on its completion comment.

No prior rejection: `.out-of-scope/` holds only `as-child.md` and `provider-adapter-decomposition.md`, neither related.

The interesting half is not the renaming — it is that the current names actively mislead. A reader who trusts `committedIdentity` to be the commitment, and `eligibleIdentity` to be a phase, has it exactly backwards.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-179-the-activation-identity-names-in-use-activation-point-at`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/137 (squash-merged, branch deleted).

**The stop condition was checked before anything else.** The ref is a latest-values snapshot, not a commitment record: assigned unconditionally at the top of the layout effect every render, and read at exactly three sites — `isCurrentObservation`, `isCurrentLoad`, and the observer catch block — each comparing the latest values against ones a closure captured earlier. Nothing contradicted the brief's reading, so the rename proceeded.

Final names: the state is `committedIdentity` / `setCommittedIdentity`, so `sourceCommitted = committedIdentity === currentActivationIdentity` reads in one vocabulary. The ref is `latestInputsRef`, typed `ActivationInputs` — `configuration`, `loading`, `nativeOptions` and `sourceKey` are exactly the inputs that decide activation, three of which compose `activationIdentityKey`. The closure locals read `inputs`.

Worth recording: the ref was first named `renderedRef` / `RenderedValues`, and both `/review` axes caught it for the same fault this issue exists to remove. `CONTEXT.md` binds "rendered" to the DOM (**Part** is "a named element in the rendered DOM"), and `activation.test.tsx` already uses that sense for this feature, so `rendered.sourceKey` would have read as the key currently mounted — which is the thing `sourceCommitted` gates. The locals were then `latest` before settling on `inputs`: `current` and `latest` are synonyms, so the two comparison blocks in `isCurrentLoad` would have contrasted on recency when the distinction they exist to draw is kind — session values versus the latest render's inputs.

Landing gate: prettier, eslint, `tsc -b`, 860 tests across 46 files, and `/review` on both axes.

Verified rather than assumed:
- **No behaviour change.** 23 lines each way in one file, every hunk an identifier substitution. No test file modified — `packages/react/test/activation.test.tsx` passes untouched.
- **No public API change, no changeset.** `packages/react`'s `exports` publishes only `./dist/index.js` and `./theme.css`; `index.tsx` re-exports just `PlayerLoadingStrategy`, `PlayerMediaMount` and `PlayerPreload` from `use-activation.js`, imports `useActivation` without re-exporting it, and `ActivationInputs` is not exported from the module at all.
- **Out of scope respected.** `activation: 'eligible'` untouched at both sites; no `CONTEXT.md` change; no state machine or `PreProviderActivation` change.
- **No comment needed rewording** — none of the file's comments reasoned in the old vocabulary.

The glossary does need an **Activation identity** entry, as this issue anticipated. Filed as [SIDEPRO-183](#sidepro-183) rather than actioned here, since the brief made that a maintainer decision.

## SIDEPRO-180 — Document the spec label as an intentional exemption from the triage state machine

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-29
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-180/document-the-spec-label-as-an-intentional-exemption-from-the-triage

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - SIDEPRO-56 — Scenario generator: origin grid × fleet-statistics vehicle mix × fill volumes
  - [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) — Reely MVP: product contract and delivery tracker

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) is deliberately exempt from the triage state machine, but that exemption is recorded only inside the issue it applies to. Triage looks at `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`, finds nothing, and flags it as untriaged — as happened on 2026-07-29.

**Current behavior:**

`docs/agents/triage-labels.md` maps the five canonical state roles and the three category labels, and states the invariant: alongside its state label, every issue gets exactly one category label. Nothing in it mentions any exemption.

[SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) ("Reely MVP: product contract and delivery tracker") carries only the project's own `spec` domain label — no state role, no category role. That is intentional, and its body explains why:

> Carries the `spec` label: this is the durable product contract, not work to pick up — `/factory` ignores it because it queues on `ready-for-agent`.

The reasoning is sound and the arrangement works. The problem is purely where it is written down. A triage sweep reads the label mapping, not the body of the issue it is about to classify, so a `spec` issue presents as never-triaged every time. Applying a state role to silence that would be worse than the noise: it is a living contract, and `ready-for-agent` in particular would queue a document as work.

**Desired behavior:**

`docs/agents/triage-labels.md` records that `spec` marks an issue as outside the state machine, so a sweep can recognise it and move on. A reader should come away knowing three things: which label marks the exemption, why such an issue carries neither a state nor a category role, and that the Factory's Queue ignores it by construction rather than by convention — it selects on `ready-for-agent`, which these issues do not carry.

Keep it short. This is a mapping file, not an essay — the surrounding entries are a table plus a few lines each.

**Key interfaces:**

* `docs/agents/triage-labels.md` — the file triage actually consults. The exemption belongs here because this is where a sweep looks.
* The `spec` label itself is one of the project's own domain labels, not a Factory triage label. Say what it means without implying the Factory owns it.
* [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) is the only current instance; the exemption should be stated as a rule rather than as a special case for one issue, since it will apply to any future contract or umbrella document.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `docs/agents/triage-labels.md` states that a `spec`-labelled issue is outside the state machine and carries neither a state nor a category role.
- [ ] It says why — a durable contract or umbrella document is not a unit of work, and giving it `ready-for-agent` would queue a document for implementation.
- [ ] It notes that the Queue ignores such issues by construction, because selection requires `ready-for-agent`.
- [ ] The existing state-role and category-label tables and the "exactly one of each" invariant are unchanged apart from being scoped by the new exemption.
- [ ] It stays proportionate to the file — a short section, not a rewrite.

**Out of scope:**

* Changing [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179)'s labels, or any label on any issue. This documents an existing arrangement; it does not apply it.
* Introducing new labels, or promoting `spec` to a Factory-owned triage label.
* Changing the Factory's Queue-selection behaviour. It already does the right thing; this only records why.
* `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`, or `docs/agents/domain.md`.

**Notes:**

Surfaced during a `/triage` sweep on 2026-07-29 that flagged [SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) as the sole unlabeled issue. It is not unlabeled in any meaningful sense — the sweep simply had no way to know that, which is the whole defect.

No prior rejection: `.out-of-scope/` holds only `as-child.md` and `provider-adapter-decomposition.md`, neither related.

Small, but it pays out every sweep. The alternative is re-deciding the same question each time, and eventually deciding it wrong.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-180-document-the-spec-label-as-an-intentional-exemption-from-the`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-29*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/138 (squash-merged, branch deleted).

`docs/agents/triage-labels.md` gains a short `## Issues outside the state machine` section, and the category invariant gains a qualifying clause — left absolute, "every issue gets exactly one category label" contradicts the exemption three lines below, and a reader who stops at the category table (the exact reading that misfired) would still conclude a category belongs on a spec issue. Both tables are byte-identical.

**The review changed the substance, not just the wording.** The first draft said "nothing has to remember to skip these". That is false, and I verified it against the Factory plugin: `skills/factory-adopt/SKILL.md` states as a hard rule that "every open issue carries exactly one category label and exactly one state label when the sweep ends — not zero, not two." Only Queue selection is free; a sweep must apply the exemption deliberately. The two mechanisms are now stated apart, because collapsing them would have been a worse failure than the gap this issue was filed to close — it reads as permission to do nothing, addressed to exactly the reader who has to do something.

Three other review findings applied: the point-in-time "[SIDEPRO-139](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/179) is the current instance" was dropped, since nothing would prompt an update when a second spec issue is filed; "it scopes both tables above" was replaced, since a project label cannot scope a mapping of role names; and the `spec` label is now described accurately as a **Side projects** team label whose meaning here is this repo's convention — SIDEPRO-56 in another project carries `spec` alongside `ready-for-human` and `Feature`, so a team-wide claim would have been untrue.

Landing gate: prettier, eslint, `tsc -b`, 860 tests across 46 files, and `/review` on both axes.

Out of scope respected: no label changed on any issue, no new label introduced, `spec` not promoted to a Factory triage label, and no change to `AGENTS.md`, `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md` or `docs/agents/domain.md`.

Two things surfaced in the PR rather than actioned:

1. **This file is Factory stamp content**, generated from the plugin's `templates/stamp/docs/agents/triage-labels.md`, so it now diverges from the template. Verified safe — `/factory-adopt` does not overwrite a differing `docs/agents/*` file, it surfaces the diff for the maintainer — but the next adopt run will raise it.
2. **The exemption contradicts the upstream Factory rule from the outside.** A sweep that reads the plugin and not this file still misfires. Recording it here is what this issue asked for and is correct as scoped, but closing the loop fully would need the plugin rule to acknowledge that per-repo exemptions exist. That is a Factory change, not a Reely one.

<a id="sidepro-182"></a>

## SIDEPRO-182 — Give published outputs a CONTEXT.md entry and link ADR-0002 from the Styling section

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-182/give-published-outputs-a-contextmd-entry-and-link-adr-0002-from-the

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) — Report the media's intrinsic aspect ratio as a CSS custom property
  - [SIDEPRO-183](#sidepro-183) — Define activation identity in CONTEXT.md
  - [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) — Record how the library publishes CSS custom properties outward (ADR-0002)
  - [SIDEPRO-185](#sidepro-185) — "Output" names two different things once CONTEXT.md defines it

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Surfaced by the Standards axis of `/review` during the Factory session that landed [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) (ADR-0002, PR #135). Filed rather than fixed because [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177)'s brief put `CONTEXT.md` explicitly out of scope.

## The gap

ADR-0002 establishes **output** as a category — a CSS custom property the library writes onto the part it describes, for a consumer's stylesheet to read, in the same family as `data-state`. That term is now load-bearing in three places: the ADR itself, `theme.css`'s header, and `Contract.mdx`. `CONTEXT.md` has no entry for it.

`docs/agents/domain.md` treats a needed concept missing from the glossary as a signal of a real gap, so this is the documented trigger for an entry rather than a stylistic preference.

Two specifics:

* **No glossary entry.** The Styling section defines **Token** (`CONTEXT.md:72-75`) but has nothing for the outbound direction. A contributor reading only `CONTEXT.md` still meets the same fork [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) was filed to close — they just meet it one file earlier.
* `_Avoid_: custom property` **on the Token entry** now over-reaches. It was written when every `--reely-*` name was a token; it now reads as forbidding the exact phrase the new category needs, and `Contract.mdx:268` already uses "custom property" correctly for a non-token.
* **One-way link.** `CONTEXT.md:57` says "See [ADR-0001](<docs/adr/0001-structural-css-ships-inline.md>) for why the first two are separate." ADR-0002 gets no reciprocal link, so the glossary points at half the CSS decisions on record.

## Suggested shape

An **Output** entry in the Styling section, defined by direction (library writes, consumer reads) to sit opposite **Token**, with `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` as the instance and a pointer to ADR-0002. Narrow the Token entry's `_Avoid_` list so it no longer forbids "custom property" outright. Add ADR-0002 beside the existing ADR-0001 link.

Whether the entry is called **Output** or something else is a naming decision for the maintainer — ADR-0002 uses "output" but does not claim the glossary must.

## Out of scope

* Changing ADR-0001 or ADR-0002. Both landed in PR #135 and the argument is settled; this issue records it in the glossary, it does not re-litigate it.
* Any code change.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** `CONTEXT.md` has no entry for **Output**, though the term is load-bearing in shipped prose across three files, and its Styling section links ADR-0001 without ADR-0002.

**Current behavior:**

`CONTEXT.md`'s Styling section defines **Structural geometry**, **Appearance** and **Token**. All three describe values travelling *inward* — what a primitive reads, or what the optional stylesheet carries. Nothing names the outward direction.

The word is nevertheless already in use, consistently, in shipped prose:

* `Contract.mdx` opens its aspect-ratio section with "One more output, and the only one that is not an attribute", and its worked example says "`--reely-media-aspect-ratio` is an output, not a token".
* ADR-0002 argues the category at length — published measurements are outputs, "in the same family as `data-state`".
* `theme.css`'s header names the property as deliberately absent from the token table and points at `Contract.mdx`.

So a contributor meets the term three times and finds it defined nowhere. `docs/agents/domain.md` treats exactly that as the signal of a real gap.

Separately, the Styling section's lead reads "See ADR-0001 for why the first two are separate." ADR-0002 now exists and is unlinked, so the glossary points at half the CSS decisions on record.

**Desired behavior:**

An **Output** entry in the Styling section, and both ADRs linked from the section lead. The decisions below were settled with the maintainer during triage and are not open:

* **One broad entry, not a narrow one.** An output is anything the library states about itself for a consumer's CSS or tests to read — a part name, a `data-state`, or a measurement written as a CSS custom property. A narrower "published property" entry covering only custom properties would contradict `Contract.mdx`, which already calls the attributes outputs too.
* **Defined by direction.** The library writes it and the consumer reads it, the opposite direction to a **Token** — the property a primitive reads and a consumer sets. Direction is the whole distinction; rest the definition on it rather than on assertion.
* **Placed immediately after Token**, so the contrast sits beside the thing it contrasts with.
* **No `_Avoid_` line.** The Token contrast belongs in the definition, the way **Viewport** says "Not the browser viewport" and **Source** says "Distinct from the `<source>` elements". Nine of eleven entries carry `_Avoid_`; those two do not, and this is the same shape.
* **ADR-0002 linked by extending the section lead**, not from inside the entry — no entry in this glossary carries a link. "the first two" stays accurate once Output is appended, since it counts Structural geometry and Appearance.

**A claim in the issue body above is retracted.** It said the Token entry's `_Avoid_: variable, custom property` now over-reaches, reading as a ban on the phrase the new category needs. That is wrong: `_Avoid_` lines are scoped to their own entry, and the glossary proves it against itself — **Part** avoids "element", yet the file writes "the media element filling its viewport" and "the `<source>` elements the native provider renders". **Leave the Token entry exactly as it is.**

**Key interfaces:**

* `CONTEXT.md` — the Styling section: its lead line, and the new entry.
* `CONTEXT.md`'s **Token** entry — what Output is defined against. Paraphrase its direction; do not change it.
* ADR-0002 — the settled argument. The glossary summarises, the ADR reasons; do not restate the ADR in the entry.
* `Contract.mdx` — the authority for what "output" already means here. The entry must not narrow it.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `CONTEXT.md`'s Styling section contains an **Output** entry, positioned immediately after **Token**.
- [ ] The entry defines an output by direction — the library writes, the consumer reads — and names all three kinds: a part name, a `data-state`, and a measurement written as a CSS custom property.
- [ ] It states the Token contrast in its own text and carries no `_Avoid_` line.
- [ ] The Styling section's lead links both ADR-0001 and ADR-0002, and its claim about "the first two" is still accurate.
- [ ] The **Token** entry is byte-identical, `_Avoid_` line included.
- [ ] Every other entry, in all three sections, is byte-identical.
- [ ] The entry is proportionate — the glossary's entries run two to four lines each.
- [ ] `pnpm format:check` passes.

**Out of scope:**

* Changing the **Token**, **Structural geometry** or **Appearance** entries in any way.
* Adding an **Activation identity** entry — that is [SIDEPRO-183](#sidepro-183), landing separately in the Loading section. Both touch `CONTEXT.md`; expect to rebase if that lands first.
* Changing ADR-0001 or ADR-0002. Both are settled and landed in #135.
* Changing `Contract.mdx`, `theme.css` or `Theme.mdx`. [SIDEPRO-174](#sidepro-174) and [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) documented the property and its category; this documents the *word*.
* Any code change. Documentation only, so no changeset — matching this repo's other docs-only commits.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-182-give-published-outputs-a-contextmd-entry-and-link-adr-0002`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/139 (squash-merged, all seven required checks green).

`CONTEXT.md` gains an **Output** entry immediately after **Token**, defined by direction — the library writes, the consumer reads — naming a part name, a `data-state`, and a measurement written as a CSS custom property. The Styling section lead now links ADR-0002 beside ADR-0001. The **Token** entry and every other entry are byte-identical.

`/review`'s Spec axis returned zero findings. Its Standards axis raised five; three were closed by the Agent Brief's own settled decisions (the absent `_Avoid_` line, the Styling placement, the three-kind enumeration). The remaining two — the word "output" already naming a non-consumer-readable style property in `index.tsx`'s comments, and the now-stale axis enumeration in `CONTEXT.md`'s intro — are filed as [SIDEPRO-185](#sidepro-185).

## SIDEPRO-183 — Define activation identity in CONTEXT.md

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-29
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-183/define-activation-identity-in-contextmd

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) — The activation identity names in use-activation point at each other's meaning
  - [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) — Rename mediaEligible to match the glossary's committed source
  - [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) — Give published outputs a CONTEXT.md entry and link ADR-0002 from the Styling section

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Raised by [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179), which explicitly invited it: "If this work suggests the glossary needs an 'activation identity' entry, say so and it will be triaged as domain modelling — that is a maintainer decision, not a side effect of a rename." Both `/review` axes independently flagged the same gap.

## The gap

"Activation identity" carries real weight in `packages/react/src/use-activation.ts` — `activationIdentityKey()`, `currentActivationIdentity`, and (after [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179)) `committedIdentity` all name it. `CONTEXT.md` defines none of it.

That leaves **Committed source** defined in terms of something undefined:

> The source whose media element may mount, because its identity matches the one activation committed to.

"its identity" is the activation identity — the tuple of source key, loading strategy and activation configuration that `activationIdentityKey` joins. A reader has to reconstruct it from the hook to understand the entry that depends on it.

## Suggested shape

An **Activation identity** entry in the Activation section, defined as the source, loading strategy and configuration that together decide whether an activation still applies — a change to any of them retiring the current commitment. **Committed source** then reads against a defined term rather than a bare noun.

Exact wording and whether the term is the right one are maintainer decisions; `/domain-modeling` is the tool.

## Also worth folding in

`packages/react/test/activation.test.tsx:362,366` still name the committed-source concept "eligibility" / `renderedSecondSourceAsEligible`, which `CONTEXT.md`'s **Committed source** entry lists under `_Avoid_: eligible media`. Left over from [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167); both [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) and [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) out-scoped pre-existing test prose they did not author, so it has never had an owner.

## Out of scope

* Renaming anything in `use-activation.ts`. [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) settled those names.
* Any behaviour change.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement

**Summary:** `CONTEXT.md` defines **Committed source** against "its identity", a term the glossary never defines, and the activation tests name that same concept with the word its `_Avoid_` line forbids.

**Current behavior:**

`CONTEXT.md`'s Loading section defines **Committed source** as "The source whose media element may mount, because its identity matches the one activation committed to." The phrase "its identity" is load-bearing and undefined — a reader has to reconstruct it from the activation hook to understand the entry that depends on it.

The concept is real and already named in code: an identity key is computed from the source key, the loading strategy and the activation configuration, and the hook holds both the current identity and the one activation committed to. Changing any of the three retires the commitment. Nothing in the glossary says so.

Separately, the activation test suite names the committed-source concept using "eligible" — a test whose title speaks of "interaction eligibility", and a variable recording whether a second source rendered "as eligible". **Committed source**'s own `_Avoid_` line lists "eligible media". What that test asserts is that a new source's media element does *not* mount on the render that introduces it, which is Committed source exactly — **not** the `activation: 'eligible'` phase, which is a different and legitimate concept. [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) and [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) both out-scoped pre-existing test prose they did not author, so this has never had an owner.

**Desired behavior:**

An **Activation identity** entry, **Committed source** resting on it, and the two test identifiers using the committed vocabulary. Settled with the maintainer during triage and not open:

* **The entry is called Activation identity**, adopting the term the code already uses rather than coining beside it.
* **It is the tuple, not the string.** The source, loading strategy and configuration that together decide whether an activation still applies — change any of them and the current commitment is retired. The serialised key is an encoding detail; the joining character is an implementation choice and does not belong in domain language.
* **Placed immediately before Committed source**, so the term is defined where it is used.
* **Committed source changes by one word**: "its identity" becomes "its activation identity". Its `_Avoid_: eligible media` line and the rest of its wording stay exactly as they are.
* **The test identifiers take committed vocabulary**: the variable parallels the hook's own `sourceCommitted` boolean, and the title describes commitment rather than eligibility. What the test exercises is untouched.

**Key interfaces:**

* `CONTEXT.md` — the Loading section, which currently runs Activation, Lifecycle, Committed source.
* `CONTEXT.md`'s **Committed source** entry — one word changes; nothing else.
* The activation hook's identity-key function and the state holding the committed identity — read them to get the tuple's members right. **Do not change them**; [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) settled those names and they landed in #137.
* The activation test asserting that a new source is not committed on the render that introduces it.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `CONTEXT.md`'s Loading section contains an **Activation identity** entry, positioned immediately before **Committed source**.
- [ ] The entry defines the identity as the combination of source, loading strategy and configuration, and says that changing any of them retires the current commitment.
- [ ] It defines the concept rather than the serialised key — no joining character, no key format.
- [ ] **Committed source** reads "its activation identity" and is otherwise byte-identical, `_Avoid_: eligible media` included.
- [ ] Every other entry in `CONTEXT.md` is byte-identical.
- [ ] No identifier or test title in the activation test suite uses "eligible" for the committed-source concept, and the renamed variable parallels the hook's `sourceCommitted`.
- [ ] **The renamed test passes with no change to its logic.** Its assertion, its setup and what it exercises are unchanged — if you find yourself editing an assertion, something has gone wrong.
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint` and `pnpm format:check` all pass.

**Out of scope:**

* `activation: 'eligible'` — the activation phase, meaning loading has been permitted. A legitimate, glossary-backed concept for a different thing. Do not sweep it, in the hook or anywhere else.
* Renaming anything in the activation hook. [SIDEPRO-179](#sidepro-179) settled those names; they landed in #137.
* Adding an **Output** entry to the Styling section — that is [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182). Both touch `CONTEXT.md`; expect to rebase if that lands first.
* Other pre-existing "eligible" prose in the test suite that is genuinely about the activation phase. Only the committed-source usages are in scope.
* Any behaviour change whatsoever.
* A changeset — nothing reachable from a package's published entry changes.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Working on branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-183-define-activation-identity-in-contextmd`, cut from `main` after [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) landed (#139), so no rebase is needed.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/140 (squash-merged, all seven required checks green).

`CONTEXT.md`'s Loading section gains an **Activation identity** entry immediately before **Committed source**, defined as the tuple rather than the serialised key. **Committed source** now reads "its activation identity" and is otherwise byte-identical, `_Avoid_: eligible media` included. The two activation-test identifiers that named the committed-source concept "eligible" are renamed — `secondSourceCommitted` parallels the hook's `sourceCommitted` — with the test's setup, probe expression and assertion unchanged. `activation: 'eligible'`, the phase, is untouched everywhere.

`/review`'s Spec axis returned zero findings. Its Standards axis found the entry's first sentence factually wrong, and it was corrected before landing: the identity tuple decides whether a **commitment** still applies, not whether an **activation** does. A `nativeOptions` change retires a started load without touching `committedIdentity` (`use-activation.ts:212-217`), and `isCurrentLoad` gates on more besides — so the looser claim contradicted the entry's own second sentence. It now reads "The source, loading strategy and configuration an activation commits to."

One Standards finding was deliberately not actioned: that "configuration" reads as general player configuration, where in code `ActivationConfiguration` is the `'valid' | 'invalid-interaction-autoplay'` verdict derived from loading × autoplay. The Agent Brief settled the three member names, and the Spec axis reached the same wording independently and said it would not change it. Recording it here rather than overriding a settled decision — worth a follow-up if you think the glossary should say which configuration it means.
