## SIDEPRO-185 — "Output" names two different things once CONTEXT.md defines it

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-30
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-185/output-names-two-different-things-once-contextmd-defines-it

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-177](#sidepro-177) — Record how the library publishes CSS custom properties outward (ADR-0002)
  - [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) — Give published outputs a CONTEXT.md entry and link ADR-0002 from the Styling section
  - [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) — Rename mediaEligible to match the glossary's committed source

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Surfaced by the Standards axis of `/review` during the Factory session that landed [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) (the **Output** glossary entry). Filed rather than fixed because [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182)'s brief put every other entry, the intro paragraph and all code out of scope.

## The collision

[SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) defined **Output** in `CONTEXT.md` as something the library states about itself *for a consumer to read* — a part name, a `data-state`, a published measurement.

`packages/react/src/index.tsx:849-852` already uses the word for something else, and for something with the opposite property:

> `// The only exception is a property derived from player state — that is the primitive's output, not layout, and stays after `...style`.`

That output is a style property the primitive computes from player state (`Poster`'s `visibility`), spread *after* `...style` precisely so a consumer **cannot** override it. It is not consumer-readable and not in the same family as `data-state`. Same word, opposite direction.

It appears in the same sense in `packages/react/test/style-precedence.test.tsx:7` and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-overlay-style-precedence-89-design.md:37`.

`docs/agents/domain.md` tells agents to use terms as `CONTEXT.md` defines them. After [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) an agent reading that comment finds a definition that does not fit it. This repo has treated the identical failure as worth a commit before — [SIDEPRO-167](#sidepro-167) renamed `mediaEligible` because "eligible" named two concepts.

## Also worth folding in

`CONTEXT.md:5-6` enumerates the language's axes — "what they can target (parts), what they may restyle (structural geometry, appearance, tokens)". Output is now a fourth Styling term and a category defined by contrast with Token, and the sentence does not account for it. [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) out-scoped the intro, so it has never had an owner.

## Open questions for the maintainer

* Which use gives way — rename the code-comment sense, or narrow the glossary entry? The glossary entry is the newer of the two but is backed by ADR-0002 and by shipped prose in `Contract.mdx` and `theme.css`; the code-comment sense is older but lives only in comments and a spec doc, never in an identifier.
* Whether the resolution wants an `_Avoid_` line on the **Output** entry. [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) deliberately shipped without one, and that decision was settled with the maintainer — reopening it is this issue's to propose, not to assume.

## Out of scope

* Re-litigating ADR-0002 or the shape of the **Output** entry as [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) landed it.
* Any behaviour change. `#89`'s style-precedence rule is settled; only what it is *called* is in question.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-07-30*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement (`Improvement`)
**Summary:** "Output" names two concepts. The #89 style-precedence sense is renamed to **state-derived property** and given the glossary entry it never had, so "output" means only what `CONTEXT.md` defines.

**Current behavior:**

Two unrelated concepts share the word "output".

*Sense A — the glossary's.* Defined in `CONTEXT.md`'s Styling section and argued in ADR-0002 ("A measurement the library publishes is an output, not a token"): something the library states about itself for a consumer's CSS or tests to read. Its instances are part names, `data-state` values, and `--reely-media-aspect-ratio`.

*Sense B — the #89 style-precedence rule.* A style property a primitive computes from player state, spread *after* `...style` so a consumer cannot override it. Its one instance is `Poster`'s `visibility`, computed from poster state.

The two are most visibly at odds in `@reely/react`'s entry module, where the comment introducing the #89 precedence rule calls a state-derived property "the primitive's output", and a comment six lines below calls `--reely-media-aspect-ratio` "the library's one output property" — a claim that holds only under sense A.

Sense B borrowed the word because it has no name of its own. Its mirror image does: **Structural geometry** is in the glossary, defined as the positioning a primitive needs, "set inline by the primitive, ahead of the consumer's `style` prop". That entry describes the *before* half of the #89 rule. The *after* half has no entry, which is why it reached for a word that was already taken. `docs/agents/domain.md` calls a concept missing from the glossary "a real gap".

**Desired behavior:**

1. **"Output" means sense A only, everywhere.** Sense B is a **state-derived property** — the phrase the Contract docs page's own precedence bullet already leads with ("State-derived properties are the primitive's output…"), so no new vocabulary is invented, only promoted.

2. **`CONTEXT.md`'s Styling section gains a `State-derived property` entry**, sibling to `Structural geometry` and stating the other half of the #89 rule: a property a primitive computes from player state, spread after the consumer's `style` prop because overriding it would pin a state machine rather than adjust layout. Give it an `_Avoid_` line naming `output` — that is the drift this issue exists to stop.

3. **`CONTEXT.md`'s opening paragraph accounts for `Output`.** It enumerates the axes the project's language separates — what a consumer composes, what they can target, what they may restyle, how a player gets from mounted to playing. `Output` is absent, and it does not belong under "what they may restyle": it is what the library publishes for the consumer to read, and it spans part names and `data-state` as well as a custom property. One clause, in the sentence's existing voice.

**Key interfaces:**

Prose, comments and one glossary entry. **No exported type, function signature, CSS property name, or runtime behavior changes.** No identifier in the codebase carries either sense, so nothing renames in code.

Sites to evaluate, found by searching for the word rather than by path:

- The comment in `@reely/react`'s entry module that introduces the #89 spread-order rule ("that is the primitive's output, not layout").
- The header comment of the #89 style-precedence test suite, which states the contract in two lines ("Properties derived from player state are the primitive's OUTPUT").
- The Contract docs page's precedence bullet ("State-derived properties are the primitive's output. Your `style` does not win.") — this one is shipped consumer-facing prose, not a comment.
- The Contract docs page's closing note that `data-reely-part` and `data-state` "are spread after your props, so they are the primitive's output — yours to read from CSS and tests, not to set." **This sentence stays.** Those two *are* outputs in sense A; the sentence is correct as written and is the clearest statement of why the two senses got confused. Do not rewrite it into the new term.
- The main React test suite's passing reference to "pinning a state machine's output". Judge it: if it reads as ordinary English rather than a claim about the domain term, leave it.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Searching the repo for `output` finds no use in `packages/` or `apps/` that means a state-derived style property.
- [ ] Every remaining use in `packages/`, `apps/` and `examples/` matches the `Output` glossary entry — a part name, a `data-state`, or a published measurement.
- [ ] `CONTEXT.md`'s Styling section carries a `State-derived property` entry with an `_Avoid_: output` line.
- [ ] `CONTEXT.md`'s opening paragraph names outputs among the axes it enumerates.
- [ ] The Contract docs page's precedence bullet states the rule without calling the property an output, and the rule it states is unchanged.
- [ ] The Contract docs page's `data-reely-part` / `data-state` sentence is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- [ ] The `Output` glossary entry and ADR-0002 are unchanged.
- [ ] The #89 style-precedence suite passes with no assertion edited — the contract is renamed, not altered.
- [ ] Lint, typecheck and the full test suite pass.

**Out of scope:**

- **Any behavior change.** #89's spread-order rule and ADR-0002's publication rule are both settled. Only what one of them is *called* is in question.
- **The `Output` entry's wording, and an `_Avoid_` line on it.** [SIDEPRO-182](#sidepro-182) shipped it without one deliberately, and this resolution removes the reason to revisit: once sense B has its own name, there is no synonym for `Output` to warn against. The `_Avoid_` line belongs on the new entry, pointing the other way.
- **Re-litigating ADR-0002**, or ADR-0001's inbound/outbound boundary.
- **The dated design record under `docs/superpowers/specs/` for #89.** It records what was decided on the day it was written, under the vocabulary of that day. Leave it; it is history, not a contract.
- **Renaming any identifier.** Neither sense appears in one, and inventing a rename would make this a code change.

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-185-output-names-two-different-things-once-contextmd-defines-it`, from `main` at `236962e`.

Working the agent brief above, with both maintainer decisions settled at triage: rename the #89 sense to **state-derived property** (sense A keeps the word), and fold in the `CONTEXT.md` opening paragraph.

Beyond the brief, three things the work turned up:

* The Styling section's ADR-0001 pointer named its entries by position ("why the first two are separate"). Inserting an entry between them re-aimed it at the wrong pair, so it names structural geometry and appearance outright.

<a id="sidepro-186"></a>

## SIDEPRO-186 — Split the react package's 2,780-line entry module into primitive-family modules

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-30
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-186/split-the-react-packages-2780-line-entry-module-into-primitive-family

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-135](#sidepro-135) — Provider adapters: the create*Provider closures are 574-783 lines each

**Description**

## Problem Statement

The react package's entire primitives surface lives in one entry module of roughly 2,780 lines: about 25 primitives, three contexts, the public hooks, and the shared types. Finding a primitive means scrolling past every other one, every change lands in the same file, and reviews carry noise from unrelated neighbours. The package already demonstrates the target shape — icons, the activation hook, and the provider loaders each live in their own module — but the entry module never adopted it.

## Solution

Split the entry module into cohesive modules grouped by primitive family, and reduce the entry module to shared foundations plus re-exports. The public API stays identical: same package entry, same exported names and types, no behavior change. Consumers, tests, and Storybook notice nothing.

## Commits

Each commit extracts one cohesive family. Code moves without modification beyond imports/exports, and each commit leaves the repo green: lint, typecheck, and the full unit suite pass before the next commit starts.

 1. Extract the shared player context, the poster context, and the public hooks (the state selector hook, the active-cues hook, the actions hook) into a shared module; the entry re-exports them. These are extracted first because everything else depends on them, which avoids import cycles later.
 2. Extract poster normalization and the poster primitives (Poster, PosterImage).
 3. Extract Viewport and Media, including the media aspect-ratio output property.
 4. Extract Root and its activation wiring.
 5. Extract loading and error presentation: ActivationButton, LoadingIndicator, ErrorDisplay, together with the buffering-delay constants they share.
 6. Extract captions presentation: Captions, CaptionsButton, CaptionsMenu.
 7. Extract transport controls: PlayButton, MuteButton, VolumeSlider, SeekSlider, Time.
 8. Extract display-session controls: FullscreenButton, PipButton, AirPlayButton.
 9. Extract the Controls container.
10. Extract the settings menu family: SettingsMenu, its trigger and content, menu items, the radio group and items, and their contexts.
11. Extract Gestures.
12. Final sweep: confirm the entry module is now only shared types plus re-exports, then run the full gate — lint, typecheck, unit tests, bundle budgets, packaging verification, Storybook suite, e2e visual suite. Record the package's gzip size before and after in the PR description.

## Decision Document

* The package keeps its single public entry; the new modules are internal and are not added to the package's exports map.
* Every exported name, every exported type, and the composed player namespace stay identical. This is a move-only refactor.
* Grouping is by primitive family — what a consumer composes together — not by line-count targets.
* No renames: primitives, part names, data attributes, and props are untouched.
* The split follows the sibling-module pattern already present in the package (icons, activation, provider loaders).

## Testing Decisions

* No new tests. The existing suites exercise the package through its public entry, not through internals, which makes them the harness for this refactor: they must be green before the work starts and green, unchanged, after every commit.
* Per-commit gate: lint, typecheck, unit tests. Final gate adds the bundle budget check (the react package's 18 KB gzip budget must still pass), packaging verification, the Storybook suite, and the e2e visual suite.

## Out of Scope

* Any behavior, prop, or naming change.
* Splitting or reorganizing the test files.
* The core package and the provider packages — tracked in their own issues.
* New abstractions shared between the extracted modules; if two families want to share something new, that is a separate discussion.

## Further Notes

The bundle-budget requirement is not incidental: keeping the published bundle slim is an explicit goal of this maintainability pass. If the split moves the gzip size at all (it should not — same code, same single built entry), the final commit must explain why.

<a id="sidepro-187"></a>

## SIDEPRO-187 — Split the core package's entry module into vocabulary, detection, and store modules

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-30
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-187/split-the-core-packages-entry-module-into-vocabulary-detection-and

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-135](#sidepro-135) — Provider adapters: the create*Provider closures are 574-783 lines each

**Description**

## Problem Statement

The core package is a single entry module of roughly 1,670 lines holding four distinguishable responsibilities: the project's public type vocabulary (state, events, sources, capabilities, the provider adapter contract), source detection, a set of internal safety helpers (defensive freezing, safe notification and teardown, error construction), and the player store itself. Every package in the workspace imports from core, so this one file is where all cross-cutting reading and reviewing happens, and unrelated concerns share one scroll.

## Solution

Split the entry module by responsibility, with the entry reduced to the store plus re-exports — or a pure barrel if the store also moves. Public API stays identical: same single entry, same exported names and types, no behavior change.

## Commits

Each commit is move-only (imports/exports aside) and leaves lint, typecheck, and the full unit suite green.

1. Extract the public type vocabulary — playback and player state, events, sources, text tracks and cues, capabilities, availability, and the provider adapter contract — into a types module; the entry re-exports it. Types go first because everything else depends on them.
2. Extract source detection: the guards, the detection success/failure construction, and the string-versus-descriptor resolution.
3. Extract text-track utilities (labeling and related pure helpers).
4. Extract the internal safety helpers: defensive freezing of state fragments, safe notify/unsubscribe/destroy, and the error constructors. These are internal — not re-exported.
5. What remains is the player store and its initial-state construction. Either it stays as the entry module's body or moves to a store module with the entry as a barrel — whichever leaves the entry clearer.
6. Final sweep: run the full gate — lint, typecheck, unit tests, bundle budgets (the core package's 10 KB gzip budget must still pass), packaging verification. Record gzip size before and after in the PR description.

## Decision Document

* The package keeps its single public entry; new modules are internal to the package and not added to the exports map.
* Every exported name and type stays identical. Move-only.
* The split is by responsibility (vocabulary / detection / helpers / store), not by size targets.
* Internal helpers stay internal: nothing that was previously unexported becomes public.

## Testing Decisions

* No new tests. The existing suites (including the dedicated source-detection and autoplay suites) exercise the package through its public entry and act as the harness: green before, green and unchanged after every commit.
* Per-commit gate: lint, typecheck, unit tests. Final gate adds bundle budgets and packaging verification.

## Out of Scope

* Any behavior or naming change to the store, events, or detection.
* The react and provider packages — tracked in their own issues.
* Re-designing the adapter contract or event vocabulary.

## Further Notes

Keeping the published bundle slim is an explicit goal of this pass: core's 10 KB gzip budget is the hard line, and the split should not move the built output at all.

<a id="sidepro-188"></a>

## SIDEPRO-188 — Decompose the native provider's create closure by lifecycle seam

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-30
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-188/decompose-the-native-providers-create-closure-by-lifecycle-seam

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-189](#sidepro-189) — Decompose the HLS provider's create closure by lifecycle seam

**Description**

## Problem Statement

The native provider keeps effectively its whole implementation inside one create closure of roughly 630 lines. Everything shares one lexical scope, so any state is reachable from any handler and the units inside are not separately testable. This was measured in the parent issue and deliberately not acted on; the maintainer reversed that decision on 2026-07-30 (see the parent issue's reopen comment).

## Solution

Extract the closure's contents by lifecycle seam — load/attach, playback commands, tracks and captions, presentation modes (fullscreen, picture-in-picture, AirPlay), teardown — into functions that take their dependencies explicitly. No shared cross-provider abstraction: this issue covers the native provider only, in its own PR, per the parent issue's prerequisites.

## Commits

1. Baseline: run the real-providers suite for this provider locally (the environment flag documented in the repo). If it cannot run or is not green, stop and report on this issue instead of proceeding — a green baseline is a hard prerequisite.
2. Rewrite the repo's recorded out-of-scope decision on provider decomposition so it records the 2026-07-30 reversal and points at the parent issue, keeping repo and tracker in agreement.
3. Extract the module-level pure helpers that already sit outside the closure (error mapping, availability values, range conversion, command-result construction) into their own module — move-only, entry re-exports nothing new.
4. Extract the tracks/captions seam from the closure.
5. Extract the playback-command seam (the command runner and per-command handling).
6. Extract the presentation-mode seam (fullscreen, picture-in-picture, AirPlay, including the WebKit-specific paths).
7. Extract load/attach and teardown.
8. Re-run the real-providers suite; compare with the baseline. Run the full gate: lint, typecheck, unit tests, bundle budgets, packaging verification.

Each commit leaves lint, typecheck, and the unit suite green.

## Decision Document

* Extraction is by lifecycle seam, never by line count — a prerequisite recorded on the parent issue that still binds.
* Extracted units receive explicit dependencies; shared mutable closure state shrinks with each seam extracted.
* No shared base or lifecycle abstraction across providers; anything shared would be speculative (that part of the original decision stands).
* The package's public API — the create factory and its exported types — is unchanged.

## Testing Decisions

* The real-providers suite green before and after is the hard gate for this refactor; the unit suites test the adapter at its boundary through the package entry and must stay green, unchanged, at every commit.
* New unit tests for an extracted seam are welcome where a behavior was previously unreachable in isolation, but they must test the seam's contract, not the extraction's mechanics.

## Out of Scope

* Behavior or API changes.
* The other three providers — each has its own child issue under the parent.
* Any cross-provider shared abstraction.

## SIDEPRO-189 — Decompose the HLS provider's create closure by lifecycle seam

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-07-30
- **Completed:** 2026-07-30
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-189/decompose-the-hls-providers-create-closure-by-lifecycle-seam

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-190](#sidepro-190) — Decompose the YouTube provider's create closure by lifecycle seam
  - [SIDEPRO-196](#sidepro-196) — Decide how provider-specific seams fit the glossary's seam enumeration
  - [SIDEPRO-188](#sidepro-188) — Decompose the native provider's create closure by lifecycle seam

**Description**

## Problem Statement

The HLS provider keeps effectively its whole implementation inside one create closure of roughly 780 lines — the largest of the four adapters. Everything shares one lexical scope, so any state is reachable from any handler and the units inside are not separately testable. This was measured in the parent issue and deliberately not acted on; the maintainer reversed that decision on 2026-07-30 (see the parent issue's reopen comment).

## Solution

Extract the closure's contents by lifecycle seam into functions that take their dependencies explicitly. The package already keeps engine selection, environment detection, and live-state derivation at module level; the closure seams to extract are the engine attach paths (native HLS versus the hls.js engine), the fatal-error recovery policy, subtitle and track handling, quality levels, playback commands, and teardown. This issue covers the HLS provider only, in its own PR, per the parent issue's prerequisites.

## Commits

1. Baseline: run the real-providers suite for this provider locally (the environment flag documented in the repo). If it cannot run or is not green, stop and report on this issue instead of proceeding.
2. Extract the module-level pure helpers that already sit outside the closure (range reading, live-state comparison, error construction) into their own module — move-only.
3. Extract the fatal-error recovery policy (the bounded network/media recovery logic) from the closure.
4. Extract the subtitle and track seam.
5. Extract the quality-levels seam.
6. Extract the playback-command seam.
7. Extract the engine load/attach seam (both engine paths) and teardown.
8. Re-run the real-providers suite; compare with the baseline. Run the full gate: lint, typecheck, unit tests, bundle budgets, packaging verification.

Each commit leaves lint, typecheck, and the unit suite green.

## Decision Document

* Extraction is by lifecycle seam, never by line count — a prerequisite recorded on the parent issue that still binds.
* Extracted units receive explicit dependencies; shared mutable closure state shrinks with each seam extracted.
* No shared base or lifecycle abstraction across providers; anything shared would be speculative (that part of the original decision stands).
* The package's public API — the create factory, the loader, and the exported types — is unchanged.

## Testing Decisions

* The real-providers suite green before and after is the hard gate; the unit suites (including the dedicated captions suite) test the adapter at its boundary through the package entry and must stay green, unchanged, at every commit.
* New unit tests for an extracted seam are welcome where a behavior was previously unreachable in isolation — the recovery policy is the obvious candidate.

## Out of Scope

* Behavior or API changes.
* The other three providers — each has its own child issue under the parent.
* Any cross-provider shared abstraction.

## Further Notes

Recommended to pick up after the native provider's decomposition lands, which proves the extraction pattern on the simplest adapter.

The egress IP resolves to a datacenter AS, which is the same IP-reputation mechanism `e2e/buffered-real.spec.ts:12-18` already documents for CI runners. The parent's prerequisite is worded per provider, so the Vimeo gate is satisfied; flagging it because the YouTube half of that suite is unrunnable here until the egress changes.

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** Decide whether the glossary's seam enumeration is exhaustive or exemplary, and amend CONTEXT.md so provider-specific seams have a defined place.

**Current behavior:**
CONTEXT.md's Adapters section defines **Seam** by enumeration — playback, presentation, tracks and captions, attachment. The provider decompositions introduced seams that fall outside that list: a quality-levels seam and a fatal-error recovery seam in the HLS provider, and further provider-specific ones in the YouTube and Vimeo providers. A reader of the glossary today cannot tell whether those modules are seams that the definition forgot, or something else that should not have been called a seam. The standards review flagged the gap, and `docs/agents/domain.md` says a concept missing from the glossary is a real gap to take to `/domain-modeling` rather than patch ad hoc.

**Desired behavior:**
CONTEXT.md answers the question outright, so a future provider decomposition knows what to call a module it extracts. Two resolutions are on the table and either is acceptable if argued in the doc:

1. Mark the four listed seams as canonical but not exhaustive, and give a naming rule for provider-specific ones.
2. Define each recurring provider-specific seam — quality ladder, error recovery — as a glossary term in its own right.

Whichever is chosen, the definition must be decidable: someone extracting a new module should be able to read it and know whether what they have is a seam, without asking.

**Key interfaces:**

* The **Seam** entry in CONTEXT.md's Adapters section — the definition itself, and whether its list is presented as closed or open
* Any glossary cross-references that assume the four-item list is complete
* `/domain-modeling` is the skill that owns edits to CONTEXT.md; follow its CONTEXT-FORMAT conventions rather than editing the file freehand

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] CONTEXT.md states explicitly whether the seam enumeration is exhaustive or exemplary
- [ ] The quality-levels seam and the fatal-error recovery seam each have a defined place under the chosen resolution — either covered by a naming rule, or defined as terms
- [ ] The definition is decidable: it gives a reader a test for whether a given extracted module is a seam
- [ ] Any glossary text that contradicts the chosen resolution is updated in the same change

**Out of scope:**

* Renaming any existing module. The names the decompositions landed should stand unless the glossary decision positively demands otherwise.
* Re-opening how the providers were decomposed. That work is complete and closed.
* Adding seams to providers that do not have them.

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/150 (squash-merged as `c15998b`).

Resolution 1 of the two the brief offered: the seam list is canonical but not closed, with a decidable test replacing the enumeration. A module is a seam when it exclusively owns one slice of the adapter's state and the commands over it — which admits HLS's quality levels and fatal-error recovery, YouTube's time updates and Vimeo's chromeless availability, and excludes `loader.ts`, whose memo is module-global rather than one adapter's. The naming rule ratifies the names already landed: a seam is named for the slice it owns.

Two earlier drafts were rejected by the review gate and rewritten: one asserted seams "fold their patches in" to the host (false — every seam publishes straight through `emit`), and one imposed a narrow-dependency test that excluded `createHlsAttachment` and `createNativeAttachment`, both canonical seams.

Follow-up noticed but left alone as out of scope: the header comment at `packages/provider-youtube/src/time-updates.ts:44` calls it "The polling seam", naming the mechanism rather than the slice it owns — the one place in the repo the new naming rule contradicts.

## SIDEPRO-197 — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-03
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-197/build-backpackvideo-wrapper-skeleton-first-parity-stories-and-parity

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205) — Convert Wistia stories and wire wistia playerConfig passthrough
  - [SIDEPRO-202](#sidepro-202) — Add aspect ratios, play-icon sizes and visual variants to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-201](#sidepro-201) — Add external play-state control to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-200](#sidepro-200) — Add viewport-driven pause/resume to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210) — The loop prop is silently ignored by the YouTube and Vimeo providers

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Build the BackpackVideo compat wrapper's core, convert the first four Backpack stories onto it, and create the parity matrix that tracks the whole effort.

**Current behavior:**
EF Backpack's video components are built on react-player. Nothing in this repo demonstrates that Reely can stand in for it, so the claim is untested: there is no wrapper exposing Backpack's `VideoPlayer` prop API, no converted story, and no record of which Backpack stories Reely can and cannot reproduce.

**Desired behavior:**
A `BackpackVideo` component lives in the storybook app — not a published package — and reimplements the core of Backpack's `VideoPlayer` prop API on Reely primitives, closely enough that Backpack's own story args can be copied near-verbatim. A new "Backpack parity" storybook section holds the converted stories, and a parity matrix document records the status of every Backpack story across the whole effort.

The wrapper accepts, in this slice: `url` (a source string — Reely's own source detection picks the provider), `playing` (start playing on load), `muted`, `loop`, `controls` (show or hide the provider's controls), `showPlayIcon` (default true), `className`, and `onPlayChange(isPlaying)`. When `showPlayIcon` is on and the video is paused, a centered play-icon overlay appears and disappears once playback starts. With controls hidden, the whole player surface is a play/pause toggle carrying an accessible label that flips between "Play video" and "Pause video" — Backpack's behavior.

Behavior parity is the goal, with close visuals via story-local CSS. Pixel fidelity to Backpack's own styling stack is explicitly not.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideo`'s props, named exactly as Backpack's `VideoPlayer` names them — the point of the wrapper is that a Backpack consumer's props keep working
* Reely's source-detection entry point, which resolves a URL string to a provider; the wrapper passes `url` through rather than selecting a provider itself
* Reely's playback commands and player state, which back `playing`, `muted`, `loop`, `controls` and the `onPlayChange` callback
* The parity matrix document: one row per Backpack story with columns for the Backpack story name, the Reely story that replaces it, a status of `full` / `partial` / `gap`, and notes

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `BackpackVideo` renders both a Vimeo and a YouTube source through Reely, with every prop above behaving as it does in Backpack
- [ ] The play-icon overlay appears when paused and disappears when playing
- [ ] With controls hidden, clicking anywhere on the player toggles playback, and the accessible label reflects the current state
- [ ] `onPlayChange` fires both on a user toggle and on a programmatic state change
- [ ] Four stories run in the workbench under a "Backpack parity" section, using Backpack's own args: `Default` (Vimeo `https://vimeo.com/336066147`, muted), `YouTubeVideo` (`https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mhN3E_hlWmU`), `WithControls`, and `Loop`
- [ ] A parity matrix document exists with one row for every story in Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx`, `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx` and `VideoHoverPreview.stories.tsx` — rows not yet converted start as `gap`, naming the issue that will close them
- [ ] Storybook story tests, including axe, pass per this repo's conventions

**Out of scope:**

* Light mode, cover images and the hover effect — a separate issue
* Viewport-driven pause and resume — a separate issue
* External play-state control — a separate issue
* Aspect ratios, play-icon sizes and visual variants — a separate issue
* Wistia support of any kind, which needs a provider that does not exist yet
* Publishing the wrapper as a package. It exists to prove the swap is possible, not to ship.
* Reproducing Backpack's styling stack. Story-local CSS is the intended approach.

**Source material:** Backpack's video stories at `~/Documents/projects/backpack/stories/components/Video/` and its components at `~/Documents/projects/backpack/src/components/Video/`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (1)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-03*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/151 (squash-merged as `3b46acd` into `backpack-parity`, the milestone's integration branch — the maintainer confirmed milestone work integrates there rather than per-issue into `main`).

`BackpackVideo` lives in the storybook app and reimplements `url`, `playing`, `muted`, `loop`, `controls`, `showPlayIcon`, `className` and `onPlayChange` on Reely primitives. Nine deterministic stories under `Backpack parity/Video` plus `Real playback/BackpackVideo`, and `docs/backpack-parity.md` tracking all 38 Backpack stories — 2 full, 2 partial, 34 gap.

**Three review rounds, and the first two found the deliverable hollow.** The wrapper originally set `loading="interaction"` with no activation affordance, so no provider ever attached: `play()` returned `not-ready` and never touched activation. Every story "worked" only because the mock writes provider state straight onto the controller. It now renders `Player.ActivationButton` and switches to eager loading with autoplay when `playing` is set, and a real `youtube-nocookie.com` embed attaches and hands over to the wrapper's toggle. Fixing that exposed a second defect — gating the toggle on `activation === 'ready'` left an autoplaying player with zero click targets, so nothing could pause it. The gate is now the exact complement of `ActivationButton`'s own render condition.

Two acceptance criteria are met only partially, recorded in the matrix rather than papered over:

- `loop` never reaches the Vimeo or YouTube providers — `loadProvider` forwards `nativeOptions` to the HLS and native branches only. Filed as [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210).
- `controls` renders Reely's own control bar; Backpack forwards the prop to react-player and gets the provider's native chrome.

Two repo-level problems surfaced and were fixed here: `apps/storybook/README.md`'s determinism rule stated a bright line the repo could not keep, and `real-playback.contract.test.ts` scanned only the top-level story directory, so any story in a subdirectory escaped the `real-playback` → `!test` tag check.

**Follow-up, deliberately not fixed:** mock-backed stories still get an outer `Player.Root` that the wrapper's own root shadows, with parameters staged into both controllers. Fixing it means splitting the `real-playback` tag into two concerns — infrastructure work beyond this brief.

**Environment note:** Vimeo's provider never resolves `player.ready()` under headless Chromium (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:269`), so the Vimeo half of real attachment is unverified there. YouTube completes. Worth a look in a real browser.

## SIDEPRO-198 — Build @reely/provider-wistia

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-03
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-198/build-reelyprovider-wistia

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205) — Convert Wistia stories and wire wistia playerConfig passthrough

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Add a Wistia provider package covering the playback core, so Wistia-hosted media plays through Reely.

**Current behavior:**
Reely has providers for native media, HLS, YouTube and Vimeo. A Wistia URL is not detected, so it resolves to no provider and cannot play. Several of Backpack's video stories use Wistia-hosted media, which makes Wistia the one gap that blocks a complete parity demo.

**Desired behavior:**
A new provider package plays Wistia-hosted video through the same contract every other provider satisfies. A Wistia URL passed as a player source is detected, lazy-loads the provider, and plays. A consumer who never plays Wistia ships none of its code.

Scope is the playback core the Backpack stories need, not full coverage of Wistia's API: load and attach through Wistia's player embed API; play, pause, mute, loop and controls visibility; and current-time and duration state.

Capability reporting must be honest, per this repo's standing policy on providers: anything Wistia cannot honour answers `unavailable` with a reason, and each answer is measured against the real SDK rather than assumed.

**Key interfaces:**

* The provider contract in the core package — the same one the existing providers implement. Model the package's shape on the Vimeo provider, whose decomposition by lifecycle seam is the current pattern: loader, attachment, playback, presentation, adapter values, capability reporting.
* Source detection in the core package, extended to recognise Wistia URLs: the `*.wistia.com/medias/<id>` form and `wistia.net` embed URLs
* The react package's lazy provider-loader registry, which is what keeps an unused provider out of a consumer's bundle
* The provider capability record, whose `unavailable` answers carry reasons
* Existing provider ADRs, which constrain how a new provider is allowed to differ from the others

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A Wistia URL given as a player source is detected, lazy-loads the provider, and plays
- [ ] Play, pause, mute, loop and controls-visibility commands are honoured; playing state, current time and duration are reported
- [ ] Capabilities are reported honestly, with a reason on every `unavailable`, verified against the real SDK rather than assumed
- [ ] The package README documents the capability matrix, matching the other provider packages
- [ ] Bundle-budget and packaging tests pass
- [ ] Unit tests pass, and an `@real` end-to-end test against a live Wistia media runs green locally — `https://wesleyluyten.wistia.com/medias/oifkgmxnkb` is a usable fixture

**Out of scope:**

* Wistia features beyond the playback core — chapters, captions, analytics, the Wistia-specific embed chrome
* Wiring Wistia options through the BackpackVideo wrapper's `playerConfig`, which is a separate issue
* Converting any Backpack story. This issue delivers the provider; the stories come later.
* Changing how any existing provider works

**Note:** independent of the wrapper work, so it can run in parallel with it. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-03*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-198-build-reelyprovider-wistia`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `3b46acd`. Work lands on `backpack-parity`, per the milestone's integration-branch convention.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-03*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: **[PR #152](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/152)**, squash-merged to `backpack-parity` as `83e012a`.

## What shipped

- **`@reely/core`** — `WistiaSource`, `'wistia'` on `PlayerProvider`, and detection for the account media page, the account embed, and both embed-iframe hosts.
- **`@reely/provider-wistia`** — the adapter on Wistia's Aurora player (`@wistia/wistia-player@0.7.12`), decomposed by lifecycle seam to match `provider-vimeo`.
- **`@reely/react`** — lazy loader branch, div mount, and the rollup external that keeps the SDK out of react's bundle.
- **Proof** — a real-playback Storybook story and `e2e/wistia-smoke.spec.ts`.

## Acceptance criteria

| Criterion | Status |
| --- | --- |
| Wistia URL detected, lazy-loads, plays | met — verified in a live browser |
| Play/pause/mute/loop/controls honoured; playing state, time, duration reported | met |
| Capabilities honest, reason on every `unavailable`, measured not assumed | met |
| README documents the capability matrix | met |
| Bundle-budget and packaging tests pass | met |
| Unit tests pass; `@real` e2e green locally against the fixture | met — 997/997 unit, `@real` 3/3 |

## Two things worth knowing

**The npm package is only the web-component shell — it fetches the playback engine from `fast.wistia.com` at runtime.** Unlike `provider-vimeo`, this provider does not run without reaching Wistia's CDN. Its own dist is 3.68 KB gzip; the SDK a consumer resolves at load time is ~177 KB gzip. Disclosed in the package README.

**The live run found a defect the unit suite structurally could not.** The adapter's event-name literals and the test fixture's event list came from the same documentation, so a shared misreading would pass every unit test while publishing nothing. The `@real` spec records what the live element actually dispatches, independent of both — and found that `seeking` cannot bracket a seek on this player: `seeked` fires roughly a millisecond *before* its paired `seeking`, so round-trip wiring pinned Reely's `seeking` true for the rest of the session. `seeking` is no longer bound; `seeked` alone reports the settled playhead, and the README states the consequence.

## Follow-ups, not filed yet

1. `provider-vimeo`'s `adopt` has the same pre-override volume/rate defect fixed here for Wistia (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/playback.ts:183-209`) — confirmed, left untouched because changing an existing provider was out of scope.
2. The command error-classification table is now copied across providers, with a third divergent variant in `provider-native`. It belongs in `@reely/core`.
3. `scripts/check-bundle-budgets.mjs` measures only `dist/index.js`, never sibling chunks. Summing `dist/*.js` would have caught the 800 KB payload that briefly landed inside `@reely/react`'s dist during this work.
4. Wistia has no CI-routed (non-`@real`) e2e spec, unlike YouTube and Vimeo — so its only e2e coverage never runs in CI.

## SIDEPRO-199 — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-199/add-light-mode-cover-images-and-hover-effect-to-backpackvideo

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix
- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-204](#sidepro-204) — Convert VideoHoverPreview stories to a Reely-based wrapper
  - [SIDEPRO-203](#sidepro-203) — Convert AutoplayVideo stories to a Reely-based wrapper
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-202](#sidepro-202) — Add aspect ratios, play-icon sizes and visual variants to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) — A failed load presents BackpackVideo's cover click as playing video
  - [SIDEPRO-211](#sidepro-211) — Compare BackpackVideo against Backpack's own Storybook

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Add Backpack's pre-play presentation layer to BackpackVideo — light mode, cover images, and the hover effect.

**Current behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` mounts the provider immediately and shows a play-icon overlay while paused. It has no concept of a cover image, so every Backpack story that presents a thumbnail before playback — the majority of them — cannot be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
The wrapper can present a cover image instead of mounting the player, and only mounts the provider when the viewer asks for playback. Clicking the cover starts playback and removes the cover.

New props: `light` (show a thumbnail rather than mounting the player; the provider's own thumbnail is fetched for Vimeo and YouTube the way Backpack's video-data hook does it), `placeholderImageSrc` (a custom cover image URL, which wins over any fetched thumbnail), `alt` (applied to the cover image), `renderCustomImage` (a custom element type used to render the cover, for consumers with their own image component), and `hoverEffect` (a zoom on the cover while hovered and not playing, default true).

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideo`'s props, extended with the five above and named as Backpack names them
* The thumbnail lookup for Vimeo and YouTube — a per-provider fetch that turns a source URL into a poster image URL, mirroring what Backpack's own video-data hook does
* Reely's poster or placeholder surface, and whichever Reely mechanism defers mounting the provider until activation — the wrapper should build on it rather than reimplement deferred mounting
* `renderCustomImage` takes an element type, so a consumer's own image component receives the resolved src and alt

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `light` shows a thumbnail, fetched for both Vimeo and YouTube, without mounting the provider iframe until first play
- [ ] `placeholderImageSrc` overrides the fetched thumbnail
- [ ] `renderCustomImage` renders the cover through the supplied element type, and `alt` reaches the rendered image
- [ ] The cover zooms on hover, and `hoverEffect={false}` disables it
- [ ] Clicking the cover starts playback, and playback actually begins once the cover is removed
- [ ] These stories are converted with Backpack's args: `Light`, `WithoutHoverEffect`, `VimeoCoverImage`, `YouTubeCoverImage`, `CustomCoverImage`, `CustomCoverImageYouTube`, `YouTubeShortsVideoAndCustomCoverImage` (its cover-image behavior; the aspect-ratio prop may land with the aspect-ratios issue), and `WithRenderCustomImage`
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`, with notes on any approximation
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* Aspect ratios and play-icon sizing, even where a converted story also exercises them
* Viewport-driven pause and resume
* Wistia cover images, which need the Wistia provider
* Building a general-purpose image component. `renderCustomImage` exists so a consumer supplies their own.

**Source material:** Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-03*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-199-add-light-mode-cover-images-and-hover-effect-to`, cut from `backpack-parity`. The PR will target `backpack-parity`, per the milestone's integration-branch convention.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

Landed: PR https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/153, squash-merged into `backpack-parity` as `53ab35f`. All ten PR checks passed before the merge (e2e chromium/firefox/webkit, hls-native-webkit, hls-paths, package, static, storybook, unit, visual). Local gate green too: `pnpm test` 1011, `pnpm test:storybook` 107, `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [x] `light` shows a thumbnail, fetched for both Vimeo and YouTube, without mounting the provider iframe until first play — `useVideoThumbnail` calls each provider's own oEmbed endpoint (not the `noembed.com` proxy), with Backpack's Shorts normalisation. The wrapper already passed `loading="interaction"` to `Player.Root`, so deferred mounting is Reely's own mechanism rather than a reimplementation.
- [x] `placeholderImageSrc` overrides the fetched thumbnail — and short-circuits the lookup entirely, so a caller with a cover never pays for a fetch.
- [x] `renderCustomImage` renders the cover through the supplied element type, and `alt` reaches the rendered image — see the divergence note below on what "reaches" means here.
- [x] The cover zooms on hover, and `hoverEffect={false}` disables it.
- [x] Clicking the cover starts playback, and playback actually begins once the cover is removed.
- [x] Stories converted with Backpack's args: `Light`, `WithoutHoverEffect`, `VimeoCoverImage`, `YouTubeCoverImage`, `CustomCoverImage`, `CustomCoverImageYouTube`, `YouTubeShortsVideoAndCustomCoverImage` (cover-image behaviour only — `aspectRatios` stays with [SIDEPRO-202](#sidepro-202)), `WithRenderCustomImage`. Anything that fetches a thumbnail or loads a remote cover lives under `Real playback/BackpackVideo`, tagged `real-playback` and `!test`, because the deterministic suite is offline.
- [x] Parity matrix rows moved off `gap`, with approximation notes.
- [x] Story tests pass.

Nothing from the Out of scope list was touched: no aspect ratios, no play-icon sizing, no viewport pause/resume, no Wistia covers, no general-purpose image component.

## One deliberate divergence from the brief

**`light` defaults to `false`, against Backpack's own `true`.** Under `light: true` the wrapper fetches a thumbnail on mount, and defaulting that on would put a network request behind every already-converted deterministic story, which the workbench's offline guard forbids. The deferral `light` buys in Backpack the wrapper already has. Recorded in the prop's doc comment and in the parity matrix preamble.

## Follow-ups found, not filed

1. `playing: true` combined with `placeholderImageSrc` shows the cover while `loading="eager"` mounts `Player.Media` behind it; Backpack does not mount the player region at all in that combination. No story here uses that pair — `AutoplayVideo`'s `WithCustomPlaceholderImage` does, under [SIDEPRO-203](#sidepro-203).
2. `alt` reaches the DOM but not the accessibility tree: `Player.Poster` is `aria-hidden`, where Backpack labels its cover container with `role="button"` + `aria-label`. The labelled affordance here is the real "Play video" button underneath, which is arguably better, so the markup was left alone and the divergence noted in the matrix rows that carry an `alt`. Whether Reely should offer a non-`aria-hidden` poster surface is a real question.
3. Optimistic `isPlaying` on a cover click can strand the label: if the provider fails to load, the cover is gone and the button reads "Pause video" until a second click. Backpack behaves the same way.
4. `video-thumbnail.ts` carries its own YouTube/Vimeo URL matching beside `@reely/core`'s `detectSource`, which the wrapper already uses to pick a provider, and the two disagree at the edges (`youtube.com/embed/ID` plays but gets no thumbnail). Mirroring Backpack's `useVideoData` was the brief; folding the lookup onto `detectSource` would delete all three regexes.
5. The hover helper skips rather than fails when no browser-mode driver resolves, so the two hover stories would assert nothing on that path. It is provably not taken today (a control that raises the expected scale to an impossible value fails under `pnpm test:storybook`), but nothing pins it that way.

## Worth knowing for future work in this file

`vitest/browser` must never be imported at story-module scope. It is a virtual module that only exists inside Vitest browser mode; its on-disk stub throws at module evaluation, which took the whole `Backpack parity/Video` file down in `pnpm dev` and compiled to `null.elementLocator(...)` in the static build — while `pnpm test:storybook` stayed green throughout. It is now a guarded dynamic import inside `play`. The test suite is structurally blind to this failure, so a change here needs the dev server and the static build checked by hand.

<a id="sidepro-200"></a>

## SIDEPRO-200 — Add viewport-driven pause/resume to BackpackVideo

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-200/add-viewport-driven-pauseresume-to-backpackvideo

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix
- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-203](#sidepro-203) — Convert AutoplayVideo stories to a Reely-based wrapper
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-211](#sidepro-211) — Compare BackpackVideo against Backpack's own Storybook

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Add Backpack's viewport-driven pause and resume to BackpackVideo, observed against the viewport or a custom scroll container.

**Current behavior:**
Once `BackpackVideo` starts playing it keeps playing regardless of whether it is on screen. A viewer who scrolls past a playing video leaves audio and network activity running behind them. Backpack pauses in that situation, so its stories covering the behavior cannot be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
A playing video pauses when it leaves the observed area and resumes when it returns. The behavior is on by default and can be turned off, and the observed area can be a scroll container rather than the viewport — which is what makes it work for a player inside a modal or a custom scrolling region.

New props: `pauseOnOutOfViewport` (default true), `threshold` (the intersection threshold, default 0), and `intersectionObserverRoot` (the scroll-container element to observe against, default null meaning the viewport).

Resume applies only to a video that was playing when it left. A video the viewer paused deliberately must not start itself when scrolled back into view.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideo`'s props, extended with the three above and named as Backpack names them
* An IntersectionObserver over the player element, with `threshold` and `intersectionObserverRoot` passed through as the observer's own threshold and root
* Reely's play and pause commands, driven by intersection changes rather than by user input
* `onPlayChange`, which must report the pause and the resume like any other transition — the converted stories use it to drive a visible badge

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A playing video pauses when scrolled out of the observed root and resumes when scrolled back in
- [ ] `pauseOnOutOfViewport={false}` leaves it playing when out of view
- [ ] `intersectionObserverRoot` works when given a scrollable container element, observing against that container rather than the viewport
- [ ] A video the viewer paused by hand does not resume on re-entry
- [ ] `InPage`, `WithPauseOnOutOfViewport` and `WithoutPauseOnOutOfViewport` are converted, including the scroll-container layout with the sticky playing/paused badge driven by `onPlayChange` — that badge is how the behavior is checked by hand
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* Autoplay on viewport entry, which is the AutoplayVideo composition's job and a separate issue
* Cover images and light mode
* Pausing for any reason other than intersection — tab visibility, network conditions, and similar are not in this issue

**Source material:** Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-200-add-viewport-driven-pauseresume-to-backpackvideo`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `53ab35f`. The PR will target `backpack-parity`, per the brief's source-material note.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

Landed: PR https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/154, squash-merged into `backpack-parity` as `2fa5cfd`. All ten PR checks passed before the merge. Local gate: `pnpm test` 1033, `pnpm test:storybook` 111, `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`.

## Acceptance criteria

- [x] A playing video pauses when scrolled out of the observed root and resumes when scrolled back in — `InPage` pins the full cycle against a real scroll container watched by the browser's own `IntersectionObserver`.
- [x] `pauseOnOutOfViewport={false}` leaves it playing when out of view.
- [x] `intersectionObserverRoot` works when given a scrollable container element — every deterministic story observes against the container, not the viewport.
- [x] A video the viewer paused by hand does not resume on re-entry — see the design note below; this one needed a second pass to be true on every affordance.
- [x] `InPage`, `WithPauseOnOutOfViewport` and `WithoutPauseOnOutOfViewport` converted, with the scroll-container layout and the sticky badge driven by `onPlayChange`.
- [x] Parity matrix rows move off `gap` — 12 `full`, 3 `partial`, 23 `gap` across 38 rows, recounted by scanning rows.
- [x] Story tests pass.

Nothing from Out of scope was touched: no autoplay-on-entry, no cover/light changes, no pausing for tab visibility or network conditions.

## The one criterion that needed a second pass

"A video the viewer paused by hand does not resume on re-entry" was first implemented by having the wrapper's own click handlers cancel the pending resume. That left a hole: under `controls: true` the click target is `Player.Controls`, whose pause arrives through the player-report fold rather than a handler — so play → scroll out → pause by hand → scroll back **restarted the video**, against the viewer's last instruction. Backpack has the same hole, and the first review pass let it stand as parity. It is now fixed on its own merits: the hook records the playback it asked for, and any transition it did not request drops the pending resume. That subsumed the explicit cancellation, so one mechanism covers every affordance instead of needing a handler wired to each.

## Design notes for whoever touches this next

- **`isPlaying` is read from a ref, never as a dependency of the decision effect.** As a dependency, every play/pause while off screen re-runs it and re-arms the resume, so a video paused *while* off screen springs back on scroll-in. Re-adding it fails six tests.
- **An explicit `playing={false}` lifted while off screen must not be re-paused.** An earlier revision had `controlledPaused` in the decision effect's dependencies, which re-ran the effect when the parent lifted the pause and immediately paused the video the parent had just asked to play. Now read from a ref; two tests pin it.
- Every guard here was mutation-checked, not assumed — each fails exactly the tests named for it.

## Known limits, both recorded in the PR

1. The **fold ordering** (player report → off-screen intent → `playing` prop) is reasoned and documented but pinned by nothing: both adjacent swaps leave the suites green, because either conflict needs two sources changing in one commit while the intent always arrives in its own. The precedence *outcome* is pinned.
2. **Hand-pausing while off screen** cannot be pinned by a browser story — the automation driver scrolls a click target into view before clicking, undoing the scroll under test. Covered at the Vitest level instead, `controls` path included.

## Scope direction recorded during this issue

The maintainer clarified mid-issue that the wrapper is **not** meant to be a faithful reimplementation of Backpack or react-player. Two goals only: Reely's features should be solid on their own merits, and the stories should cover the same *scenarios* Backpack's stories cover, so react-player can be swapped out later. Comments that argued a behaviour *because* Backpack does it were rewritten to stand on their own; citations into the Backpack checkout are kept only where they name where a prop or a scenario came from. This is also what turned the `controls`-path resume from an accepted divergence into a bug worth fixing. [SIDEPRO-211](#sidepro-211) is the follow-up that would make the scenario claim checkable rather than asserted.

Also renamed the module off the word "viewport": `CONTEXT.md` defines Viewport as the player's own box, explicitly not the browser's, and the new code was using it for the browser sense.

<a id="sidepro-201"></a>

## SIDEPRO-201 — Add external play-state control to BackpackVideo

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-201/add-external-play-state-control-to-backpackvideo

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Let a parent component drive a BackpackVideo's play state from outside, the way Backpack's carousel integration does.

**Current behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` owns its own play state. Nothing outside the component can start it, pause it, or hand control back. Backpack solves this with a shared atom, which is how a carousel plays the video on the active slide and pauses it when the slide changes. Without an equivalent, that integration story cannot be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
An external controller can start a specific `BackpackVideo`, pause it, and release control of it, while the wrapper's own click-to-toggle keeps working and `onPlayChange` reports every transition whatever caused it. Build this on Reely's command API rather than introducing an atom library.

Two regressions documented in Backpack's own story must not reappear:

1. **One click starts playback.** Activating an external play command must start the video on the first activation, not require a second.
2. **An external pause is honoured after a manual play.** Once the viewer has started the video by hand, an external pause command must still stop it — a stale guard that ignores external commands after manual interaction is the specific bug to avoid.

**Key interfaces:**

* Reely's imperative command API — whatever handle or ref exposes play and pause on a mounted player is what the external controller drives
* A way to address one player instance among several, so a controller can act on the right one
* "Release control" — after it, the wrapper behaves as if never externally driven, and a later external command works again
* `onPlayChange`, which fires for externally-caused transitions as well as user-caused ones

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] An external play command starts the video on first activation, with no double-activation needed
- [ ] An external pause command is honoured after the viewer has started the video manually
- [ ] Releasing control returns the wrapper to its normal behavior, and a later external command still works
- [ ] `onPlayChange` fires for external transitions and user transitions alike
- [ ] `WithEvents` is converted, logging `onPlayChange`
- [ ] A carousel-integration story mirrors Backpack's `SocialCarouselAtomIntegration`: three buttons driving one player instance — simulate slide active (play), simulate slide change (pause), and reset (release control)
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* Building an actual carousel. The story simulates slide changes with buttons, exactly as Backpack's does.
* Adopting jotai or any other atom library. Reely's command API is the intended mechanism.
* Coordinating several players so that starting one pauses the others — not what this issue asks for.

**Source material:** Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (6)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Design guidance from the audit behind [ADR-0003](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/155), because this issue covers the same ground as Backpack's own external-control mechanism and should not reproduce it.

Backpack coordinates external play state through `videoPlayingAtom`, a module-global jotai atom typed `boolean | undefined` (`src/components/Video/video.store.ts:22` in the beta checkout). Two structural problems, both worth avoiding here:

1. **It is level-triggered, so it cannot repeat a transition.** The sync effect's dependencies are `[playing, parentPlaying]` (`useVideoPlayerState.ts:140`), so the sequence `false → viewer plays → false` is silently dropped: the atom already holds `false`, nothing changes, and an orchestrator's pause never lands. `undefined` has to mean "no opinion" to make the mechanism work at all, which needs an invariant comment plus two "do not reintroduce" warnings (`useVideoPlayerState.ts:47-55`) recording the two regressions it has already caused.
2. **Scoping is the consumer's problem.** Correctness depends on each instance being wrapped in a `ScopeProvider`, and nothing in `VideoPlayer` enforces it — an unwrapped page drives every video on it at once.

Also note `playing ?? parentPlaying` (`:118,132`): passing the `playing` prop permanently blinds the atom for that instance.

**What to do instead:** edge-triggered requests scoped to a `Player.Root`, never a shared level signal. The wrapper already has the shape — `off-screen-pause.ts` signals through an identity-keyed intent object precisely so that the same request can be made twice, and the wrapper folds it in with `useChanged`. Reely's command API is imperative and awaited, so this issue should not need a mirrored state atom at all.

One more thing to read first: this issue adds a **fourth** source of playback truth alongside the player's own report, the off-screen intent and the `playing` prop. The order those three fold in is documented in `backpack-video.tsx` but pinned by no test — both adjacent swaps leave the suites green, because either conflict needs two sources changing in one commit. Adding a fourth makes that ordering materially harder to reason about, so it is worth pinning it as part of this work rather than inheriting the gap.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-201-add-external-play-state-control-to-backpackvideo`, off `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Parked on an unanswered question.** A Factory Loop Session picked this up, hit a fork the brief does not settle, pinged the maintainer, and shelved the work when the deadline passed.

## The question

`PlayerController.play()` returns `{ ok: false, reason: 'not-ready' }` when no provider is attached (`packages/core/src/player-controller.ts:383-384`). A `BackpackVideo` that is not asked to start on load gets `loading: 'interaction'` (`backpack-video.tsx:387`), so it sits dormant, and the only thing that activates it is `activateFromInteraction` (`packages/react/src/use-activation.ts:265-297`) — reachable through `usePlayer()`, which `packages/react/src/index.tsx` does not export.

So the `PlayerHandle` this wrapper already forwards **cannot** satisfy acceptance criterion 1, "an external play command starts the video on first activation". Two ways out, and they are materially different work:

**A — expose activation on `@reely/react`'s public surface.** Add it to `PlayerActions`/`PlayerHandle`. External control is then the ref `BackpackVideo` already forwards: no new prop, and the wrapper's existing player-report fold (`backpack-video.tsx:224`) makes `onPlayChange` report externally caused transitions for free. Cost: widens the library's public API, which this brief does not mention. Note the deterministic stories already consume that ref position for the mock player (`backpack-video.stories.tsx:195`), so a story driving external control through it has to merge two refs.

**B — keep it wrapper-only.** A new edge-triggered request prop on `BackpackVideo`, in the identity-keyed shape `off-screen-pause.ts` already uses, with the first external play escalating `Player.Root`'s `loading` from `interaction` to `eager` and playing on ready. No library change; a second activation route only external control would ever use, and a genuine fourth source in the fold.

The session's recommendation was **A**, on the grounds that it is the smaller change and that under it no fourth source of playback truth is added at all — external commands arrive as ordinary player reports, so the fold stays three deep.

## A related note for whoever answers

Under either option, **"release control" has no referent.** Reely's commands are imperative and retain nothing, so there is nothing for a third button to release. Backpack's third button exists only because its mechanism is a tri-state atom (`video.store.ts:22`). Acceptance criterion 3 is either vacuous or the story ships a no-op button — worth recording as a deliberate divergence rather than implementing a mechanism to give it something to do.

## The shelved work

Branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-201-add-external-play-state-control-to-backpackvideo`, pushed, one commit `aff6786` — no PR opened.

It carries only the fold-order pin this issue's earlier comment asked for: two tests in `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/off-screen-pause.contract.test.ts` that fail if either adjacent pair in the fold is swapped, each verified failing against the hand-applied swap. All 23 pre-existing tests in that file were blind to both. `pnpm test` (1035), `typecheck` and `lint` all pass; `pnpm test:storybook` was not run, since nothing in the commit touches stories or the component.

Nothing of the external-control mechanism itself is written — that is what the question blocks.

One unverified finding, recorded so it is not lost rather than acted on: Backpack states the same precedence as a comment (`useVideoPlayerState.ts:30-38`), but declares its prop effect (`:127-140`) *before* its viewport effect (`:144-154`) while both call `setPlaying`, which would let the observer land last and beat the prop in a commit where both re-run. Read and cited by an implementation subagent, **not** independently confirmed — it must be re-read before it reaches `docs/backpack-parity.md`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Maintainer decision: option A — expose activation on `@reely/react`'s public surface.**

`activateFromInteraction` joins `PlayerActions`/`PlayerHandle` (`packages/react/src/use-activation.ts:265-297`, currently reachable only through the unexported `usePlayer()`). External control is then the `PlayerHandle` ref `BackpackVideo` already forwards — no new prop on the wrapper.

Two consequences for whoever implements this:

- **No fourth source of playback truth is added.** External commands arrive as ordinary player reports, so the wrapper's fold stays three deep and `onPlayChange` reports externally caused transitions through the path that already exists (`backpack-video.tsx:224`). The fold-order pin the shelved commit carries is therefore complete as it stands.
- **The deterministic stories already use the ref position for the mock player** (`backpack-video.stories.tsx:195`), so a story driving external control through it has to merge two refs.

Still open and unchanged: **"release control" has no referent.** Reely's commands retain nothing, so the third button in the carousel story is a no-op recorded as a deliberate divergence from Backpack's tri-state atom (`video.store.ts:22`) — not a mechanism built to give it something to release.

Un-parked: `needs-info` → `ready-for-agent`. Work waiting on `pedrosousa13/sidepro-201-add-external-play-state-control-to-backpackvideo` at `aff6786`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Resumed by a Factory Loop Session, working under the maintainer's option-A decision above.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-201-add-external-play-state-control-to-backpackvideo`, already pushed at `aff6786` (the fold-order pin) — brought up to date with `backpack-parity` before continuing.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/157 → `cbf6759` on `backpack-parity`.

Built under the option-A decision above. `activateFromInteraction` now sits on `PlayerHandle`/`PlayerActions`, so the ref `BackpackVideo` already forwards can start a dormant interaction-loading player. No wrapper change, no new prop, and the playback fold stays three deep.

Three things worth carrying forward:

- **An external play command is the pair `activateFromInteraction()` then `play()`** — one code path for both the dormant and the already-ready player, since each call no-ops in the case the other handles. Pinned as issuing exactly one play (`packages/react/test/activation.test.tsx`).

Gates run against the merged tip, not taken on trust: `pnpm test` 1080, `pnpm test:storybook` 121, `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`.
