## SIDEPRO-202 — Add aspect ratios, play-icon sizes and visual variants to BackpackVideo

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-202/add-aspect-ratios-play-icon-sizes-and-visual-variants-to-backpackvideo

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Add Backpack's layout and appearance props to BackpackVideo — aspect ratios, play-icon sizes, and visual variants.

**Current behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` takes whatever size its container gives it and renders one fixed play-icon size with no decoration. Backpack's stories cover portrait video, two visual variants, a larger play icon, and a theme-config escape hatch, and none of them can be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
The wrapper controls its own aspect ratio, play-icon size, and visual treatment, approximated with story-local CSS rather than Backpack's own styling stack.

New props: `aspectRatios` (either a single value such as `'16/9'`, `'9/16'` or `'natural'`, or a map keyed by breakpoint such as `{ s: 'natural' }`), `playIconSize` (`m` and `xl` at minimum), `variant` (`shadow-m` for a box shadow, `outline` for a border), and a theme-config-shaped escape hatch letting a story override the variant classes.

Close visuals are the goal, not pixel parity. Every approximation is recorded in the parity matrix notes rather than left for a reader to discover.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideo`'s props, extended with the four above and named as Backpack names them
* `aspectRatios` accepts a scalar or a breakpoint map; the breakpoint keys should match Backpack's own so story args copy across unchanged
* `'natural'` means the media's own intrinsic ratio rather than a fixed one — Reely already reports intrinsic aspect ratio as a CSS custom property, so use that rather than measuring separately
* The theme-config object's shape, which one story renders as JSON to document the wrapper's defaults

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A portrait 9/16 story lays out correctly, including its cover image and play icon
- [ ] A breakpoint-map `aspectRatios` value applies the right ratio per breakpoint
- [ ] `'natural'` follows the media's intrinsic ratio
- [ ] `playIconSize` changes the play icon's rendered size
- [ ] The shadow and outline variants render, and the theme-config escape hatch overrides them
- [ ] These stories are converted with Backpack's args: `YouTubeShortsVideo` (9/16, `https://www.youtube.com/shorts/n3eC51ZaDlk`), `WithShadowVariant`, `WithOutlineVariant`, `WithXLSizePlayIcon`, `WithThemeConfig`, and `DefaultThemeConfig` (rendering the wrapper's default style object as JSON)
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`, with every visual approximation noted
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* Reproducing Backpack's styling stack. Story-local CSS is the intended approach and pixel parity is not the bar.
* Building a general theming system for the wrapper. The escape hatch exists so one story can override classes.
* Any new aspect ratio Backpack's stories do not use

**Source material:** Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-202-add-aspect-ratios-play-icon-sizes-and-visual-variants-to`, off `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Landed.** PR [#156](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/156) → `8bfbf01` on `backpack-parity`.

All eight acceptance criteria covered. Matrix now reads 18 `full`, 3 `partial`, 17 `gap`; the six named rows moved off `gap`, and `YouTubeShortsVideoAndCustomCoverImage` lost its `aspectRatios` clause.

Gates, run by the session rather than taken on trust: `pnpm test` 1069, `pnpm test:storybook` 119, `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check` — all green.

## Two maintainer decisions recorded here

- **The play icon's default drops from 4rem to 3rem.** The old size was Backpack's `xl`, not its default `m` (`VideoPlayIcon.tsx:22-39` → `Icon/icon-wrapper.styles.ts:46-48`). Every story in both suites looks different for it. Approved.
- `variant` and `playIconSize` ship two values each rather than Backpack's four, because every other value would be a hand-approximated CSS rule with no story asserting it. `aspectRatios` carries Backpack's whole 18-value union instead — one rule per *shape* of value there, with a contract test per member, and none of them a ratio Backpack does not define. That is also the answer to the brief's "no new aspect ratio Backpack's stories do not use": every member is Backpack's own (`additional-types.ts:193-211`).

## What review caught, since it matters for the next issue

- **A false claim about Backpack recorded as a match.** An earlier revision asserted neither side sets `box-sizing: border-box`, and pinned the outlined box at 482px. Backpack loads Tailwind's preflight (`src/scss/base/_index.scss:1` is `@tailwind base`, no `preflight: false`), so its box is `border-box` and keeps its declared width. The wrapper now sets it too, and `WithOutlineVariant` asserts both the 480px width and the `box-sizing` that causes it.
- **Five wrong `file:line` citations**, all corrected against the checkout. Two of them were written by this session, not by an implementation subagent — the ADR-0003 discipline has to apply to the orchestrator's own prose as much as to a subagent's.

Two Backpack inconsistencies found while verifying, now in the matrix's "Where Reely is better":

1. Its `aspectRatios` JSDoc claims `@default '16/9'` (`VideoPlayer.tsx:76-77`) where the default 111 lines later is `{ s: 'natural' }` — accidentally right about the pixels, wrong about the value.
2. Its breakpoint widths disagree with themselves at `xl`: `BreakpointValues` says `1240` (`additional-types.ts:234`), its Tailwind `xl:` screen is `1264px`. Since `useAspectRatio` emits Tailwind prefixes, ratios switch at 1264 while anything reading the type switches at 1240. The wrapper uses 1264, because that is what the behaviour being reproduced compiles to.

Also renamed: `backpack-video-css.ts` → `backpack-video-styles.ts` (with its contract test), since the file now owns the wrapper's prop types and resolvers rather than only a CSS string.

## SIDEPRO-203 — Convert AutoplayVideo stories to a Reely-based wrapper

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-203/convert-autoplayvideo-stories-to-a-reely-based-wrapper

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-200](#sidepro-200) — Add viewport-driven pause/resume to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-204](#sidepro-204) — Convert VideoHoverPreview stories to a Reely-based wrapper
  - [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205) — Convert Wistia stories and wire wistia playerConfig passthrough

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Build a BackpackAutoplayVideo composition on the wrapper and convert Backpack's non-Wistia AutoplayVideo stories onto it.

**Current behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` requires an interaction to start playing. Backpack ships a separate `AutoplayVideo` component that starts muted playback the moment the video enters the viewport, with no interaction at all, and none of its stories can be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
A `BackpackAutoplayVideo` composition in the storybook app mirrors Backpack's `AutoplayVideo`: a muted video that begins playing automatically when it enters the viewport and pauses when it leaves. An optional placeholder image shows until playback starts.

This is a composition over the wrapper, not a rewrite of it. The viewport machinery and the cover-image handling already exist by the time this issue runs; the new behavior is that entering the viewport starts playback rather than merely resuming it.

Muted is not optional here. Browsers refuse unmuted autoplay, so the composition must guarantee muted playback rather than leaving it to a caller.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackAutoplayVideo`'s props, named as Backpack names them, including the placeholder image prop
* The intersection observation already built for viewport pause and resume — reuse it rather than adding a second observer
* The distinction this composition adds: first entry starts playback, where the wrapper's own behavior only resumes something already playing

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] The video autoplays muted on viewport entry with no user interaction
- [ ] It pauses on viewport exit and resumes on re-entry
- [ ] A custom placeholder image shows until autoplay starts
- [ ] Every non-Wistia story in Backpack's `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx` is converted with the same args
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* The Wistia autoplay story, which belongs to the Wistia stories issue
* Unmuted autoplay, which browsers block
* Changing `BackpackVideo`'s own defaults. This is a composition over it.

**Source material:** Backpack's `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-203-convert-autoplayvideo-stories-to-a-reely-based-wrapper`, cut from `backpack-parity` (the milestone's integration branch — the PR targets it, not `main`).

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: PR [#158](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/158) → `b07ca59` on `backpack-parity`.

`BackpackAutoplayVideo` composes `BackpackVideo`. Playback on entry comes from Reely's own `loading: 'viewport'` strategy plus muted autoplay, reached through a wrapper-internal prop the public `BackpackVideo` forces off at both the type and the runtime level — `BackpackVideoProps` is byte-identical to before. Backpack instead plays at mount and corrects with `pauseOnOutOfViewport`; nothing plays off screen here, and the preload margin is dropped to nothing because under this strategy loading early also plays early.

Converted: `Default`, `WithCustomPlaceholderImage`, `InPage` — deterministic (`Backpack parity/AutoplayVideo`) and real-playback (`Real playback/BackpackAutoplayVideo`) — plus a Reely-only `HeldPaused`. `WistiaVideo` stays a gap for [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205). Matrix: 38 rows, **22 full, 4 partial, 12 gap**.

Three things a later issue will trip over:

1. **The deterministic stories cannot carry Backpack's Vimeo URL.** Viewport loading commits a resolvable source with nothing clicked, so an offline suite needs `mock://`. Every other parity story gets away with a real URL only because nothing commits it. [SIDEPRO-204](#sidepro-204)'s hover preview will hit this the moment it loads without a click.
2. **`threshold` reaches the pause, not the start.** `Player.Root` exposes `loading` and `loadMargin` only, so viewport activation carries no threshold: with `threshold > 0`, autoplay starts at the first visible pixel and the pause hook stops it at once — one spurious play/pause pair through `onPlayChange`. Unexercised today, documented where a caller meets the prop, closable only in the primitive.
3. **Two `IntersectionObserver`s run per autoplaying video** — Reely's activation one and the wrapper's off-screen-pause one. The brief asked for one; they answer different questions for different owners and neither is hand-rolled, so the intent holds under [ADR-0003](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/blob/main/docs/adr/0003-backpack-is-a-scenario-source-not-a-spec.md). A contract test asserts the number on purpose. Recorded in the matrix as a departure from this brief, not only from Backpack.

**One finding for triage, not fixed here.** Roughly eleven `backpack-video.tsx:N` citations in `off-screen-pause.ts`, `off-screen-pause.contract.test.ts` and `docs/backpack-parity.md:62` were already stale before this issue and were left alone to keep the diff narrow. This session also found two citations whose ranges disagreed with each other. ADR-0003's citation discipline has no gate behind it, so it rots continuously: a test that parses `file:line` citations and asserts the target still contains a named anchor would close it, but every citation needs an anchor added first — a repo-wide change worth its own issue.

Review: both axes, two rounds of fixes — a widened public API closed at type and runtime level, three duplications extracted, the `Viewport` glossary conflict reworded, 15 citations re-pointed and verified by content. Gates on the landed tip: `pnpm test` 1093, `pnpm test:storybook` 125, typecheck, lint, format:check, plus dev-server and static-build checks by hand.

## SIDEPRO-204 — Convert VideoHoverPreview stories to a Reely-based wrapper

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-04
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-204/convert-videohoverpreview-stories-to-a-reely-based-wrapper

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210) — The loop prop is silently ignored by the YouTube and Vimeo providers
  - [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205) — Convert Wistia stories and wire wistia playerConfig passthrough
  - [SIDEPRO-203](#sidepro-203) — Convert AutoplayVideo stories to a Reely-based wrapper

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Build a BackpackVideoHoverPreview composition that plays a short muted preview on hover, and convert Backpack's VideoHoverPreview stories onto it.

**Current behavior:**
Nothing in the parity work responds to hover. Backpack ships a `VideoHoverPreview` component that shows a cover image at rest and plays a short looping muted preview while hovered — the pattern used for video thumbnails in a grid. None of its stories can be converted.

**Desired behavior:**
A `BackpackVideoHoverPreview` composition mirrors Backpack's: a cover image at rest; on hover, muted playback starts; after a preview window has elapsed, playback loops back to the start and continues while the hover lasts; on hover end, the cover returns and the video resets.

Backpack's default preview window is 3 seconds.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideoHoverPreview`'s props, named as Backpack names them, including whatever controls the preview window length
* The cover-image handling already built for the wrapper — reuse it rather than adding a second cover implementation
* Reely's current-time state and seek command, which is what makes the loop-back-to-start behavior possible
* Hover here means pointer hover. Touch devices have no hover state, so the resting cover is the correct behavior there.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Hovering starts a muted preview from the start of the video
- [ ] After the preview window elapses, playback returns to the start and continues while the hover lasts
- [ ] Leaving hover restores the cover image and resets the video
- [ ] Every story in Backpack's `VideoHoverPreview.stories.tsx` is converted with the same args, including its `DefaultThemeConfig` JSON-dump story
- [ ] The parity matrix rows for those stories move off `gap`
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* A touch-device equivalent such as tap-to-preview. Backpack does not have one.
* Audio during the preview. It is muted by design.
* Changing the wrapper's own hover zoom, which is a separate cover-image behavior

**Source material:** Backpack's `VideoHoverPreview.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-204-convert-videohoverpreview-stories-to-a-reely-based-wrapper`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `b07ca59` (the milestone's integration branch — the PR targets it, not `main`).

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: PR [#159](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/159) → `b5f7e49` on `backpack-parity`. All nine stories converted. Matrix: 38 rows, **31 full, 4 partial, 3 gap** — the three remaining gaps are all Wistia ([SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205)).

**This brief had a factual error.** It says "Backpack's default preview window is 3 seconds"; the source says `duration = 5` (`src/components/Video/VideoHoverPreview.tsx:48`, JSDoc `@default 5` at `:30`, in the v4 beta checkout). The matrix row for `WithCustomDuration` repeated the same claim. Both corrected; the implementation uses 5.

Divergences, all recorded in the matrix:

1. **The restart is position-driven** — it watches reported `currentTime` and seeks to zero on the report that crosses `duration`, where Backpack counts wall-clock from when playback was *requested* (`setInterval(duration * 1000)`), so load latency, a blocked play or a buffering stall desynchronise its interval from the video's actual position.
2. **`Player.Poster` cannot host a returning cover** — Reely hides it permanently per source once playback is reported, so `BackpackVideo`'s cover can never come back and this composition owns its own cover layer. Not a preference; there is no seam through the primitive. This is the constraint a fourth composition will meet too.
3. **One named control, not four tab stops.** Backpack's focusable hover root encloses three more, one of them nameless (`VideoCoverImage` sets `tabIndex={0}` while suppressing role and name under `role='img'`).
4. **`loop` is inert on Vimeo in both directions** ([SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210)), so `WithoutLoop` matches Backpack only coincidentally. The matrix says so rather than claiming the prop works.

Beyond the component: a reporting `seekTo` on the workbench mock, which had none — without it neither the rewind nor the restart was observable in any story. Three modules came out of duplication this exposed (`video-cover-image.tsx`, `compose-class-name.ts`, `use-on-change.ts`); `BackpackVideo`'s rendered output is unchanged, verified against the branch point.

**The review caught a real bug**, worth recording because the shape recurs: hover start never seeked to zero — only hover end did — so a keyboard-started playback carried its position into the next hover, and because the window watcher seeds from the player's current position, the restart then never fired at all. Fixed with a rewind on hover start plus an already-playing guard no pointer user can reach, mutation-proven.

**Escalating the citation-rot finding from [SIDEPRO-203](#sidepro-203), because this issue paid for it three times.** Moving code moved roughly 150 `file:line` citations, and two attempts to re-point them by offset arithmetic both produced wrong numbers before the third did it by content match — each pass silently invalidating citations the previous one had fixed. ADR-0003 requires the citations to be true and nothing enforces it. Two concrete asks for triage: a test that parses `file:line` citations and asserts the target still contains a named anchor, and the anchor convention (`` `:499`, `ref={ref}` ``) applied repo-wide so such a test can exist. This branch writes anchors on every citation it touched; the rest of the repo has none.

Gates on the landed tip: `pnpm test` 1130, `pnpm test:storybook` 134, typecheck, lint, format:check, plus all 75 stories verified by hand in both the dev server and the static build.

- **State:** Done

**Relations**

  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-204](#sidepro-204) — Convert VideoHoverPreview stories to a Reely-based wrapper

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** Add the playerConfig passthrough to BackpackVideo, convert the Wistia stories, and close out the parity matrix.

**Current behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` exposes no way to pass provider-specific options through to the underlying player. Backpack does, via a `playerConfig` prop that merges per-provider option objects over its own defaults. The Wistia stories depend on it, and by this point the Wistia provider exists but nothing in the parity demo uses it.

**Desired behavior:**
`BackpackVideo` accepts `playerConfig` — an object of per-provider option bags merged over the wrapper's defaults, with the caller's values winning on conflict, matching Backpack's own merge semantics. Its `wistia` entry reaches the Wistia provider, covering at minimum the player colour, the swatch setting that disables the blurred placeholder, and autoplay.

With the Wistia stories converted, the parity matrix is complete: every row across all three Backpack story files is `full` or `partial` with notes, and no row is left as a `gap`.

**Key interfaces:**

* `BackpackVideo`'s `playerConfig` prop — keyed by provider, with each value an options bag for that provider. A caller's value wins over the wrapper's default for the same key; unspecified keys keep the default.
* The Wistia provider's own options surface, which is what the `wistia` entry feeds
* Wistia's player colour, swatch, and autoplay options specifically, since the stories exercise them
* The parity matrix document, which this issue is responsible for finishing

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `playerConfig.wistia` options reach the Wistia player — a story with a red player colour and no swatch visibly shows both taking effect
- [ ] A caller's `playerConfig` value overrides the wrapper's default for the same key, and unspecified keys keep their defaults
- [ ] `WistiaVideo` (`https://wesleyluyten.wistia.com/medias/oifkgmxnkb`, light false) and `WistiaWithPlayerConfig` (swatch false, player colour `ff0000`) are converted with Backpack's args
- [ ] The Wistia autoplay story from `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx` is converted
- [ ] The parity matrix has no `gap` rows left across `Video.stories.tsx`, `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx` and `VideoHoverPreview.stories.tsx` — every row is `full` or `partial`, and every `partial` carries a note saying what falls short
- [ ] Story tests pass

**Out of scope:**

* Wistia provider features beyond what these options need. The provider's scope was set by its own issue.
* Passthrough options for providers other than Wistia, beyond whatever the merge mechanism gives for free
* Converting any story that is not Wistia-related. Everything else is already done by this point.

**Source material:** Backpack's `Video.stories.tsx` and `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx`. All work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (5)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-205-convert-wistia-stories-and-wire-wistia-playerconfig`, based on `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-04*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Parked on one question only the maintainer can answer.** The implementation is finished and green; nothing is half-done.

## The question

This brief's Desired behavior asks the `wistia` entry to cover "the player colour, the swatch setting that disables the blurred placeholder, **and autoplay**".

Colour and swatch are wired. Autoplay is not: `playerConfig.wistia` refuses `autoPlay`, `silentAutoPlay` and `preload` by type, because Reely already owns activation through `Player.Root`'s own `autoplay` and `preload` props, and letting a provider bag also start playback puts two machines in charge of one decision. The converted autoplay story reaches Backpack's result — a silent Wistia start with no poster flash — through the wrapper's own muted autoplay plus the two presentation keys (`stillUrl` → `poster`, `wmode: 'transparent'` → `transparentLetterbox`). The trade is recorded as a divergence in `docs/backpack-parity.md`.

**Accept that divergence, or wire the three keys through to the Wistia element?** Accepting needs no code change — the branch is ready to land as it stands.

## The shelved work

Branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-205-convert-wistia-stories-and-wire-wistia-playerconfig`, pushed, 11 commits on top of `backpack-parity`. No PR yet.

Every acceptance criterion except the autoplay clause above is met:

- `playerConfig.wistia` reaches `<wistia-player>`: `playerColor`, `swatch`, `stillUrl` → `poster`, `wmode: 'transparent'` → `transparentLetterbox: true`. The seam is a new `providerOptions` prop on `Player.Root`, wired for Wistia only.
- Backpack's merge semantics: a caller's value wins per key, an omitted key keeps the default.
- All three Wistia stories converted as deterministic plus real-playback pairs, with Backpack's own args.
- The parity matrix has no `gap` rows left: 38 rows, 34 `full`, 4 `partial`, every `partial` carrying its note.

Gates, all run rather than assumed: `pnpm test` 1153, `pnpm test:storybook` 137, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` — green. Every story was also rendered by hand in the dev server and in a static build.

Two review-driven fixes landed on top of the five implementation commits: the wrapper's `swatch` default no longer emits `swatch="true"` unconditionally (an absent attribute is how Wistia defers to media-level config, so the old default silently overrode it), and ~134 `file:line` citations that this branch's inserted lines had left pointing at the wrong code were repointed by anchor-grep, each now carrying a symbol anchor.

## Left for later, deliberately

- **~27 citations into `packages/react/src/index.tsx` are unresolvable**, pointing at lines 138–2724 of a file that is now 104 lines. That is pre-split rot this branch did not cause and did not make worse. Worth its own issue.
- A handful of minor review findings are recorded in the branch's own progress ledger rather than fixed: two story assertions that restate their own args, a stale meta description in `backpack-autoplay-video-real.stories.tsx` that still says "a real Vimeo embed", and two matrix notes that could name the human-checked story the way row 124 does.

## Re-entry

Answer the question here, then move this issue `needs-info` → `needs-triage` → `ready-for-agent`. A later session finds the work waiting on the branch. If the answer is "accept the divergence", the remaining work is opening the PR and landing it.

No code change follows from this. The work waiting on `pedrosousa13/sidepro-205-convert-wistia-stories-and-wire-wistia-playerconfig` lands as it stands. Returning the issue to `ready-for-agent`.

## SIDEPRO-209 — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-02
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-209/owasp-sweep-over-the-backpack-parity-milestone

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205) — Convert Wistia stories and wire wistia playerConfig passthrough
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix
  - [SIDEPRO-198](#sidepro-198) — Build @reely/provider-wistia
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213) — Derive the thumbnail lookup from detectSource instead of matching URLs twice
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-221](#sidepro-221) — Two dependency advisories are open and nothing in CI would have told us
  - [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) — The Vimeo iframe sets no referrer policy and grants encrypted-media unconditionally
  - [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219) — Nothing states what a Reely page must allow in its CSP, or what a substituted provider script would reach
  - [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218) — A provider's oEmbed response is trusted into an img src with no validation
  - [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — The Vimeo chromeless probe fires on attach whether or not custom controls are wanted
  - [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216) — The YouTube host option chooses the embed origin and is never validated
  - [SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215) — Wistia presentation options reach element attributes with no validation

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** Run a full OWASP Top 10 pass over the code as it stands once the Backpack parity milestone has landed, and file every finding as its own issue.

**Current behavior:**
The Backpack parity milestone adds real attack surface to this library. A source URL supplied by a consumer is parsed by source detection and used to construct a provider embed. A new provider loads a third-party script from Wistia's infrastructure at runtime. Thumbnail lookup makes network calls to provider APIs and renders the URLs they return as image sources. A `playerConfig` object supplied by a consumer is merged and passed into a third-party player. None of that has been reviewed as a whole, and no issue in this milestone owns reviewing it.

**Desired behavior:**
A single audit pass over the Project's code as it stands, structured by the OWASP Top 10, with every finding recorded as a new issue rather than fixed in place. The audit reports; it does not repair. That separation is deliberate — a sweep that fixes as it goes produces a diff nobody reviewed against a threat model, and it hides how much was actually found.

Areas the milestone's own work makes worth looking at, without limiting the sweep to them:

* **Source URL handling** — what a hostile URL string can do as it passes through detection and into an embed URL or iframe `src`. Scheme handling, host matching that can be spoofed by a lookalike host, and anything that reaches an HTML sink.
* **Third-party script loading** — how each provider's SDK is fetched and injected, whether integrity or origin is checked, and what a compromised or substituted script would reach.
* **Thumbnail lookup** — the provider API calls, what is trusted in the response, and what happens when a response is hostile or the endpoint is unreachable.
* **Options passthrough** — what a consumer's `playerConfig` object can reach in a third-party player, and whether any key can escape its intended scope.
* **Cross-origin surface** — iframe attributes, `postMessage` handling between page and player, and any origin check that is missing or too loose.

**Key interfaces:**

* The source-detection entry point and every URL it parses
* Each provider's loader — the seam that fetches and attaches a third-party SDK
* The thumbnail lookup and its handling of provider API responses
* The `playerConfig` merge and everything downstream of it
* Any `postMessage` listener, and the origin it does or does not check

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Every OWASP Top 10 category is considered against this codebase, and each one is either tied to a finding or explicitly recorded as not applicable with a reason — a silently skipped category is not a pass
- [ ] Each finding is filed as its own issue labeled `needs-triage`, describing the weakness, the path that reaches it, and the impact if exploited
- [ ] No finding is fixed in this issue. Code changes belong in the issues the findings produce, after they are triaged.
- [ ] A summary comment on this issue lists every finding filed, and states plainly where the sweep found nothing

**Out of scope:**

* Fixing anything the sweep finds. Findings become issues; the maintainer triages them.
* Auditing the third-party player SDKs themselves. The boundary is how this library loads and talks to them.
* Penetration testing a live deployment. This is a code audit.
* Changing any behavior the milestone's issues deliberately chose

**Note:** filed under the Factory's security-sweep convention, which asks every milestone touching the attack surface to carry one. Blocked by the milestone's attack-surface issues so it audits the code as it actually lands.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. All four blockers ([SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197), [198](#sidepro-198), [199](#sidepro-199), [205](#sidepro-205)) are Done, so the sweep audits the code as it actually landed. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-209-owasp-sweep-over-the-backpack-parity-milestone`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `97ab2cb`.

This issue reports; it does not repair. Findings land as new issues labeled `needs-triage`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

## Sweep complete — 7 findings filed

A full OWASP Top 10 (2021) pass over the code as it stands on `backpack-parity` at `97ab2cb`. Every finding is its own `needs-triage` issue; nothing was fixed here.

| Issue | Category | Finding |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215) | A03 Injection | `playerColor` and `poster` reach `<wistia-player>` attributes with no validation |
| [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216) | A10 SSRF / A05 | The YouTube `host` option chooses the embed origin and is never validated |
| [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) | A04 Insecure Design | Light mode discloses every viewer to YouTube and Vimeo before anything is clicked |
| [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218) | A08 Integrity Failures | A provider's oEmbed response is trusted into an `img src` with no validation |
| [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219) | A08 / A05 | Nothing states the CSP a Reely page needs, or what a substituted provider script would reach |
| [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) | A05 Misconfiguration | The Vimeo iframe sets no referrer policy and grants `encrypted-media` unconditionally |
| [SIDEPRO-221](#sidepro-221) | A06 Outdated Components | Two dependency advisories open (both dev-only), and nothing in CI checks |

## Every category, and where it landed

- **A01 Broken Access Control** — *no finding.* The library enforces no access control and holds no protected resource; the provider owns that. The one access-adjacent value it handles is Vimeo's unlisted-video `hash`, and it was followed end to end: validated as `[A-Za-z0-9]+` (`packages/core/src/source-detection.ts:28-29`, `isVimeoHash`), then sent only to Vimeo's own origins — the embed URL (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:60`) and `vimeo.com/api/oembed.json` (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/chromeless-availability.ts:10-11,34`). It never reaches a third origin.
- **A02 Cryptographic Failures** — *no finding.* No cryptography, no secrets, no credential storage, no persistence of any kind. Transport is HTTPS throughout: `detectSource` rejects every scheme but `http:`/`https:` (`packages/core/src/source-detection.ts:205-208`) and every endpoint this library constructs itself is `https:`.
- **A03 Injection** — [SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215). Also checked and clean: no `innerHTML`, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no `eval`, no `new Function` anywhere in `packages/*/src` or the storybook stories. Every URL this library builds goes through the `URL` API with `searchParams.set` rather than string concatenation (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:59-65`), and the one `querySelector` interpolation uses a module constant (`packages/provider-youtube/src/loader.ts:91-93`).
- **A04 Insecure Design** — [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217).
- **A05 Security Misconfiguration** — [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220), plus the second reading of [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216) and [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219).
- **A06 Vulnerable and Outdated Components** — [SIDEPRO-221](#sidepro-221).
- **A07 Identification and Authentication Failures** — *no finding.* This library authenticates nobody and has no session, token, or identity concept. Provider-side authentication happens inside the provider's own iframe or element, out of reach.
- **A08 Software and Data Integrity Failures** — [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218) and [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219).
- **A09 Security Logging and Monitoring Failures** — *no finding, and the category does not transfer.* A client-side library has no log to write and no monitor to alert; failures are published to the consumer through the provider-state seam, which is where a consumer's own monitoring belongs. Recorded as considered rather than skipped.
- **A10 Server-Side Request Forgery** — [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216), which is the closest analogue available in a browser library: a caller-controlled origin driving a request. No server-side fetch exists in this codebase to forge against.

## What the audit found already right

Worth stating, because a fix for any finding above must not undo it:

- Source detection validates before it trusts: host equality against fixed lists for YouTube and Vimeo, a registrable-suffix match for Wistia's per-customer subdomains, and per-provider id patterns (`packages/core/src/source-detection.ts:22-32,58-77`). Percent-encoding is rejected when malformed (`:201`), and a scheme other than `http`/`https` fails outright (`:205-208`).
- Privacy defaults are on rather than off: Vimeo gets `dnt=1` unless explicitly disabled (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:62`), Wistia gets `do-not-track="true"` on the same terms (`packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts:201`, `setOption('doNotTrack', …)`), and YouTube defaults to `youtube-nocookie.com` (`packages/provider-youtube/src/index.ts:103`).
- Every runtime dependency of every published package is pinned exactly, not by caret range.
- Three of four provider SDKs arrive as npm dependencies rather than remote script tags.

## One convention note for triage

None of the seven carries a milestone. Assigning one is a maintainer decision, and inventing one for a finding is exactly what the milestone convention says an agent sweep must not do — so they are left unassigned for the next triage pass to place.

## Scope note

No code changed on this issue, so there is no PR. The branch `pedrosousa13/sidepro-209-owasp-sweep-over-the-backpack-parity-milestone` was cut and is being deleted unused; the seven issues above and this comment are the whole deliverable.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Correction to this sweep's own record, 2026-08-05.** Triage verified finding [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) against the code and found the sweep had got its trigger wrong. Recording it here rather than editing the summary above, so the audit trail shows what the sweep claimed and what checking it produced.

The sweep filed [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) as *"Light mode discloses every viewer to YouTube and Vimeo before anything is clicked"*, grouping three requests under one trigger. Two of the three do not fire on that trigger:

| Sweep claim | Verified |
| --- | --- |
| Thumbnail oEmbed fetch pre-click | **Holds.** Fires from an effect on mount, gated only on `light && !startsPlaying` (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:501-502`) |
| Vimeo chromeless probe pre-click | **Not on that trigger.** `probe()` runs inside `start()` (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:268`), which needs an attach |
| `iframe_api` from `www.youtube.com` pre-click | **Not on that trigger.** Same reason — the loader runs on attach |

The error: `BackpackVideo` computes `loading = 'interaction'` for its ordinary light-mode path (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:493-497`), so under `<BackpackVideo light>` the provider does not attach until the click, and neither the probe nor the script runs before it. The sweep inferred a shared trigger from three requests sitting near each other instead of tracing each one.

Both facts still hold on a trigger the sweep did not name: **`Player.Root` defaults `loading = 'viewport'`** (`packages/react/src/root.tsx:99`), so a direct Reely consumer who never sets `loading` gets provider attach on scroll into view — probe and script both before any interaction. That is the real exposure, and it is in published packages rather than the storybook wrapper, which makes those two halves more serious than the sweep ranked them, not less.

Where the three now live, per the maintainer's decision to reuse existing homes rather than mint new issues:

- **[SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217)** — retitled to the Vimeo probe alone. `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`, Medium.
- **[SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219)** — absorbed the YouTube script-origin fact; it already owned that script and the docs gap. `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`, Medium.
- **[SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213)** — absorbed the thumbnail-on-mount fetch; it already rewrites that hook. Note recorded there that deferring the fetch is *not* the fix, since the cover is the pre-click surface and deferring it defeats `light`.

Nothing was dropped. The category count in the summary above is unchanged — A04 still ties to [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217), A08 to [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219) and [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213).

## SIDEPRO-210 — The loop prop is silently ignored by the YouTube and Vimeo providers

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** Provider hardening
- **Created:** 2026-08-03
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-210/the-loop-prop-is-silently-ignored-by-the-youtube-and-vimeo-providers

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197) — Build BackpackVideo wrapper skeleton, first parity stories, and parity matrix
  - [SIDEPRO-259](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/214) — startTime and endTime are silently ignored by the YouTube, Vimeo and Wistia providers
  - [SIDEPRO-249](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/205) — The play icon's post-click divergence is deliberate but undeclared in the parity matrix
  - [SIDEPRO-239](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/195) — Backpack wrapper's control bar omits SeekSlider, Time, MuteButton, VolumeSlider, and FullscreenButton
  - [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) — Let a consumer choose between Reely-drawn controls and the provider's own chrome
  - [SIDEPRO-204](#sidepro-204) — Convert VideoHoverPreview stories to a Reely-based wrapper

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Found by the review gate while implementing [SIDEPRO-197](#sidepro-197), which needed a looping Vimeo source and could not get one.

**Current behavior:**
`Player.Root`'s `loop` prop becomes part of `nativeOptions` (`packages/react/src/root.tsx:433`), and `loadProvider` forwards `nativeOptions` only to the HLS and native branches (`packages/react/src/provider-loaders.ts:22,29`). The YouTube (`:36`) and Vimeo (`:43`) branches never receive it, and neither provider package references `loop` anywhere. So `<Player.Root loop source="https://vimeo.com/..." />` type-checks, reports no error, and does not loop.

**Why it matters:**
The prop is silently inert rather than refused. A consumer has no way to discover the limitation short of watching the video end — and Reely's own capability vocabulary exists precisely so a provider can report what it cannot do.

**Desired behavior:**
Either loop reaches the YouTube and Vimeo providers (both SDKs support it — YouTube via its `loop`/`playlist` player vars, Vimeo via its `loop` embed parameter), or the limitation becomes visible through the capability surface instead of being dropped on the floor. Which of the two is the decision this issue needs.

**Notes:**
Not a regression — this looks like it has always been the case. Filed as `needs-triage` rather than picked up directly, because the fix is one of two designs and that choice is the maintainer's. `docs/backpack-parity.md` records the `Loop` row as `partial` and points here.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug
**Summary:** Make `Player.Root`'s `loop` prop work on YouTube, Vimeo and Wistia, and retire Wistia's duplicate spelling of the same capability

**Decision taken during triage.** Of the two designs the body offers, **make it work everywhere** is chosen. Reporting the limitation through `PlayerCapabilities` was considered and rejected: all three providers can loop, so a capability entry would be documenting a gap we had chosen not to close. No capability entry is added.

**The scope is wider than the body says.** The body names YouTube and Vimeo. Verified during triage, the real picture is:

| Provider | Honors `Player.Root`'s `loop` today | Why |
| --- | --- | --- |
| native | Yes | implements it directly |
| HLS | Yes | delegates to the native provider, which implements it |
| YouTube | **No** | never receives the native playback options `loop` travels in |
| Vimeo | **No** | same |
| Wistia | **No** | same — but it has its **own** `loop` option in its provider options, which maps onto an end-of-video behaviour |

So three providers drop the prop, and Wistia is the worst of the three: the capability works, under a second name, while the documented prop does nothing. That duplicate spelling is part of this issue.

**Current behavior:**

`loop` is folded into the native playback options at the react layer, and those options are forwarded only to the HLS and native provider branches. The YouTube, Vimeo, and Wistia branches receive their own provider-specific options instead and never see `loop`. `<Player.Root loop source="https://vimeo.com/…" />` type-checks, reports no error, and does not loop.

**Desired behavior:**

`loop` is honored by every provider, through each one's own mechanism:

- **YouTube** — its player vars support looping, which for a single video requires the video id also be given as the playlist. Both parts are needed; setting only the loop var silently does nothing, which is the same failure this issue is about.
- **Vimeo** — its embed accepts a loop parameter on the embed URL.
- **Wistia** — the existing end-of-video behaviour its own `loop` option already drives.

`providerOptions.wistia.loop` becomes redundant. Retire it: `Player.Root`'s `loop` is the one spelling. If it must remain for compatibility, it has to resolve consistently with the root prop rather than fighting it, and its doc comment must say which wins — but removal is preferred, and this library is pre-1.0.

**Key interfaces:**

- The react layer's provider-loading step, where native playback options are forwarded to some branches and not others. That asymmetry is the root cause, and the fix should make `loop` reach every provider rather than special-casing three call sites.
- `YouTubeProviderOptions`, `VimeoProviderOptions`, `WistiaProviderOptions` — whether `loop` should travel as a provider option or stay in the shared playback options is the implementer's call, but the consumer-facing answer must be a single `loop` prop on `Player.Root`.
- `WistiaProviderOptions.loop` — removed, or documented as resolving with the root prop.
- `docs/backpack-parity.md` records the `Loop` row as `partial` and points at this issue. It stops being partial once this lands.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `<Player.Root loop />` loops on a YouTube source
- [ ] `<Player.Root loop />` loops on a Vimeo source
- [ ] `<Player.Root loop />` loops on a Wistia source
- [ ] `loop` still works on native and HLS sources, unchanged
- [ ] Omitting `loop` does not loop on any of the five providers
- [ ] For YouTube, both the loop var and the single-video playlist var are set — a test pins this, since setting one without the other is a silent no-op
- [ ] `providerOptions.wistia.loop` is removed, or its interaction with the root prop is tested and documented
- [ ] `docs/backpack-parity.md`'s `Loop` row no longer reads `partial`
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, and `pnpm lint` stay green

**Out of scope:**

- Adding a `loop` entry to `PlayerCapabilities` — explicitly rejected during triage
- Looping a playlist or a sequence of sources; this is single-source looping only
- `endTime` and `startTime`, which travel in the same options bag and have the same forwarding asymmetry. If they are also dropped on these three providers, that is a separate issue worth filing — note it, do not fix it here

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-210-the-loop-prop-is-silently-ignored-by-the-youtube-and-vimeo`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/173 — squash-merged to `main` with the full required check set green, e2e across chromium, firefox and webkit included.

`loop` now follows the `controls` idiom from ADR-0004: one home on `Root`, folded into the active provider's own bag, with `PlayerProviderOptions` omitting the key so a second spelling is unrepresentable. `providerOptions.wistia.loop` is retired as a consumer spelling; the provider-level key stays as Wistia's own channel and is now documented as such.

**Acceptance criteria** — all met except one that could not apply:

- The `docs/backpack-parity.md` `Loop` row criterion is **not applicable on `main`**. That file exists only on the `backpack-parity` branch. Whoever lands that branch should flip the row there.

**Two things worth recording:**

1. **The brief's premise about re-attach cost was not tested, and a first draft got it wrong.** An early version of this change claimed that folding `loop` into the provider bag causes the embed to re-attach on a `loop` change. It does not. `nativeOptionsEqual` (`packages/react/src/use-activation.ts:113`) already compared `loop` on every source type, with no branch on the source — so a `loop` change always rebuilt the provider. The fold changes what that rebuild produces, not whether it happens. The wrong framing had reached the changeset and ADR-0004 before review caught it. **If a later brief in this milestone reasons about activation identity, check it against `nativeOptionsEqual`'s call sites rather than assuming a fold introduces the cost.**

2. **`startTime` and `endTime` were confirmed to have the identical asymmetry** and are filed as [SIDEPRO-259](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/214), per this brief's out-of-scope instruction. They are not a copy of this fix: Vimeo and Wistia have no native end-time, so an end time is either emulated in the adapter or reported unavailable — the `PlayerCapabilities` question this issue was able to reject outright.

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** Nothing currently checks the `BackpackVideo` wrapper against the component it stands in for. Build a comparison harness that drives both Storybooks and asserts the wrapper matches Backpack's own geometry and pre-play behaviour.

**Current behavior:**

Parity is asserted three ways today, and each has a hole:

* `pnpm test:storybook` — 107 play-function tests in real Chromium, but every one runs against the **mock provider**. No real video is involved in any deterministic story, by design (the offline guard in `apps/storybook/.storybook/vitest.setup.ts`).
* `Real playback/BackpackVideo` — the stories that do attach a real provider carry `!test`, so they are excluded from the automated run and are only ever checked by hand.
* `pnpm test:e2e --project=visual` — visual baselines exist (`e2e/__screenshots__/`) but all five cover the reference player. `grep -rln backpack e2e/` returns nothing: no e2e test touches the wrapper at all.

So two claims are currently unverified by anything: that the wrapper genuinely plays a real Vimeo/YouTube/Wistia video, and that what it renders resembles Backpack's own output.

**Desired behavior:**

A harness that builds and serves both Storybooks, walks matched story pairs, and reports divergences.

**Compare measurements, not pixels.** `backpack-video-css.ts:1-10` states the wrapper reproduces Backpack's *behaviour*, not its styling stack — story-local CSS and Backpack's class names, none of its tailwind-variants or theme tokens. A pixel diff would therefore be red on every row by construction, and would say nothing. What is actually comparable, per story pair:

* the player frame's aspect ratio and its box relative to its container
* the cover image's geometry within that frame, and its `object-fit` result
* the play icon's rendered size and whether it is centred
* the hover zoom factor (Backpack's own is `scale(1.05)` over `200ms`)
* whether the player/iframe region is mounted before activation, and after
* the accessible name of the click target in each state

A masked pixel diff is worth having only on the real-playback side, with the video region itself masked out.

**Key interfaces:**

* Backpack v4 beta lives at `/Users/pedrosousa/Documents/apps/backpack/beta` (`@ef-global/backpack` `4.5.0-beta.19`), `node_modules` already installed. Build its Storybook with `npm run build:storybook`; the Video stories are `stories/components/Video/Video.stories.tsx`, 25 exports.
* Reely's side is `pnpm --filter @reely/storybook build`, already used by hand during [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199)'s verification.
* Playwright is already a devDependency with a `visual` project in `playwright.config.ts`; `e2e/visual.spec.ts` is the reference for how this repo drives a served Storybook.
* The story-pair mapping already exists as prose in `docs/backpack-parity.md` — 38 rows naming the Backpack story and its Reely counterpart. Consider deriving the harness's pair list from that table so the two cannot drift.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Both Storybooks build and serve from one command
- [ ] The harness walks the story pairs and reports, per pair, each measurement above with Backpack's value and Reely's side by side
- [ ] A real divergence fails the run; an intentional one (styling, documented in the matrix) does not
- [ ] The `full` rows in `docs/backpack-parity.md` are backed by an actual measurement, not only by prose
- [ ] Real playback is covered: at least one test asserts the wrapper attaches a provider and plays, rather than only driving the mock

**Out of scope:**

* Making the wrapper match Backpack's *styling* — the divergence is deliberate and recorded
* Wiring this into required CI checks. Land it runnable by hand first; a Backpack checkout is a local prerequisite and CI has no access to it
* Fixing whatever divergences the harness finds — those become their own issues

**Also worth fixing while here:** every brief and plan so far cites `/Users/pedrosousa/Documents/apps/backpack/v3/...`, but v4 beta is the real target. The two are byte-identical for `Video.tsx`, `VideoTypes.ts`, `VideoCoverImage.tsx` and `hooks/useVideoData.ts`, so no landed work is invalidated — `VideoPlayer.tsx` differs only by the `playerConfig` merge ([SIDEPRO-205](#sidepro-205)'s subject) and the stories only by the `WistiaWithPlayerConfig` story the matrix already tracks. The stale paths in `docs/backpack-parity.md` should point at `beta`.

**Source material:** Backpack v4 beta's `Video.stories.tsx` and `docs/backpack-parity.md`. Work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

## Triage verification — brief amended

Moved to `ready-for-agent`. The brief in the issue body stands, with one correction and two confirmations.

* No e2e test references backpack. The claim that the wrapper has zero e2e coverage holds.

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-211-compare-backpackvideo-against-backpacks-own-storybook`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `cbb477e`.

Prerequisite verified before pickup: `/Users/pedrosousa/Documents/apps/backpack/beta` exists at `4.5.0-beta.19` with `node_modules` installed, and `stories/components/Video/Video.stories.tsx` is present alongside `AutoplayVideo.stories.tsx` and `VideoHoverPreview.stories.tsx`. `build:storybook` is defined there.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed via https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/168, squash-merged as `0193f23` onto `backpack-parity`.

## What landed

A comparison harness under `e2e/parity/`, plus `playwright.parity.config.ts` and a `test:parity` script. It serves both Storybooks itself (Reely on 4173, Backpack on 6007), derives its pair list by parsing `docs/backpack-parity.md` rather than duplicating those 38 rows in code, and resolves story ids from each Storybook's own `index.json` so the two cannot drift silently.

Both sides are measured through one function, so a difference can never be an artefact of measuring them two different ways: aspect ratio and box, cover geometry and resolved `object-fit`, play-icon size and centring, hover zoom factor and transition duration, player-region mounting before and after activation, and the accessible name of the click target.

## Acceptance criteria

- **Both Storybooks build and serve from one command** — `pnpm test:parity`.
- **Reports each measurement per pair, both values side by side** — every pair, whether or not it passes.
- **A real divergence fails, a declared one does not** — declared divergences live in one list, each citing what `docs/backpack-parity.md` actually records. Two entries written before any sweep proved them were deleted rather than kept.
- **`full` rows backed by measurement** — partially. 26 of 38 rows are measured. The nine `VideoHoverPreview` rows fall out as unmeasurable because Backpack's player is `display:none` at rest, which is itself now filed as [SIDEPRO-255](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/211); two more rows no longer resolve ([SIDEPRO-256](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/212)). The remainder stay prose-backed.
- **Real playback covered** — `real-playback.check.ts` drives a Wistia story and polls the engine's playhead from 0.33s to 23.5s over 24 wall seconds on a 115.434s media. Wistia because it is the only one of the three whose playhead is readable from the page; Vimeo and YouTube mount cross-origin iframes.

## The harness is red, by design

The sweep found **97 undeclared divergences across 26 measured pairs**, plus the 2 unresolved matrix rows. Fixing those is out of scope here, so they are filed as **[SIDEPRO-245](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/201) through [SIDEPRO-256](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/212)** — twelve issues, all `needs-triage`, none `ready-for-agent`. Two carry an explicit caveat instead of a confirmed-bug framing: the missing-cover classes may be noembed.com answering Vimeo URLs with a 403 body under an HTTP 200, and `mounted[post]` needs a determination before anyone acts on it.

The runbook added to `docs/backpack-parity.md` says this plainly, so a first run is not misread as a broken setup. A full sweep takes about nine minutes, which is why `PARITY_PAIR_RANGE` slices it into batches.

## Verification

`format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (1198), `test:storybook` (140) all green. `pnpm test:e2e --list` unchanged at 210 tests in 16 files — the harness uses a `.check.ts` suffix so it cannot join the default e2e run, and `naming.guard.ts` fails `pnpm test` if that rule is broken.

CI: `static`, `unit`, `storybook`, `package`, `visual` and `hls-native-webkit` passed. The `e2e` job failed on all three browsers with exactly the known-red set — `poster.spec.ts:157` ([SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184)) plus three `vimeo.spec.ts` network tests — checked against the last merged PR rather than assumed.

Nothing under `apps/` or `packages/` was touched: no divergence fixed, no wrapper styling changed.

## SIDEPRO-212 — A failed load presents BackpackVideo's cover click as playing video

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-04
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-212/a-failed-load-presents-backpackvideos-cover-click-as-playing-video

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-214](#sidepro-214) — Fold BackpackVideo's alt into the play button's accessible name
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) — The background-image guard matches its own doc comments, so the e2e suite is red on every branch
  - [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) — Let a consumer choose between Reely-drawn controls and the provider's own chrome
  - [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) — Add light mode, cover images and hover effect to BackpackVideo
  - [SIDEPRO-249](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/205) — The play icon's post-click divergence is deliberate but undeclared in the parity matrix
  - [SIDEPRO-244](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/200) — The With Threshold story counts onPlayChange calls and flakes in CI
  - [SIDEPRO-243](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/199) — BackpackVideo's own toggle reports playback before the player confirms it
  - [SIDEPRO-242](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/198) — ErrorDisplay and ActivationButton disagree on whether a configuration error can be retried
  - [SIDEPRO-239](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/195) — Backpack wrapper's control bar omits SeekSlider, Time, MuteButton, VolumeSlider, and FullscreenButton

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Bug
**Summary:** When a provider fails to load after a cover click, the wrapper reports playback that never happened, loses the cover permanently, and overrides the primitive's own error label — so the viewer gets a button announced "Pause video" over a blank box, with no retry.

**Current behavior:**

Three things compound, all in `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx`:

1. The cover click is optimistic — `onClick={showsCover ? () => requestPlayback(true) : undefined}` (`:339`). Its own comment concedes Reely already queues the play (`packages/react/src/use-activation.ts:296`), so the handler's only contribution is reporting early.
2. That optimism latches: `startedPlaying` becomes `true`, so the cover is gone for good, and `onPlayChange(true)` has already fired for playback that never began. `isPlaying` stays `true` with nothing to reconcile it — `playerPlaying` never changed, so the `useChanged` fold never runs.
3. `aria-label={ariaLabel}` on `Player.ActivationButton` (`:311-317`) **overrides** the primitive's error label. `packages/react/src/loading-error.tsx:45` reads `ariaLabel ?? (isError ? 'Retry loading video' : 'Play video')`, and `children={''}` (`:341`) suppresses its "Retry" text too.

So a failed load presents a blank box with a button whose accessible name says "Pause video", and the retry affordance the primitive would have provided is suppressed.

**Desired behavior:**

A failed activation is visible and recoverable. The wrapper should reconcile its optimistic state when activation reports an error, stop overriding the label in that state, and surface the retry path the primitives already provide — `Player.ErrorMessage` and the activation button's own retry, neither of which the wrapper uses anywhere today.

**Key interfaces:**

* `state.activation === 'error'` from `Player.usePlayerState` — the signal the wrapper currently ignores
* `packages/react/src/loading-error.tsx:40-50` — how `Player.ActivationButton` labels and renders itself in the error state, and what the `aria-label` override costs
* `Player.ErrorMessage` / the error surface, unused by the wrapper
* The mock player can stage an activation error, so this is deterministically testable — `apps/storybook/.storybook/mock-player.tsx`

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] After a failed activation the accessible name is not "Pause video"
- [ ] The retry affordance is reachable — either the primitive's own or an equivalent the wrapper renders
- [ ] `onPlayChange` does not end in a `true` that never happened; the wrapper reconciles when activation errors
- [ ] A story pins the error state with a staged mock, and asserts the accessible name and the retry affordance
- [ ] Whether the optimistic handler survives at all is part of the work: if reconciliation makes it redundant, delete it

**Out of scope:**

* Redesigning Reely's error primitives
* Retry behavior for providers that fail *after* playback started

**Found by:** the audit behind [ADR-0003](<../docs/adr/0003-backpack-is-a-scenario-source-not-a-spec.md>). The optimistic handler is a copy of Backpack's `onClickPreview={start}`, kept during [SIDEPRO-199](#sidepro-199) on parity grounds — exactly the reasoning that ADR rejects. Recorded in `docs/backpack-parity.md` under "Where Reely is worse". Work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Triage verification — brief confirmed

Moved to `ready-for-agent`. The brief in the issue body is verified and stands unchanged. Two notes for whoever picks it up.

**Verified against the current tree.** [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) landed after this brief was written and shifted every line number in it. The line references in the body are therefore stale, but every substantive claim holds:

* The optimistic cover click is live, still scoped to `showsCover`, still calling `requestPlayback(true)`.
* The `aria-label` override is present. **It now appears in two places, not one** — the brief describes a single override. Both need handling, or the fix is half-applied.
* The `{''}` child on the activation button still suppresses the primitive's own Retry text.
* The activation-error label logic in the primitive is unchanged: `ariaLabel ?? (isError ? 'Retry loading video' : 'Play video')`. So the override still costs exactly what the brief says it costs.
* The wrapper reads `state.activation === 'ready'` and nothing else. It never inspects the `'error'` state. The brief's claim that this is "the signal the wrapper currently ignores" is confirmed literally — there is no error handling to extend, only to add.

**Navigate by construct, not by line number.** Find the optimistic handler, the `aria-label` bindings, and the `{''}` child by searching for them. The body brief's line numbers predate a landed change.

**Sequencing:** [SIDEPRO-214](#sidepro-214) folds a caller's `alt` into the same accessible name and is now wired `blockedBy` this issue, so it will not be picked until this lands. That means this issue is free to change the labelling however is correct — including removing the `aria-label` override outright — without coordinating. Do not defer label decisions to 214.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-212-a-failed-load-presents-backpackvideos-cover-click-as-playing`, cut from `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/167 (squashed as `cbb477e` on `backpack-parity`).

**What shipped.** The optimistic `onClick` on `Player.ActivationButton` is deleted, so the wrapper reports only what the player confirms and Reely's own `activateFromInteraction` still starts playback from that click. Deletion removed the source of the phantom `onPlayChange(true)` rather than correcting it afterwards, so no reconciliation branch was added — one could never fire. The surface now reads the activation error state: it passes no `aria-label` and no empty child there, so the primitive's "Retry loading video" and "Retry" apply, the play icon is suppressed, and `Player.ErrorDisplay` renders the reason message-only with `pointer-events: none` so it does not swallow the retry click. The cover survives a failed load, because `startedPlaying` now flips only on a confirmed report.

**All five acceptance criteria met**, verified by the `/review` Spec axis.

**One limit, recorded not fixed.** For `error.category === 'configuration'` no retry is offered at all: `ActivationButton` disables itself there while `ErrorDisplay` treats the same error as recoverable. The two primitives disagree and this wrapper follows the activation button, because reconciling them is a change to Reely's error primitives, which this issue put out of scope. A comment names the contradiction and the `ActivationErrorConfiguration` story pins the behavior. Filed as [SIDEPRO-242](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/198).

**Also filed from the reviews**: [SIDEPRO-243](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/199), the wrapper's own toggle `button` still reports playback at click time — the same shape as the bug fixed here, on a path this issue did not cover.

**Tests.** New contract test `backpack-video-activation-error.contract.test.ts`, plus stories for the staged error surface, the cover surviving a failed activation, and the configuration-error case. Typecheck, lint, `format:check`, `pnpm test` 1197/1197 and `pnpm test:storybook` 140/140 all green locally and in CI. The `e2e` job is red, with the same four failures already-merged PR #166 carries — `poster.spec.ts:157` is [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184), and three `vimeo.spec.ts` network tests. Nothing here added to them.
