## SIDEPRO-213 — Derive the thumbnail lookup from detectSource instead of matching URLs twice

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-04
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-213/derive-the-thumbnail-lookup-from-detectsource-instead-of-matching-urls

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — The Vimeo chromeless probe fires on attach whether or not custom controls are wanted
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218) — A provider's oEmbed response is trusted into an img src with no validation

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** The cover-thumbnail lookup re-matches source URLs with its own substring patterns beside `@reely/core`'s `detectSource`, and the two disagree at the edges.

**Current behavior:**

`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts:16,18` matches with unanchored patterns — `/vimeo\.com\//`, `/youtu\.be\//`, `/youtube\.com\/watch\?.*v=/` — where `packages/core/src/source-detection.ts:66-69,240-245` compares a parsed `url.hostname` exactly. Two consequences:

* `https://example.com/vimeo.com/1` matches the lookup and fires a request at Vimeo's oEmbed endpoint for a URL Reely's own detection rejects.
* `youtube.com/embed/ID` resolves to a provider and plays (`source-detection.ts:86`) but yields no endpoint, so `light: true` silently shows no cover.

The wrapper already depends on `detectSource` indirectly for provider choice, so the source kind and the video id are both already derivable — the second matcher exists only because Backpack's `useVideoData` has one.

A related gap in the same file: a thumbnail is refetched on every mount, with no cross-mount cache. And YouTube's oEmbed returns `hqdefault`, where Backpack reaches a 480px image via a string hack on noembed's response (`hooks/useVideoData.ts:29-39` in the beta checkout) — so Reely's YouTube covers are slightly softer.

**Desired behavior:**

One source of truth for what a URL is. The lookup takes `detectSource`'s answer — kind plus video id — and maps that to an oEmbed endpoint, so a URL that plays either has a cover or provably has no oEmbed concept. Plus a cache keyed by endpoint so a remount does not refetch.

**Key interfaces:**

* `detectSource` and its result type in `packages/core/src/source-detection.ts` — what it already returns for each provider
* `thumbnailEndpoint` / `useVideoThumbnail` in `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts`, and their contract tests
* Whether `@reely/core` exports enough for a consumer to do this without reaching into internals is part of the question — if it does not, say so rather than reaching

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A URL that `detectSource` rejects never triggers a thumbnail request
- [ ] `youtube.com/embed/ID` gets a cover under `light: true`, or there is a stated reason it cannot
- [ ] The substring matchers are gone, not merely tightened
- [ ] A remount of the same source does not refetch
- [ ] Existing contract tests keep passing; new cases cover both divergences above
- [ ] `thumbnail_url` from the oEmbed response is accepted only as an `https:` URL on the provider's own image hosts — anything else resolves to `undefined`, the way every other failure in this hook already does (absorbed from [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218); the response is currently type-checked as a string and nothing more, `video-thumbnail.ts:88`)

**Out of scope:**

* Chasing YouTube image resolution through undocumented URL rewriting. Note it in the matrix and move on if the oEmbed response has no higher-resolution field.
* Any change to `detectSource` itself

**Found by:** the audit behind [ADR-0003](<../docs/adr/0003-backpack-is-a-scenario-source-not-a-spec.md>). Recorded in `docs/backpack-parity.md` under "Where Reely is worse". Work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

## Triage notes — 2026-08-05

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Both divergences are confirmed, empirically rather than by reading.** The real `detectSource` from `packages/core/dist/index.js` was run against the real matcher bodies from `video-thumbnail.ts:16,18`:

| URL | `detectSource` | thumbnail lookup |
| -- | -- | -- |
| `https://example.com/vimeo.com/1` | failure `unsupported-string` | fires Vimeo oEmbed |
| `https://www.youtube.com/embed/dQw4w9WgXcQ` | success `youtube` | `undefined` — no cover |
| `https://attacker.example/?ref=vimeo.com/x` | failure | fires Vimeo oEmbed |
| `https://vimeo.com/76979871` | success `vimeo` | fires Vimeo oEmbed (correct) |

**Nothing gates the call site.** `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:501-502` passes the raw `url` string to `useVideoThumbnail` with no detection check, so under `light: true` the request goes out for a URL Reely itself rejects. A caller-supplied `placeholderImageSrc` suppresses it, and `light: false` never reaches it.

**Impact bound, which the original brief did not state:** `video-thumbnail.ts` lives in `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/` — the compat wrapper, not a published package. No code a Reely consumer installs contains this. The exposure is the demo app plus anyone copying the wrapper as the migration example it is meant to be. That bound is why this is `Improvement` at Medium priority rather than a security escalation.

**The cover renderer is a plain** `<img src>` (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-cover-image.tsx:68,70`) — no inline style, no `background-image`. So there is no CSS-injection path from a hostile `thumbnail_url`, and React escapes the attribute: the residual harm is an arbitrary cross-origin fetch (a tracking pixel with the page's referrer), not script execution. Fix accordingly — validate the URL, do not reach for escaping.

**Absorbed:** [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218) (`A provider's oEmbed response is trusted into an img src with no validation`) is closed into this issue. It asked for the response check in the same ~40 lines this refactor rewrites, and two issues over the same lines invites the second agent to re-litigate the first's design. Its substance survives as the acceptance criterion added above.

**Unverified:** the claim that YouTube's oEmbed caps at `hqdefault`. It needs a live request, and this issue's own "Out of scope" already rules out chasing image resolution.

## Also absorbed from [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — the mount-time third-party request

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Added 2026-08-05. [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) was filed claiming this hook's oEmbed fetch is a pre-consent disclosure. **The fetch is confirmed** — it runs from the effect on mount, gated only on `light && !startsPlaying` (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:501-502`), with no interaction required and no detection check. It lands here because this issue already rewrites the hook.

**But "defer it until the click" is not the fix, and an agent must not implement that.** The cover *is* the pre-click surface: a thumbnail fetched only after the viewer clicks would never be seen, so deferring the request defeats `light` entirely. Backpack's own default has the same shape for the same reason. The request is inherent to the feature; what is missing is that nothing says so.

So the ask reduces to stating it, plus confirming the two suppressions already work:

- [ ] The hook's doc comment states that `light: true` costs one request to the provider's oEmbed endpoint at mount, before any interaction, and that this cannot be deferred without defeating the cover
- [ ] Pinned by test: `light: false` causes no request, and a caller-supplied `placeholderImageSrc` causes none either — both are already true in the current code (`video-thumbnail.ts:56-57`), so these are regression guards for the rewrite, not new behavior

The `detectSource` criterion at the top of this brief already covers the other half of what 217 wanted: a URL that does not resolve to a provider never causes a request at all.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-213-derive-the-thumbnail-lookup-from-detectsource-instead-of`, based on `backpack-parity` per the brief.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/161 (squash-merged into `backpack-parity`).

`thumbnailEndpoint` now derives from `detectSource` alone — the unanchored substring matchers and `normalizeYouTubeUrl` are gone, and both provider endpoints are built from the parsed video id, so `youtu.be`, Shorts, `/embed/`, `music.youtube.com` and `player.vimeo.com` links all canonicalise to the same endpoint as the watch URL they point at. A URL `detectSource` rejects now fires no request at all.

Also in scope and done: `thumbnail_url` is accepted only as an `https:` URL on the queried provider's own image hosts, matched per provider (absorbed from [SIDEPRO-218](#sidepro-218)); an endpoint-keyed module-level cache holds the in-flight or settled promise so a remount does not refetch, evicting on failure so a transient error retries; the `AbortController` gave way to a `cancelled` flag, since a shared request must not be aborted for one unmount. The hook's doc comment now states that `light: true` costs one oEmbed request at mount and why it cannot be deferred without defeating the cover — [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217)'s half of the brief, answered by documenting rather than deferring.

Left undone deliberately: YouTube's `hqdefault` softness, which was out of scope, stays recorded in `docs/backpack-parity.md` as a standing limitation.

Verification: `pnpm test` 1170/1170 across 62 files, plus `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`, `docs:check` and `test:docs` all clean. The contract test grew from 19 to 31 cases. `/review` ran both axes — Standards found no violations, and both Spec findings were coverage gaps, closed in `177fa55`.

## SIDEPRO-214 — Fold BackpackVideo's alt into the play button's accessible name

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** Backpack parity
- **Created:** 2026-08-04
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-214/fold-backpackvideos-alt-into-the-play-buttons-accessible-name

**Relations**

- **Blocked by:**
  - [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) — A failed load presents BackpackVideo's cover click as playing video
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) — The background-image guard matches its own doc comments, so the e2e suite is red on every branch
  - [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) — Let a consumer choose between Reely-drawn controls and the provider's own chrome
  - [SIDEPRO-239](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/195) — Backpack wrapper's control bar omits SeekSlider, Time, MuteButton, VolumeSlider, and FullscreenButton

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** A caller's `alt` reaches the DOM but no accessibility tree, so the text a consumer supplies to describe their cover is dead weight.

**Current behavior:**

The cover image renders inside `Player.Poster`, which forces `aria-hidden="true"` after its prop spread (`packages/react/src/poster.tsx:60-65`). So `alt` survives as a DOM attribute — which is what the stories query — and reaches nothing assistive.

Backpack instead labels the cover container itself, `role="button"` + `aria-label={alt}` (`VideoCoverImage.tsx:99-101` in the beta checkout). That is worse in two ways and Reely should not copy it: `alt` defaults to `''`, so the common case is an unnamed button (axe `button-name`), and when it is set the button is named after the picture rather than after what activating it does. Reely's affordance is a real button reading "Play video" / "Pause video" with `aria-pressed`, which is the better shape.

But "our labelling is better" does not make the discarded text acceptable. A consumer passing `alt="EF campus tour, Brighton"` has said something useful about *this* video, and it currently goes nowhere.

**Desired behavior:**

A non-empty `alt` contributes to the accessible name of the affordance that acts on it, rather than being dropped — so a screen-reader user hears which video they are about to play, not just "Play video". An empty or absent `alt` leaves today's name exactly as it is.

**Key interfaces:**

* `backpack-video.tsx`'s `ariaLabel` and the two affordances it labels — the wrapper's own toggle and `Player.ActivationButton`
* `packages/react/src/poster.tsx:60-65` — why nothing inside the poster is reachable
* Note the interaction with [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212): that issue removes the `aria-label` override in the error state. Whichever lands second must not undo the other. Sequence them, or do them together.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] With a non-empty `alt`, the play affordance's accessible name includes it, and the name still says what the button does
- [ ] With no `alt`, the accessible name is unchanged from today
- [ ] The `aria-pressed` play/pause semantics survive
- [ ] A story asserts the accessible name in both cases, via role + name rather than by attribute
- [ ] `docs/backpack-parity.md`'s four `alt`-carrying rows drop the "reaches the DOM but not the accessibility tree" note once it stops being true

**Out of scope:**

* Making `Player.Poster` a non-`aria-hidden` surface. Whether Reely should offer one is a real question, but it is a primitives question, not a wrapper one, and it needs its own issue.
* `renderCustomImage`'s own `alt`, which a consumer controls entirely

**Found by:** the audit behind [ADR-0003](<../docs/adr/0003-backpack-is-a-scenario-source-not-a-spec.md>). Recorded in `docs/backpack-parity.md` under "Where Reely is worse". Work lands on the `backpack-parity` branch.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Triage verification — brief confirmed, sequencing decided

Moved to `ready-for-agent`, and **wired `blockedBy` [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212)**.

**Verified against the current tree.** `Player.Poster` still forces `aria-hidden="true"` after its prop spread, so a caller's `alt` still reaches the DOM and nothing assistive. The premise holds. Line numbers in the body brief predate [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) and have shifted — navigate by construct.

**Sequencing resolved.** The body brief says "whichever lands second must not undo the other. Sequence them, or do them together." That is now enforced by the tracker rather than by prose: this issue is blocked by [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212), so the Queue will not offer it until 212 lands. Prose an agent might skim is a weak guarantee; a blocker is not.

**What that means for this issue's implementer.** [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) removes or narrows the `aria-label` override on the activation button — it currently appears in two places. By the time this is picked up, that work is done. So:

* Read what 212 actually landed before designing the name. Do not assume the override still exists, and do not reintroduce one to carry `alt`.
* The accessible name must still say what the button *does*. Folding in `alt` means composing with "Play video" / "Pause video", not replacing it — an `alt` that displaces the action verb is a regression, not this issue's goal.
* The `aria-pressed` semantics and the empty-`alt` behavior are both acceptance criteria already in the body brief. They still apply.

**Everything else in the body brief is unchanged**, including its out-of-scope boundaries — `Player.Poster`'s `aria-hidden` surface stays a primitives question for a separate issue, and `renderCustomImage`'s own `alt` stays the consumer's.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Picked up by a Factory Loop Session

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-214-fold-backpackvideos-alt-into-the-play-buttons-accessible`, cut from `backpack-parity` at `0193f23`.

Blocker [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) is **Done**, so this is unblocked. Reading what 212 actually landed before designing the accessible name, per the triage comment.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Landed

PR [#169](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/169), squash-merged as `224929b` onto `backpack-parity`, branch deleted.

`BackpackVideoSurface` composes the play affordance's accessible name from `alt`: `Play video: <alt>` / `Pause video: <alt>` where there is one, today's bare `Play video` / `Pause video` where there is not. The `alt` is trimmed first, so a whitespace-only one is the absence it looks like. Both affordances the wrapper labels pick it up; the activation-error branches are untouched, so [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212)'s handover to the primitive's own "Retry loading video" survives with a non-empty `alt` in play.

### Acceptance criteria

- [x] Non-empty `alt` in the name, action verb still first
- [x] No `alt` → name unchanged
- [x] `aria-pressed` semantics survive
- [x] Asserted by role + accessible name, both cases — new `backpack-video-alt-label.contract.test.ts`, plus four reconciled story assertions
- [x] `docs/backpack-parity.md` rows corrected. The brief said four; there were **five** carrying the "reaches the DOM but not the accessibility tree" note, and all five are rewritten. The "Where Reely is worse" entry is gone, its substance folded into the existing "Where Reely is better" row per the [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) precedent in `cbb477e`.

### One defect the change introduced, caught in review and fixed here

`BackpackVideoHoverPreview` passed the same `alt` to its own non-`aria-hidden` cover image *and* down into `BackpackVideo`, where the prop was previously inert — that player gets `light={false}` and no `placeholderImageSrc`, so its own cover never renders. Folding the text into the button's name would have made a screen-reader user meet the same description twice. The composition now withholds the prop; pinned by a test.

### Worth knowing for the next issue in this milestone

- **`WithRenderCustomImage` passes no `alt` to the component at all** — the `alt` lives inside the consumer's own `<img>`. So [SIDEPRO-214](#sidepro-214) does not change that row, and it now reads as the one cover row where neither side has an `alt` to expose.
- **`e2e/parity/declared-divergences.ts` keeps all three `accessibleName` declarations.** They are still genuine divergences — Backpack names a `DIV` after the picture alone, Reely a `BUTTON` leading with the action — just narrower now. Only the prose changed. A future `pnpm test:parity` sweep will measure `["BUTTON[Play video: custom cover image]"]` on the two rows that pass an `alt`.
- **Pre-existing citation drift, not touched.** A lot of `backpack-video.tsx:<line>` citations across `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/` were already stale before this branch — `const showsCover` is cited as `:333` and has been at `:376` or lower since [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223). `docs/backpack-parity.md`'s `VideoHoverPreview` tab-stop row also still cites `VideoCoverImage.tsx:99-100` for the pair this PR corrects to `101-102` elsewhere. Worth its own issue.

### Gates

`pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` (1206 passed), `pnpm test:storybook` (140 passed), `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` — all green. `pnpm test:e2e` and `pnpm test:parity` not run: red on every branch for unrelated reasons ([SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184)), and the parity sweep needs two live Storybook servers.

<a id="sidepro-215"></a>

## SIDEPRO-215 — Wistia presentation options reach element attributes with no validation

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** Provider hardening
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-215/wistia-presentation-options-reach-element-attributes-with-no

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) — The Vimeo iframe sets no referrer policy and grants encrypted-media unconditionally
  - [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216) — The YouTube host option chooses the embed origin and is never validated
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A03 Injection** (untrusted value into a third-party sink).

## The weakness

`WistiaProviderOptions.playerColor` and `.poster` are typed as bare `string` and written straight onto the `<wistia-player>` element as DOM attributes, with no format check, no scheme allowlist, and no length bound.

```
packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts:216   setOption('playerColor', options.playerColor);
packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts:222   setOption('poster', options.poster);
packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts:198   element.setAttribute(attributeName(option), value);
```

`playerColor` is documented as a hex colour and `poster` as an image URL, but neither is enforced. Whatever the caller passes becomes the attribute value verbatim.

## The path that reaches it

`playerConfig.wistia` on `BackpackVideo` → `translateWistiaPlayerConfig` (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video-player-config.ts:75-82`) → `Player.Root`'s `providerOptions.wistia`, a public API this repo now exports (`packages/react/src/index.tsx:32`, `export type { WistiaProviderOptions }`) → the attribute writes above.

The reason this is not merely theoretical: Backpack's own `VideoPlayer` is driven from Storyblok, so a consumer wiring `playerColor` or a still image to a CMS field hands an editor — or anyone who can write that field — direct control of both strings.

## Impact if exploited

Reely publishes no boundary here, so the blast radius is entirely whatever `@wistia/wistia-player` does with the value:

* `player-color` is consumed as a colour and reaches the element's own styling. A value that is not a colour is a CSS-injection attempt against the third-party element's internals; whether it lands depends on Wistia's interpolation, which this library does not control and does not pin with a test.
* `poster` becomes an image reference with no scheme allowlist, so `http:` (mixed content on an HTTPS page), `data:`, and arbitrary third-party origins are all accepted. An attacker-set poster is a tracking pixel that fires for every viewer, disclosing IP and user agent to a host of their choosing.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

Validate at the provider boundary rather than trusting the caller: `playerColor` against a hex pattern, `poster` against an `https:`-only URL parse, with an invalid value dropped (setting no attribute) rather than passed through. That keeps the "an omitted option sets no attribute" rule the surrounding code already relies on. Whether to also narrow the public type is a design call for triage.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug
**Summary:** Validate the Wistia provider's `playerColor` and `poster` at the provider boundary, dropping invalid values instead of writing them onto the embed element

**Decision taken during triage.** The body's "suggested direction" is adopted: validate at the provider boundary, and an invalid value sets **no attribute** rather than being passed through or throwing. The public option types stay bare `string` — narrowing them was considered and rejected as a breaking change for anyone passing a computed value.

**Verified during triage.** Both options reach the element's attribute setter unchanged. A search for hex-colour or URL parsing anywhere in the Wistia provider package returns nothing — there is no validation to extend, only to add. `WistiaProviderOptions` is re-exported from the react package's public surface, so the consumer-to-attribute path the body describes is reachable from outside the repo.

**Current behavior:**

The Wistia provider accepts a `playerColor` (documented as a hex colour) and a `poster` (documented as an image URL), both typed as bare `string`, and writes each verbatim as an attribute on the `<wistia-player>` custom element. No format check, no scheme allowlist, no length bound. Whatever the caller supplies becomes the attribute value.

**Desired behavior:**

Each option is checked at the point the provider turns options into attributes:

- **`playerColor`** — accepted only if it is a hex colour. Anything else is dropped.
- **`poster`** — accepted only if it parses as a URL with an `https:` scheme. `http:`, `data:`, relative values, and unparseable strings are all dropped.

A dropped value sets no attribute at all, which is the same observable state as omitting the option — a rule the surrounding attribute code already depends on, so this introduces no new state. Dropping must not throw, and must not prevent the rest of the attach from completing: one bad presentation option should not fail playback.

**Key interfaces:**

- The Wistia provider's options-to-attributes step — where `setOption` decides whether to write. Validation belongs here, not in the react layer, so any consumer of the provider package is covered rather than only those coming through `Player.Root`.
- `WistiaProviderOptions` — unchanged. `playerColor` and `poster` stay optional `string`.
- Whether an invalid value warrants a `console.warn` in development is the implementer's call; a silent drop is acceptable, a throw is not.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A valid hex `playerColor` still reaches the element as before
- [ ] A non-hex `playerColor` results in no `player-color` attribute on the element
- [ ] An `https:` `poster` still reaches the element as before
- [ ] An `http:`, `data:`, relative, or unparseable `poster` results in no poster attribute
- [ ] Omitting either option behaves identically to supplying an invalid one
- [ ] An invalid value does not throw and does not prevent attach or playback
- [ ] Tests cover each accept and reject case above, at the provider package's own level rather than only through a story
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, and `pnpm lint` stay green

**Out of scope:**

- Narrowing `WistiaProviderOptions`' types — explicitly rejected during triage as a breaking change
- The YouTube `host` validation ([SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216)) and the Vimeo iframe attributes ([SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220)), which are their own issues in this milestone
- Any change to what Wistia itself does with a valid value
- Sanitizing values inside the third-party player; the boundary this issue defends is Reely's own

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-215-wistia-presentation-options-reach-element-attributes-with-no`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/171

Validation sits in the Wistia provider's options-to-attributes step (`packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts`), so every consumer of the provider package is covered, not only those coming through `Player.Root`.

- `playerColor` — kept only if it is a hex colour: three, four, six, or eight digits, hash optional. The 4- and 8-digit alpha forms are accepted because the brief said "a hex colour" and dropping them would silently break a caller passing one; the hash stays optional because the existing fixture and Wistia's own examples write the value bare.
- `poster` — kept only if it begins `https://` **and** parses as an `https:` URL. The prefix test is what the parse alone misses: `new URL()` resolves `https:poster.png` to `https://poster.png/` and trims surrounding whitespace, so a scheme-prefixed relative or padded value would pass a parse-only check while the string written to the attribute was neither.

A value that fails sets no attribute — the same element state as omitting the option. Silent, never throws. `WistiaProviderOptions` unchanged, both still optional `string`.

Also in the PR: inserting the checks moved lines that two comments in `packages/react/` cite by file and line, so both were repointed, and the rule `use-activation.ts` states was amended — a provider now sets an attribute only for an option that is not `undefined` *and* passes its format check, so a rejected `playerColor` compares unequal to an absent one while building the identical element.

Gates: 1062 unit tests, typecheck, lint, format:check, plus the full required CI set on the PR — e2e included — all green.

<a id="sidepro-216"></a>

## SIDEPRO-216 — The YouTube host option chooses the embed origin and is never validated

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** Provider hardening
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-216/the-youtube-host-option-chooses-the-embed-origin-and-is-never

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) — The Vimeo iframe sets no referrer policy and grants encrypted-media unconditionally
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215) — Wistia presentation options reach element attributes with no validation

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A10 Server-Side Request Forgery** in shape (a caller-controlled origin drives a request), with **A05 Security Misconfiguration** as the second reading.

## The weakness

The YouTube provider takes a `host` option, typed as a bare `string`, and hands it to the YouTube iframe API as the origin the player embed is built from. Nothing validates it.

```
packages/provider-youtube/src/index.ts:103    host: options.host ?? 'https://www.youtube-nocookie.com',
packages/provider-youtube/src/attachment.ts:142      host,
```

The default is the right one — `youtube-nocookie.com`, the privacy-enhanced host. The problem is only the override: any string a caller supplies is accepted, including an origin with no relationship to YouTube.

## The path that reaches it

`YouTubeProviderOptions.host` is part of the provider's public options surface. `new api.Player(target, { host, … })` (`packages/provider-youtube/src/attachment.ts:142`) makes the iframe API construct its embed URL from that origin, so the resulting iframe — inside the consumer's page, granted the `allow` permissions the API sets — points wherever `host` said.

## Impact if exploited

A hostile or merely wrong `host` value silently relocates the embed to an attacker-controlled origin while every other part of the page still reads as a YouTube player. That origin then receives every viewer's request, can render arbitrary content in the player's box, and sits in a frame the surrounding page has invited. Because `origin` is also passed in `playerVars` for postMessage validation (`packages/provider-youtube/src/attachment.ts:150`), the page additionally tells that origin its own origin.

The realistic route is configuration rather than a live injection — a `host` wired to an environment variable, a CMS field, or a per-tenant setting — which is precisely the class this category exists for.

## Impact bound worth noting

This is a caller-supplied option, not a value parsed out of a video URL: `detectSource` never populates it. So a consumer who never sets `host` is not exposed, and the default is safe.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

Validate `host` against the two origins YouTube actually serves the API from (`https://www.youtube.com` and `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com`), rejecting or falling back on anything else. If a genuine need exists for a third value, an explicit allowlist option is the honest way to express it.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** bug
**Summary:** Allowlist the YouTube provider's `host` option to the two origins YouTube actually serves its iframe API from, falling back to the safe default on anything else

**Decision taken during triage.** The body's "suggested direction" is adopted: validate `host` against `https://www.youtube.com` and `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com`, and fall back to the existing default rather than throwing. No escape-hatch allowlist option — if a third origin is ever genuinely needed, that becomes its own issue with its own justification. The public option type stays bare `string`; narrowing it was considered and rejected as a breaking change.

**Verified during triage.** The option is typed as a bare `string`, and there is no allowlist, URL parse, or origin check anywhere in the YouTube provider package. The default is correct — the privacy-enhanced host — so a consumer who never sets `host` is not exposed. The exposure is entirely in the override path.

**Current behavior:**

The provider takes a `host` option and hands it to YouTube's iframe API as the origin the embed is constructed from. Any string is accepted, including an origin unrelated to YouTube. The same value also informs the `origin` player var used for postMessage validation, so a wrong host both relocates the iframe and discloses the page's own origin to it.

**Desired behavior:**

`host` is checked before it reaches the iframe API. A value matching one of the two YouTube origins is used as given. Anything else — a different origin, a malformed URL, an empty string — is discarded in favour of the `youtube-nocookie.com` default, so the provider always attaches to a YouTube origin.

Falling back rather than throwing is deliberate: a misconfigured `host` should degrade to the safe default and keep playing, not break the page. The comparison should be on the parsed origin, so trailing-slash and case differences resolve the same way rather than being treated as unrecognised.

**Key interfaces:**

- The YouTube provider's option-resolution step — where the `host` default is currently applied. Validation belongs in the same place, so both the default and the override flow through one decision.
- `YouTubeProviderOptions.host` — unchanged, still an optional `string`, still documented as defaulting to the privacy-enhanced host. Update the doc comment to state that unrecognised values fall back to it.
- The postMessage `origin` player var — it derives from the same resolved host, so validating once covers it. Confirm it does rather than assuming.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] Omitting `host` still yields the `youtube-nocookie.com` default
- [ ] `https://www.youtube.com` is accepted and used as given
- [ ] `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com` is accepted and used as given
- [ ] An unrelated origin falls back to the default and does not reach the iframe API
- [ ] A malformed or empty `host` falls back to the default without throwing
- [ ] Trailing-slash and letter-case variants of the two valid origins are accepted, not rejected
- [ ] The postMessage `origin` player var reflects the resolved host, never the rejected input
- [ ] Tests cover each case above at the provider package's own level
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, and `pnpm lint` stay green

**Out of scope:**

- An option to extend the allowlist — rejected during triage; file it separately if a real need appears
- Narrowing `YouTubeProviderOptions.host`'s type
- The Wistia option validation ([SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215)) and the Vimeo iframe attributes ([SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220))
- Anything about the YouTube iframe's `allow` permissions, which YouTube's own API sets and this repo does not control

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-216-the-youtube-host-option-chooses-the-embed-origin-and-is`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/172

`host` is resolved where the default was already applied — the provider factory every consumer of the package passes through, not only those coming via `Player.Root` — so the default and the override flow through one decision.

The value is matched on its **parsed origin** against `https://www.youtube.com` and `https://www.youtube-nocookie.com`. Comparing origins rather than strings is what makes a trailing slash or a difference in case resolve to the spelling the allowlist holds, and it collapses the shapes a string comparison would miss — `https://www.youtube.com@evil.test` and `https://user:pw@www.youtube.com` among them. Anything else falls back to the privacy-enhanced default, malformed and empty values included, rather than throwing.

`YouTubeProviderOptions.host` unchanged, still an optional `string`. No allowlist-extension option.

**One correction to the brief.** It asserted the postMessage `origin` player var "derives from the same resolved host", and asked for that to be confirmed rather than assumed. It does not, and never did — `packages/provider-youtube/src/attachment.ts:141` sets it from the embedding page's own `location.origin`, per YouTube's postMessage contract. So that acceptance criterion is not satisfiable as literally written. The half that matters holds by construction: `origin` never carries the rejected input, and the tests now pin it across the accepted and fallback cases alike.

Also in the PR: `docs/third-party-requests.md` asserted that nothing validates the value, naming this issue, and cited two lines that had already moved. Corrected — the `frame-src` that table gives is now the whole set.

**Noticed, not touched** (pre-existing stale `file:line` citations, unrelated to this change): `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-26-buffering-stall-policy-35-design.md:16` cites `provider-youtube/src/index.ts:494` in a 177-line file, and `e2e/reference.spec.ts:383` attributes `pictureInPicture: providerUnavailable` to `provider-youtube/src/index.ts` when it lives in `adapter-values.ts:64`.

Gates: 1070 unit tests, typecheck, lint, format:check, plus the full required CI set on the PR — e2e included — all green.

<a id="sidepro-217"></a>

## SIDEPRO-217 — The Vimeo chromeless probe fires on attach whether or not custom controls are wanted

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-217/the-vimeo-chromeless-probe-fires-on-attach-whether-or-not-custom

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-257](#sidepro-257) — Land the library work from backpack-parity on main, without the Backpack parity surface
  - [SIDEPRO-258](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/213) — The @real Vimeo plan tests poll customControls against stories that never opt into the probe
  - [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219) — Nothing states what a Reely page must allow in its CSP, or what a substituted provider script would reach
  - [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213) — Derive the thumbnail lookup from detectSource instead of matching URLs twice

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Retitled and narrowed on 2026-08-05.** As filed by the sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) this issue claimed three pre-consent requests sharing one trigger. Verification showed the trigger was wrong for two of them, and they have moved to the issues that own that code — see "What this issue used to claim" below. What survives is the Vimeo probe.

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Bug
**Summary:** Every Vimeo source sends a request to `vimeo.com/api/oembed.json` when its provider attaches, to answer a question — may Reely draw its own controls? — that most callers never ask.

**Current behavior:**

`createVimeoAttachment`'s `start()` calls `chromeless.probe()` unconditionally (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:268`, `const chromelessProbe = chromeless.probe();`). The probe fetches the public oEmbed record to read the owner's account tier, because Vimeo gates chromeless playback on it and reports it nowhere in the player SDK (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/chromeless-availability.ts:34`).

One branch already skips the network: `options.controls === true` resolves immediately to `unavailable` without fetching (`chromeless-availability.ts:108-112`). Every other case fetches — including the common one where the consumer never reads `customControls` from the capabilities snapshot at all.

**Why this is a defect rather than a design preference:** the request has no effect unless someone consults the verdict. `customControlsAvailability()` is a getter (`chromeless-availability.ts:121`); nothing forces a caller to call it. So for a consumer using Vimeo's own controls implicitly — by not setting `controls` and not inspecting capabilities — the probe is a third-party request whose result is discarded.

**The privacy edge, which is why the sweep found it:** with Reely's default loading strategy this happens before any interaction. `Player.Root` defaults `loading = 'viewport'` (`packages/react/src/root.tsx:99`), so the provider attaches when the player's box scrolls into view and the probe goes out then — no click, no consent. The library otherwise takes privacy defaults seriously, setting `dnt=1` on the embed unless explicitly disabled (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:62`), which is the intent this request works against.

**Desired behavior:**

The probe runs when its answer is wanted, not when the player attaches. A consumer who never asks whether custom controls are available never causes a request to Vimeo.

Preserve what the current design got right, and it is subtle: `probe` and `adopt` are deliberately separate so a superseded attach cannot overwrite a live one's verdict (`chromeless-availability.ts:88-97`), and the probe is raced against the embed's own load with a 4s fallback so a late verdict is never useless (`CHROMELESS_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS`, `:8`). Whatever replaces the eager call must keep both properties — a lazily-triggered probe still needs the generation guard and still needs to not block a ready state.

**Key interfaces:**

* `createVimeoChromelessAvailability` and its `probe` / `adopt` / `customControlsAvailability` triple (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/chromeless-availability.ts:88-123`)
* The call site in `start()` (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:268`) and how its result reaches `adopt`
* The host capabilities surface — what a consumer reads to ask the question, and whether reading it can be the trigger
* `packages/provider-vimeo/test/chromeless-availability.test.ts` and `test/index.test.ts`, which pin the current timing

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A Vimeo player that attaches and plays, with no caller reading custom-controls availability, sends no request to `vimeo.com/api/oembed.json` — asserted with a fake `fetch` that fails the test if called
- [ ] A caller that does read custom-controls availability still gets a correct verdict, and still gets it in time to gate a control bar
- [ ] The `options.controls === true` short circuit keeps skipping the network
- [ ] The generation guard survives: a probe settling after its attach is superseded cannot overwrite a live attach's verdict, with a test that pins it
- [ ] The 4s fallback still applies to whatever path triggers the probe
- [ ] Existing Vimeo tests pass unchanged, or a changed expectation is justified in the commit message

**Out of scope:**

* The YouTube script origin and the thumbnail lookup — both moved, see below
* Any change to what the verdict means, or to the account-tier lists
* A consent framework. This issue removes an unnecessary request; it does not add a consent gate.

## What this issue used to claim, and what verification found

Checked against the code rather than reasoned about, because the original filing got the trigger wrong:

| Original claim | Verdict |
| -- | -- |
| Thumbnail oEmbed fetch before any click | **Confirmed**, but it is `apps/storybook/` code, not a published package — moved to [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213), which rewrites that hook |
| This probe before any click | **Confirmed** under `Player.Root`'s `viewport` default — kept here |
| `iframe_api` fetched from `www.youtube.com` despite the nocookie default | **Confirmed** as a fact, but not on the trigger claimed — moved to [SIDEPRO-219](#sidepro-219), which owns that script |

The original filing framed all three as "light mode discloses every viewer". That was wrong: `BackpackVideo` computes `loading = 'interaction'` for the ordinary light-mode path (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:493-497`), so under `<BackpackVideo light>` the provider does not attach until the click and neither this probe nor the YouTube script runs before it. The exposure is Reely's own `viewport` default, not Backpack's light mode.

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-217-the-vimeo-chromeless-probe-fires-on-attach-whether-or-not`, based on `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/162 (squash-merged into `backpack-parity` as `c0ff5a5`).

`VimeoProviderOptions` gains `customControls?: boolean`. `probe()` now runs three branches in precedence order: `controls === true` still short-circuits to `unavailable`/`provider` with no request; `customControls !== true` resolves the unresolved value with **no request**; only an explicit opt-in reaches the oEmbed lookup, still raced against the embed's own load with the 4s fallback.

This narrows what `probe()` does rather than when it is called. The eager call site in `attachment.ts` still fires on every attach, which is what keeps the generation guard and the 4s race intact — deferring the call site would have broken both, and the brief was right to warn about it.

**On the trigger design, since the brief left it open.** `options.controls === false` was considered and rejected: `attachment.ts:61` sets `controls=0` whenever `controls !== true` and the README documents the embed as chromeless by default, so unset and `false` build the identical embed — gating on `=== false` would have two identical embeds reporting different verdicts. Reading the capability cannot be the trigger either: `playerCapabilities()` folds `customControls` into every published snapshot, so a consumer's read is indistinguishable from the adapter's own. That answers the brief's "whether reading it can be the trigger" with a no.

**Scope note worth carrying forward.** This is the provider package only. `packages/react/src/provider-loaders.ts` passes no options to `createVimeoProvider` and `PlayerProviderOptions` has no `vimeo` key — its own comment calls that a deliberate absence pending the Vimeo provider's own options issue. So a `Player.Root` consumer cannot set this option yet and the probe never fires from the React path at all. That is the intended privacy default, and the README now states it. Whoever picks up the Vimeo provider-options issue inherits wiring this through.

**Verification.** `pnpm test` 1175/1175 across 62 files; `typecheck`, `lint`, `format:check`, `docs:check`, `test:docs` all clean; provider-vimeo suite 155/155. The headline case uses a `fetch` that throws if called at all.

The generation-guard test was rewritten after review found the first version could not fail — it asserted against the first ready patch, emitted before the stale probe settles, so a wrongly-adopted second emit was invisible. It now asserts the patch count does not grow and reads the newest capabilities snapshot, and was proven by mutation: with the `isStale` guard neutered it fails (`expected […] to have a length of 3 but got 4`), and passes with it restored.

<a id="sidepro-218"></a>

## SIDEPRO-218 — A provider's oEmbed response is trusted into an img src with no validation

- **State:** Canceled
- **Labels:** `wontfix`, `Bug`
- **Priority:** No priority
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** unassigned
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-218/a-providers-oembed-response-is-trusted-into-an-img-src-with-no

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213) — Derive the thumbnail lookup from detectSource instead of matching URLs twice
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A08 Software and Data Integrity Failures** — a third party's response is trusted without a check.

## The weakness

Two gaps compound in the same lookup.

**The response is trusted.** `thumbnail_url` is taken from the oEmbed JSON, type-checked as a string, and nothing else — no scheme check, no host check:

```
apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts:88   thumbnail: typeof thumbnailUrl === 'string' ? thumbnailUrl : undefined
```

That value becomes the cover's `src` — `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:376-384`, the `<Player.Poster>` wrapping `<VideoCoverImage src={coverSrc}>`, with `coverSrc` from `useVideoThumbnail` (`:26` for the import). `BackpackVideoHoverPreview` does the same at `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video-hover-preview.tsx:187`.

**The host match that decides whether to look up at all is a substring test**, not a host comparison:

```
apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts:16   /youtube\.com\/watch\?.*v=|youtu\.be\/|youtube\.com\/shorts\//.test(url)
apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts:18   const isVimeoUrl = (url: string): boolean => /vimeo\.com\//.test(url);
```

Any URL merely *containing* `vimeo.com/` matches — `https://attacker.example/?ref=vimeo.com/` does. Core's own `detectSource` gets this right, comparing `url.hostname` against a fixed list (`packages/core/src/source-detection.ts:66-69`, `isVimeoHost`). The thumbnail path reimplements the question and reimplements it more loosely.

## The path that reaches it

Any `BackpackVideo` or `BackpackVideoHoverPreview` with a YouTube or Vimeo `url` and no `placeholderImageSrc`.

## Impact if exploited

React escapes the attribute, so this is not XSS, and `javascript:` in an `img src` is inert. What remains is real but narrower: whoever controls the oEmbed response — the provider itself, a compromise of it, or anyone in a position to answer for it — chooses an arbitrary URL that every viewer's browser then fetches. That is a tracking pixel with the page's referrer, or a request to an internal host if the page runs somewhere that resolves one. The loose matcher widens who gets asked in the first place: an unrelated URL containing the substring is sent to Vimeo's oEmbed as a query parameter, disclosing that URL to Vimeo.

## Related, and deliberately not duplicated

[SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213) already asks for the thumbnail lookup to be derived from `detectSource` instead of matching URLs twice. That change would close the matcher half of this issue as a side effect. This issue is filed for the half it would not close — trusting `thumbnail_url` — and should be resolved together with it rather than separately.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

Parse `thumbnail_url` and accept it only as an `https:` URL on the provider's own image hosts, discarding anything else the way every other failure in this hook already resolves to `undefined`.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

**Comments** (1)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

**Absorbed into [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213), not rejected.** The substance stands and is now an acceptance criterion there: `thumbnail_url` must be accepted only as an `https:` URL on the provider's own image hosts, with anything else resolving to `undefined`.

Why fold rather than keep both: 213 rewrites the same ~40 lines of `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-thumbnail.ts` that this issue asks to harden, including the substring matchers this issue also flags. Two open issues over the same lines invites the second agent to re-litigate the first's design.

Two corrections to what this issue claimed, both carried across to 213 so they are not lost:

1. **The impact bound was missing.** `video-thumbnail.ts` lives in `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/` — the compat wrapper, not a published package. Nothing a Reely consumer installs contains this code. That is why the folded work is `Improvement` at Medium priority rather than a security escalation.
2. **The cover renderer was verified, not assumed.** It is a plain `<img src>` (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/video-cover-image.tsx:68,70`) with no inline style and no `background-image`, so there is no CSS-injection path. The residual harm is an arbitrary cross-origin fetch, which is what this issue said — now checked rather than reasoned.

The substring-matcher half was confirmed empirically during triage: `https://attacker.example/?ref=vimeo.com/x` fires a Vimeo oEmbed request while `detectSource` rejects the same string. Evidence table is on 213.

No `.out-of-scope/` entry: that knowledge base records *rejected* requests, and this one is being actioned.

Closing as absorbed. Follow [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213).

<a id="sidepro-219"></a>

## SIDEPRO-219 — Nothing states what a Reely page must allow in its CSP, or what a substituted provider script would reach

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-219/nothing-states-what-a-reely-page-must-allow-in-its-csp-or-what-a

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — The Vimeo chromeless probe fires on attach whether or not custom controls are wanted
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A08 Software and Data Integrity Failures**, with **A05 Security Misconfiguration** for the missing guidance.

## The weakness

Reely injects a third-party script into the consumer's own page, with no integrity attribute and no documentation of the consequence:

```
packages/provider-youtube/src/loader.ts:67    const scriptSrc = 'https://www.youtube.com/iframe_api';
packages/provider-youtube/src/loader.ts:128   script.src = scriptSrc;
```

The script is appended to `document.head` (`:130`) and runs with the page's full privileges — same origin, same DOM, same cookies. No `integrity` and no `crossOrigin` are set. Subresource Integrity is genuinely not available here: YouTube serves `iframe_api` unversioned and mutable, so a hash would break on their next deploy. That makes it a risk to state, not a bug to fix.

A grep of the repo finds no mention of `Content-Security-Policy` anywhere in `docs/`, the README, or any package README. A consumer therefore has to discover by trial which origins to allow, and gets no warning that one of the origins they allow can execute code.

## The path that reaches it

Any YouTube source, whenever the provider loads.

## Impact if exploited

If `www.youtube.com` served a substituted or compromised `iframe_api`, that code would already be inside the consumer's page: it could read the DOM, exfiltrate anything the page holds, and act as the user. This is the standard third-party-script bargain, but here the consumer never agreed to it explicitly, because nothing told them.

## What the audit found in good order, and should not be changed by a fix here

* The other three providers do better and are worth preserving as the pattern: Wistia and Vimeo ship their SDKs as npm dependencies (`@wistia/wistia-player`, `@vimeo/player`), and Wistia's loader resolves the custom-element registration rather than pulling remote code (`packages/provider-wistia/src/loader.ts:101-138`).
* Every runtime dependency of every published package is pinned to an exact version — `@wistia/wistia-player` at `0.7.12`, `@vimeo/player` at `2.30.4`, `hls.js` at `1.6.16` — not a caret range. That is the right call for supply-chain integrity and should stay.
* `scriptSrc` is a module constant, so the `querySelector` that reuses an existing tag (`loader.ts:91-93`) interpolates nothing attacker-controlled.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

A documented CSP section — the `script-src`, `frame-src`, `img-src` and `connect-src` origins each provider needs, and the note that the YouTube script cannot carry SRI — would let a consumer make the decision deliberately. Whether that lives in the README, in `docs/`, or per-provider is a call for triage.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

## Absorbed from [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — the nocookie default does not cover the script

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Verified during triage on 2026-08-05, and it belongs here because this issue already owns that script:

The API script is fetched from `https://www.youtube.com/iframe_api` (`packages/provider-youtube/src/loader.ts:67`) **even when** `host` **is the** `youtube-nocookie.com` **default** (`packages/provider-youtube/src/index.ts:103`). The nocookie host governs the embed the iframe API then builds; it does not govern where the API itself comes from. So a request to the cookie-bearing domain happens for every YouTube source regardless, and nothing in the repo says so.

This is not fixable by changing the origin — YouTube serves the API from that one host. It is fixable only by stating it, which is what this issue is for.

**One correction carried across from** [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217)**, which claimed this fires before any interaction.** It does not, on the path that issue named: `BackpackVideo` computes `loading = 'interaction'` for its ordinary light-mode path (`apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:493-497`), so the loader does not run until the click. It *does* fire before any interaction under `Player.Root`'s own default, `loading = 'viewport'` (`packages/react/src/root.tsx:99`), where the provider attaches on scroll into view. Document the real trigger, not the one originally claimed.

## Added acceptance criteria

- [ ] The documentation states that a YouTube source causes a request to `www.youtube.com` for the API script, that this is independent of the `host` option, and that `youtube-nocookie.com` therefore limits the embed rather than every request
- [ ] It states when that request happens, in terms of the `loading` strategy — on scroll into view under the `viewport` default, on the first interaction under `interaction`, at mount under `eager`
- [ ] It states the same for every other third-party request a player makes, so a consumer can reason about a page without reading the source: the Vimeo oEmbed probe (see [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217)), Wistia's runtime asset loads, and the storybook wrapper's thumbnail lookup (see [SIDEPRO-213](#sidepro-213))
- [ ] It names the `script-src`, `frame-src`, `img-src` and `connect-src` origins per provider, and states plainly that the YouTube script cannot carry SRI because YouTube serves it unversioned

**Comments** (2)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-219-nothing-states-what-a-reely-page-must-allow-in-its-csp-or`, based on `backpack-parity`.

One decision the brief left to triage — "whether that lives in the README, in `docs/`, or per-provider" — settled as: a single new `docs/third-party-requests.md`, linked from the root README's `## Docs` and `## Honesty about providers` sections, with one-line pointers from the YouTube, Vimeo and Wistia provider READMEs. The acceptance criteria need one place naming every provider's origins across several packages, which a per-provider README cannot hold, and `docs/` already carries cross-cutting reference in `docs/backpack-parity.md`.

Note for accuracy: [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) landed earlier in this session (`c0ff5a5`), so the Vimeo oEmbed probe is now opt-in behind `VimeoProviderOptions.customControls`. The documentation will state that post-`c0ff5a5` truth rather than the behaviour this issue's body describes.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed in https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/163 (squash-merged into `backpack-parity` as `4625c9d`).

`docs/third-party-requests.md` is the new single source, linked from the root README's `## Docs` and `## Honesty about providers` sections with one-line pointers from the YouTube, Vimeo and Wistia provider READMEs. It carries the per-provider origins table (`script-src`, `frame-src`, `img-src`, `connect-src`, `media-src`), when each request leaves the page under each `loading` strategy, the SRI bargain stated rather than softened, what the audit found in good order, and a worked example CSP with instructions for unioning it across providers. Documentation only — no runtime code changed.

**Two facts the audit found that this issue did not know about.** `packages/react/src/provider-loaders.ts` calls `createYouTubeProvider(media, source.videoId)` with no options object, so **YouTube's `host` is unreachable from `Player.Root`** — through it the embed is always `youtube-nocookie.com`, and only a direct `createYouTubeProvider` caller could point it elsewhere. The same shape applies to Vimeo's options after [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217), which means the oEmbed probe never fires through the React path at all. Both are recorded in the page, and **no open issue covers closing either gap** — worth filing when work is next planned.

**A `style-src` section was added beyond the stated criteria**, because the page's own purpose demanded it. ADR-0001 commits primitives to inline structural geometry; client-side React applies it through the CSSOM, which CSP does not govern, but server-rendered markup emits it as a literal `style="..."` attribute, which `style-src` does. `tests/integrations/next-image/test.mjs:110` asserts `position: 'absolute'` before hydration, which is only observable if the server emitted the attribute. An SSR consumer under a strict policy therefore needs `style-src 'unsafe-inline'` or nonces.

**Four factual errors were caught in review and corrected**, each verified against source:

- The Wistia `frame-src` reasoning was backwards. The first draft claimed Aurora renders no iframe because the `api-ready` handshake times out on the legacy path. It does render one — the bundle writes `<iframe src>` into its shadow root from the E-v1 host, and `packages/provider-wistia/src/attachment.ts:37-39` already said so. The timeout governs Reely's attach-failure reporting, not the browser's frame load. `fast.wistia.net` is now in `frame-src`.
- `Player.LoadingError` does not exist — the export is `ActivationButton` (`packages/react/src/loading-error.tsx:28`).
- The storybook wrapper's oEmbed request is `light`-gated and `light` defaults to `false` (`backpack-video.tsx:459`), so by default it makes no request. The page now says so rather than implying it always fires.
- The `'unsafe-inline'` claim was unqualified; it is now scoped to `script-src`.

Also fixed: glossary compliance (`CONTEXT.md` prescribes "Aurora" over "the Wistia SDK"), an issue reference in place of a bare commit SHA, and the page registered in `scripts/docs-examples.mjs` so any future `ts` snippet in it is checked.

**Left open deliberately, and marked as such in the page** rather than guessed: the collection origin for Wistia's Mux Data beacon, which is fetched at runtime and not present in the shipped bundle.

**Verification.** `pnpm docs:check`, `pnpm test:docs` 23/23, `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test` 1175/1175 across 62 files — all clean.

<a id="sidepro-220"></a>

## SIDEPRO-220 — The Vimeo iframe sets no referrer policy and grants encrypted-media unconditionally

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** Provider hardening
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-220/the-vimeo-iframe-sets-no-referrer-policy-and-grants-encrypted-media

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-266](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/221) — The referrer policy [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) added covers only the Vimeo embed; YouTube and Wistia still send the full page URL
  - [SIDEPRO-261](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/216) — docs/third-party-requests.md is now wrong about what Player.Root can reach, and silent on what [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220) changed
  - [SIDEPRO-260](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/215) — The Vimeo SDK sends the embedding page's full URL to player.vimeo.com, defeating [SIDEPRO-220](#sidepro-220)'s stated aim
  - [SIDEPRO-216](#sidepro-216) — The YouTube host option chooses the embed origin and is never validated
  - [SIDEPRO-215](#sidepro-215) — Wistia presentation options reach element attributes with no validation

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A05 Security Misconfiguration.**

## The weakness

The Vimeo embed builds its iframe by hand and sets four attributes — `src`, `allow`, `allowfullscreen`, `title`:

```
packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:247   iframe.src = vimeoEmbedUrl(source, options, mount.muted);
packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:248-251   iframe.setAttribute('allow', 'autoplay; fullscreen; picture-in-picture; encrypted-media');
```

Two things are worth a decision:

* **No** `referrerpolicy`**.** The embedding page's full URL — path and query — is sent to Vimeo with the iframe request. The provider deliberately sets `dnt=1` by default (`packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:62`, `url.searchParams.set('dnt', …)`), so the intent to limit tracking is already explicit in this code; the referrer is the hole left in it. A `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` or `origin` policy would keep the embed working while sending only the origin.
* `encrypted-media` **is granted to every embed.** It is in the permission list unconditionally, whether or not the video is DRM-protected. The other three permissions are load-bearing (`autoplay`, `fullscreen`, `picture-in-picture` each back a feature Reely exposes); this one is the only member of the list nothing in the library needs.

## The path that reaches it

Every Vimeo source, on attach.

## Impact if exploited

Neither is a vulnerability on its own; together they are a wider grant than the design intends. The referrer disclosure means a page whose URL is itself sensitive — a preview link, a URL carrying an identifier — hands that URL to Vimeo on every render, which contradicts the `dnt` default sitting three lines away. The `encrypted-media` grant enables an EME capability in a frame that has no declared need for one; the honest position is either to need it or not to ask.

## Impact bound worth noting

The YouTube embed is not affected: its iframe is constructed by YouTube's own iframe API, not by this repo, so its attributes are not Reely's to set. The Wistia embed is a custom element rather than an iframe.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

Set `referrerpolicy` on the iframe, and drop `encrypted-media` unless a DRM scenario is actually supported — in which case it belongs behind the option that turns that scenario on.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement
**Summary:** Set a referrer policy on the Vimeo iframe and drop the `encrypted-media` permission nothing in the library needs

**Decision taken during triage.** Both halves of the body's "suggested direction" are adopted: set `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"`, and **remove** `encrypted-media` from the `allow` list. Keeping the grant against future DRM support was considered and rejected — if a DRM scenario is ever supported, the grant belongs behind the option that enables it, not standing open in advance.

**Verified during triage.** The Vimeo embed's iframe is built by this repo and sets exactly three attributes beside `src`: `allow`, `allowfullscreen`, `title`. There is no `referrerpolicy`. `encrypted-media` is in the `allow` list unconditionally, three lines from the deliberate `dnt=1` default — the intent to limit tracking is already explicit in this code, and the referrer is the gap left in it.

**Current behavior:**

Every Vimeo attach produces an iframe with no referrer policy, so the embedding page's full URL — path and query — is sent to Vimeo with the request. The `allow` list grants `autoplay`, `fullscreen`, `picture-in-picture`, and `encrypted-media`. The first three each back a feature Reely exposes; the fourth backs nothing in the library.

**Desired behavior:**

The iframe declares `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"`, so Vimeo receives the page's origin and not its path or query. The `allow` list drops `encrypted-media` and keeps the three load-bearing permissions.

Neither change may alter playback. `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` still sends an origin, which is what Vimeo's embed needs for domain-restricted videos, so private or domain-locked sources must keep working — verify this rather than assuming it, since it's the one way this change could regress a real consumer.

**Key interfaces:**

- The Vimeo provider's iframe construction step — where `allow`, `allowfullscreen`, and `title` are set. Both changes land here.
- `VimeoProviderOptions` — unchanged. Neither change introduces an option, and the referrer policy is not consumer-configurable.
- `docs/third-party-requests.md` — it accounts for every third-party origin a Reely page reaches. If it documents what the Vimeo iframe sends, the referrer change makes that description stale; update it if so.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] The Vimeo iframe carries `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"`
- [ ] The `allow` list contains `autoplay`, `fullscreen`, and `picture-in-picture`, and does not contain `encrypted-media`
- [ ] Fullscreen and picture-in-picture still work on a real Vimeo source
- [ ] Autoplay still works on a muted Vimeo source
- [ ] A domain-restricted or private Vimeo source still loads — the origin is still sent, only the path is withheld
- [ ] The `dnt` default is untouched
- [ ] A test asserts the iframe's attributes, so a future edit cannot silently reinstate the grant or drop the policy
- [ ] `docs/third-party-requests.md` is accurate about what the Vimeo iframe sends, if it describes this at all
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, and `pnpm lint` stay green

**Out of scope:**

- The YouTube iframe. Its attributes are set by YouTube's own iframe API, not by this repo, so they are not Reely's to change
- The Wistia embed, which is a custom element rather than an iframe
- Adding DRM support, or an option that would need `encrypted-media` back
- Making the referrer policy consumer-configurable
- A Content-Security-Policy change; the CSP documentation is its own concern

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-220-the-vimeo-iframe-sets-no-referrer-policy-and-grants`, cut from `main` at `a2562c5`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed. PR https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/174, squash-merged to `main` as `0cd318d` with all ten required checks green (`static`, `unit`, `e2e` across chromium/firefox/webkit, `package`, `storybook`, `visual`, `hls-paths`; `hls-native-webkit` skips).

**What changed.** `packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts` now sets `referrerpolicy="strict-origin-when-cross-origin"` on the embed iframe, and its `allow` list is `autoplay; fullscreen; picture-in-picture` — `encrypted-media` dropped. A test in `packages/provider-vimeo/test/index.test.ts` asserts both, so a future edit cannot silently reinstate the grant or drop the policy.

**Two decisions worth recording.**

1. **The changeset is `minor`, not `patch`.** The referrer half is a narrowing of what the embed sends, but the `encrypted-media` half is a capability withdrawal: a Vimeo Enterprise/OTT source needing EME for DRM playback relied on that grant to call `requestMediaKeySystemAccess` from inside the iframe, and stops playing after this change where it played before. That is unlike the two closest precedents (`youtube-host-allowlisted.md`, `wistia-presentation-options-validated.md`), which narrowed handling of already-invalid input and shipped as `patch`. Every package is still at `0.0.0` with `first-prerelease` unreleased, so under 0.x `minor` is the channel a breaking change travels on. The changeset body states the break risk plainly rather than describing both halves as one kind of change.

2. **`docs/third-party-requests.md` was deliberately left untouched.** The brief asked to update it *if* it describes what the Vimeo iframe sends. Both review axes checked independently: the file accounts for CSP origins and the `dnt` default and has never described referrer headers or the `allow` list for any provider, so the change makes nothing in it stale. Its `attachment.ts:60`/`:63` citations still land — the edit sits inside `start()`, below every line the doc cites.

**Acceptance criteria not verifiable here.** Three criteria need a live browser and a real Vimeo source: fullscreen/picture-in-picture still working, autoplay on a muted source, and a domain-restricted or private source still loading. The reasoning was verified rather than the behaviour — `player.vimeo.com` is always HTTPS, so `strict-origin-when-cross-origin` never hits the downgrade case that would suppress the referrer entirely, and the origin Vimeo's domain-restriction check needs is still sent on every request. Worth a real-source check if one is convenient.

`VimeoProviderOptions` is unchanged, the `dnt` default is untouched, and YouTube and Wistia were out of scope.
