<a id="sidepro-221"></a>

## SIDEPRO-221 — Two dependency advisories are open and nothing in CI would have told us

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** Medium
- **Milestone:** Provider hardening
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-221/two-dependency-advisories-are-open-and-nothing-in-ci-would-have-told

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-241](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/197) — OWASP sweep over the Provider hardening milestone
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209) — OWASP sweep over the Backpack parity milestone
  - [SIDEPRO-272](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/227) — No install cooldown is configured, and the exclude list makes it look like there is one
  - [SIDEPRO-271](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/226) — The CI audit gate's only axis is severity, so it fails loudest on the code that never ships

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Filed by the OWASP sweep on [SIDEPRO-209](#sidepro-209). **OWASP category: A06 Vulnerable and Outdated Components.**

## The weakness

`pnpm audit` reports two advisories today. Both are transitive and both are dev-only, which bounds the impact sharply — but nothing in the repo runs the check, so the next one lands unnoticed too.

| Severity | Package | Installed | Patched | Advisory |
| -- | -- | -- | -- | -- |
| High | `brace-expansion` | 5.0.8 | 5.0.9 or later | DoS via unbounded intermediate arrays, bypassing the CVE-2026-14257 mitigation (CWE-400, CWE-770) |
| Moderate | `postcss` | 8.5.21 | 8.5.23 or later | Incomplete fix of GHSA-6g55-p6wh-862q — an attacker-controlled `sourceMappingURL` reads arbitrary `.map` files when `from` is unset (CWE-22, CWE-200) |

## The path that reaches them

Both are development tooling, verified rather than assumed:

* `brace-expansion` ← `minimatch` ← `eslint` / `typescript-eslint` — reached only through `devDependencies` (`pnpm why brace-expansion`).
* `postcss` ← `vite` ← `@vitejs/plugin-react` and `@vitest/coverage-v8`, 59 paths, all through the test and build toolchain.

Neither is a runtime dependency of any published package. Checked directly: `@reely/core` and `@reely/provider-native` declare no runtime dependencies at all, and the rest declare only workspace packages plus one pinned SDK each.

## Impact if exploited

For a consumer of the published packages: none. Neither package ships. The exposure is to this repo's own contributors and to CI — a crafted lint input could stall a run, and the postcss path is a file-read primitive against whatever the build process can read. Both are worth patching on the ordinary upgrade cadence, not urgently.

The finding with the longer tail is the second one: `pnpm audit` is not run by anything. The next advisory to appear — including one that does land in a published package — arrives with nobody watching.

## Suggested direction, not a decision

Bump both, then decide whether an audit step belongs in CI and at what severity threshold it should fail. A threshold that fails on a dev-only moderate would have failed today, which is either the point or too noisy — a maintainer call, not an agent's.

Not fixed here — the sweep reports and does not repair.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** enhancement
**Summary:** Patch both open advisories and add a CI audit step that fails on high or critical, so the next advisory does not arrive unnoticed

**Decision taken during triage.** The threshold is **high and above**. Failing on moderate was considered and rejected: today's dev-only `postcss` advisory would have turned CI red, and a gate that fires routinely on transitive dev dependencies gets ignored, which defeats it. Auditing manually was also rejected — the body's actual point is that nobody is watching, and a bump alone leaves that true.

**Verified during triage.** `pnpm audit` still reports exactly two, unchanged from when the sweep filed this:

| Severity | Package | Vulnerable | Patched |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| high | `brace-expansion` | `>=4.0.0 <5.0.9` | `>=5.0.9` |
| moderate | `postcss` | `<=8.5.22` | `>=8.5.23` |

Nothing in the workflows directory or the root package scripts runs `pnpm audit` or `npm audit`. The claim that no CI step would have caught these holds.

**Current behavior:**

Two advisories are open, both transitive and both reachable only through development tooling, so neither ships to a consumer of the published packages. No automated check reports them, so the next advisory — including one that does reach a published package — appears with nobody watching.

**Desired behavior:**

Both advisories are patched by raising the transitive versions to at least the patched ones, and CI runs an audit that fails the build on a high or critical advisory while letting moderate and below pass.

The audit step must be **honest about what it lets through**: a moderate advisory should still be visible in the run's output rather than suppressed, so a maintainer reading a green build can still see it exists. A gate that hides what it tolerates is worse than no gate.

**Key interfaces:**

- The workspace's dependency resolution — both packages are transitive, so patching means an override, a resolution pin, or bumping the direct dependencies that pull them in. Prefer bumping the direct dependency where that reaches a patched version, and reach for an override only where it does not: an override outlives the reason it was added, and needs a comment saying what it is for.
- The CI workflow — a new step, alongside the existing lint and test steps rather than replacing any of them.
- Root `package.json` scripts — a named script (matching the existing `test:*` naming) is preferable to inlining the command in the workflow, so a maintainer can run the same check locally.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] `pnpm audit` reports zero high or critical advisories
- [ ] `brace-expansion` resolves to `>=5.0.9` throughout the workspace
- [ ] `postcss` resolves to `>=8.5.23` throughout the workspace
- [ ] A CI step runs the audit and fails the build on high or critical
- [ ] The same check is runnable locally through a named script
- [ ] A moderate or low advisory does not fail the build but is still visible in the step's output
- [ ] Any version override added carries a comment naming the advisory it addresses
- [ ] `pnpm test`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, and `pnpm build` stay green after the bumps
- [ ] No published package gains a runtime dependency as a result

**Out of scope:**

- Adding Dependabot, Renovate, or any other automated update service — that is a separate decision
- Raising the threshold to moderate later; if the high-only gate proves too loose, that is a follow-up
- Auditing anything beyond npm advisories — no license or supply-chain scanning
- The other Provider hardening issues in this milestone

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session.

Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-221-two-dependency-advisories-are-open-and-nothing-in-ci-would`

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-06*

Landed: https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/175 — squash-merged with all ten required checks green.

Both floors in `pnpm-workspace.yaml` were already overrides, just set low enough to admit a vulnerable version (`brace-expansion@<5.0.8`, `postcss@<8.5.18`) with the lockfile sitting on one. Raising them past the patched releases re-resolved to `brace-expansion@5.0.9` and `postcss@8.5.25`. `pnpm audit` now reports no known vulnerabilities.

A `test:audit` root script and a step in the `static` job close the "nobody is watching" half:

```
pnpm audit; pnpm audit --audit-level high >/dev/null
```

Two runs, because `--audit-level` filters what pnpm **prints** as well as what it exits non-zero on — a single filtered run reduced a moderate advisory to a bare `Severity: 1 moderate` with no package name, patched range, or advisory link, which would have failed the brief's honesty requirement. Both properties were proved on fixture trees rather than argued: moderate-only → full table printed, exit 0; pinned back to `brace-expansion@5.0.8` → exit 1.

It is a step in `static` rather than its own job because `main`'s required checks are a fixed list, so a new job would gate nothing until someone added it there. The Standards review suggested a separate job and this is why it was declined.

Two things worth knowing, neither changing the work:

- **`postcss` is not strictly dev-only**, contrary to this issue's body. `pnpm why -r postcss` finds a path `postcss → webpack → dotenv-webpack → @wistia/wistia-player → @reely/provider-wistia`, inside a published package's runtime tree. `pnpm audit --prod` is clean and the raised floor covers that path too, so nothing here differs — but the "neither package ships" framing was wrong for `postcss`.
- **The gate carries no `--ignore-registry-errors`**, so a registry outage will red `static`. Suppressing that would make the gate pass on "unknown", which is the opposite of its point. Worth a deliberate decision if `static` starts flaking, not a gap left here.

`pnpm test` (1083 tests), `typecheck`, `lint`, `build`, `format:check` and the whole `package` suite were green locally before the PR opened, and `/review` came back clean on the Spec axis (all nine acceptance criteria verified by running commands) with no hard violations on Standards.

## SIDEPRO-222 — Audit SeekSlider and Time against what a full control bar needs

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-222/audit-seekslider-and-time-against-what-a-full-control-bar-needs

**Relations**

- **Blocks:**
  - [SIDEPRO-227](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/183) — No hover scrub preview, and no thumbnail source to build one from
  - [SIDEPRO-226](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/182) — No chapter vocabulary, so a seek bar cannot show chapter markers
  - [SIDEPRO-225](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/181) — No keyboard shortcut layer for playback
  - [SIDEPRO-224](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/180) — Seeking on a live stream has no DVR window or live edge
- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) — The background-image guard matches its own doc comments, so the e2e suite is red on every branch
  - [SIDEPRO-240](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/196) — Volume slider is dropped, not folded, below 420px in the reference composition
  - [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) — Let a consumer choose between Reely-drawn controls and the provider's own chrome
  - [SIDEPRO-239](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/195) — Backpack wrapper's control bar omits SeekSlider, Time, MuteButton, VolumeSlider, and FullscreenButton
  - [SIDEPRO-238](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/194) — Responsive control folding in the reference composition is hand-rolled and duplicates markup
  - [SIDEPRO-237](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/193) — SettingsMenuContent ships a scrollable-region-focusable axe violation by default
  - [SIDEPRO-236](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/192) — Reference composition renders both sliders as unstyled native range inputs
  - [SIDEPRO-235](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/191) — Buffered-progress indicator renders nothing in the reference composition
  - [SIDEPRO-234](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/190) — Default seek-slider track color fails WCAG 1.4.11 contrast
  - [SIDEPRO-233](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/189) — Buffered-range indicator has no non-visual exposure to assistive technology
  - [SIDEPRO-232](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/188) — Idle SeekSlider reports a misleading 0:00 value with no disabled state
  - [SIDEPRO-231](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/187) — Only the HLS provider populates PlayerState.live
  - [SIDEPRO-230](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/186) — PlayerState.seeking cannot distinguish a user seek from a programmatic seek
  - [SIDEPRO-229](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/185) — SeekSlider seeks continuously with no preview or commit-on-release mode

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Improvement
**Summary:** Reely already ships `Player.SeekSlider` and `Player.Time`, but nothing composes them into a real control bar and no one has established what they cover. Four planned pieces of control-bar work are blocked on knowing.

**Why this exists:** during a design session it was asserted twice that Reely had no seek-bar or time-display primitive. That was wrong — both exist in `packages/react/src/transport-controls.tsx` and both are exported. The error was caught only when the file was read directly. Before designing live/DVR seeking, a keyboard layer, chapters or scrub thumbnails on top of them, someone has to establish what is actually there.

**What exists, verified:**

`packages/react/src/transport-controls.tsx` exports `PlayButton`, `MuteButton`, `VolumeSlider`, `SeekSlider` and `Time`; `packages/react/src/display-controls.tsx` exports `AirPlayButton`, `FullscreenButton`, `PipButton`. All are re-exported from `packages/react/src/index.tsx`.

`SeekSlider` (`:167`) already:

* gates on `state.capabilities.seek.status`
* renders buffered ranges — parts `seek-buffered` and `seek-buffered-range`, `aria-hidden`
* carries `aria-label="Seek"` and an `aria-valuetext` of `` `${formatTime(value)} of ${formatTime(duration)}` `` when a duration is known
* exposes parts `seek-slider` and `seek-slider-input`, and documents an escape hatch onto the inner range control at `:145-148`

`Time` (`:246`) takes `type = 'current'`, renders part `time`, and formats through a module-local `formatTime` (`:6`).

`PlayerState` (`packages/core/src/types.ts:106-138`) already publishes `currentTime`, `duration`, `buffered`, `seekable`, `seeking` and `live`.

**What the audit must answer.** Each of these gates a downstream issue, so answer them concretely, with `file:line` evidence, not impressions:

- [ ] **Compose the bar.** Build a story that composes every existing control into one bar — play, seek, time, mute, volume, captions, settings, PiP, AirPlay, fullscreen. Does it work? What is missing or awkward once they are side by side?
- [ ] **Scrub semantics.** Does `SeekSlider` seek continuously while dragging, or preview and commit on release? Which does each provider tolerate? Is `PlayerState.seeking` sufficient to distinguish a user drag from a programmatic seek?
- [ ] `Time` **coverage.** What values does `type` accept? Can it show duration and remaining, not just current? What does it render when `duration` is `null` (a live stream)?
- [ ] **Live.** What does `SeekSlider` do on a live source today — does it measure against `duration` (null) or `seekable`? This is the finding that shapes the live/DVR issue.
- [ ] **Keyboard.** What keyboard interaction does `SeekSlider` support today, via the native range input or otherwise? What is already conformant and what is not?
- [ ] **Why the Backpack wrapper ignored them.** `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:399-404` renders only `<Player.PlayButton />` inside `Player.Controls`, and the parity matrix recorded a single-button bar as a deliberate divergence. Establish whether `SeekSlider` was unavailable when that was written, unsuitable, or simply overlooked. The answer decides whether the wrapper is a bug or a choice.
- [ ] **Accessibility.** WCAG 2.2 AA is a release gate for this library. Audit both primitives against it and report specific failures, not a verdict.

**Deliverable:** the composed-bar story, plus findings written to `docs/superpowers/specs/` — one section per question above. File any defect you find as its own `needs-triage` issue rather than fixing it here; this issue reports.

**Out of scope:**

* Fixing anything you find, beyond what the composed story needs to render
* Live/DVR, keyboard layer, chapters, scrub thumbnails — each has its own issue, blocked by this one
* Any provider package

**Context:** `.out-of-scope/as-child.md` records that primitives render fixed elements and children are the extension point — no `asChild`. Any composition you propose has to live inside that constraint.

**The premise was wrong.** This brief says nothing composes `SeekSlider` and `Time` into a real control bar. `apps/storybook/stories/reference/reference-player.tsx:409-455` already composes all ten controls, both `Time` variants included. ## Findings

Filed as their own issues: [SIDEPRO-229](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/185), [230](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/186), [231](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/187), [232](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/188), [233](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/189), [234](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/190), [235](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/191), [236](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/192), [237](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/193), [238](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/194), [239](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/195), and [SIDEPRO-240](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/196) from the review pass.

Folded onto issues that already cover them, as comments: [SIDEPRO-223](#sidepro-223) (1 finding), [SIDEPRO-224](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/180) (3), [SIDEPRO-225](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/181) (3).

The single finding with the widest reach is [SIDEPRO-231](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/187): only the HLS provider populates `PlayerState.live` (`packages/core/src/player-controller.ts:62`). Native, YouTube, Vimeo and Wistia never do, so every live-related shortfall is latent everywhere except HLS.

## Gates

`pnpm test` 1188 passed. `pnpm test:storybook` 137 passed and unchanged, which is correct because no story was added. Typecheck, lint, format and docs clean.

`pnpm test:e2e` was not used as a gate. It is red on every branch for a reason that predates this work, now filed as [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184).

## SIDEPRO-223 — Let a consumer choose between Reely-drawn controls and the provider's own chrome

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `ready-for-agent`, `Feature`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** none
- **Created:** 2026-08-05
- **Completed:** 2026-08-05
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-223/let-a-consumer-choose-between-reely-drawn-controls-and-the-providers

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) — The background-image guard matches its own doc comments, so the e2e suite is red on every branch
  - [SIDEPRO-222](#sidepro-222) — Audit SeekSlider and Time against what a full control bar needs
  - [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210) — The loop prop is silently ignored by the YouTube and Vimeo providers
  - [SIDEPRO-214](#sidepro-214) — Fold BackpackVideo's alt into the play button's accessible name
  - [SIDEPRO-212](#sidepro-212) — A failed load presents BackpackVideo's cover click as playing video

**Description**

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

## Agent Brief

**Category:** Feature
**Summary:** A consumer cannot ask a provider to draw its own controls. Vimeo and YouTube both support it, and both are unreachable from `Player.Root`.

**Current behavior:**

Reely is headless, so "Reely draws the controls" is already the default — a consumer composes `Player.Controls` from the primitives. What has no expression is the other half: telling the provider to render its native chrome instead.

The providers are ready. `packages/provider-vimeo/src/attachment.ts:61` sets the embed's `controls` param from `options.controls === true ? '1' : '0'`, and `packages/provider-youtube` gained the same option with matching polarity (`playerVars.controls`) in commit `5b65be1`. Neither is reachable:

* `PlayerProviderOptions` (`packages/react/src/provider-loaders.ts:6-24`) has `wistia` and `youtube` keys but **no** `vimeo` **key**
* `provider-loaders.ts` passes `createVimeoProvider(media, source)` with no options at all

The visible consequence is `Backpack parity/Real/Video → WithControls`: it sets `controls: true` and gets a bar with a single play button, because `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:399-404` renders exactly `<Player.PlayButton />` inside `Player.Controls`. Backpack forwards `controls` to react-player and gets the provider's full chrome. `docs/backpack-parity.md` recorded Reely's version as a **deliberate divergence**; the maintainer has since ruled that divergence wrong.

**Desired behavior:**

A consumer can say "the provider draws its controls" and get them. When they do, Reely renders no control surface of its own — no `Player.Controls`, and no click-to-toggle overlay, which would otherwise swallow clicks meant for the provider's controls.

**Key interfaces:**

* `PlayerProviderOptions` and the loader branches (`packages/react/src/provider-loaders.ts`)
* `providerOptionsEqual` (`packages/react/src/use-activation.ts`) — see the trap below
* `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx` — the `providerOptions` memo around `:511`, the `Player.Controls` block at `:399-404`, the play-icon gate at `:388`, and the affordance table comment at `:220-236`
* Whether this belongs in per-provider bags at all is part of the work — see "The open design question"

**The trap that makes this silently fail:** `providerOptionsEqual` compares each provider bag by value to decide whether to re-attach the embed. It currently covers `wistia` and `youtube`. A `vimeo` key added without a matching line there will appear to work on first mount and then never respond to a change again. Pin it with a test that fails without the line — the `youtube` comparison added in `5b65be1` is the template.

**The open design question, which this issue should settle:** `controls` is a **cross-provider** concept — every provider has an answer to it — so a per-provider bag makes a consumer write it once per provider and get a silent no-op for whichever key they forget. `loop` already demonstrates that failure mode: it lives in `nativeOptions` and so reaches HLS and native only, which is [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210). Decide whether `controls` gets a Reely-level home that fans out to every provider, or stays in the per-provider bags, and record the reasoning. Fanning out would also give [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210) a home.

**Acceptance criteria:**

- [ ] A consumer can make Vimeo draw its own controls through `Player.Root`, and the same for YouTube
- [ ] With provider controls on, Reely renders no `Player.Controls` and no competing click surface
- [ ] Unset or off behaves exactly as today — chromeless embed, Reely's own click-to-toggle
- [ ] Whatever bag or prop is added is compared by value where the embed's re-attachment is decided, pinned by a test that fails without it
- [ ] The affordance table comment at `backpack-video.tsx:220-236` matches the new behavior
- [ ] `docs/backpack-parity.md`'s `WithControls` row is re-verdicted and the reversed divergence entry is rewritten or removed
- [ ] The cross-provider-versus-per-provider decision is recorded, in an ADR if it sets a precedent

**Out of scope:**

* The Vimeo and YouTube provider packages — both already honour the option
* Wistia
* [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210)'s actual fix, though this issue's decision should say where `loop` would live

**Verify:** `Backpack parity/Real/Video → WithControls` shows Vimeo's own control bar with no Reely play button over it. `pnpm test:storybook` must stay at exactly 137.

**Comments** (3)

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

The [SIDEPRO-222](#sidepro-222) control-bar audit found one instance of this issue's exact failure mode, on a real story.

**Backpack wrapper's controls={true} on a YouTube source likely renders two overlapping control bars, untested.** apps/storybook/stories/backpack/backpack-video.tsx:406-413 always renders Reely's own Player.Controls, currently a PlayButton, when controls is true. Separately, backpack-video.tsx:519-530 forwards the same controls value into YouTube's playerVars.controls, at packages/provider-youtube/src/attachment.ts:154, which turns on YouTube's native embed chrome, including its own seek bar. Both render at once on a YouTube source. Every WithControls and Loop story uses a Vimeo URL, so this combination has never been rendered or checked by any test.

This belongs here because the fix this issue already scopes, letting a consumer tell the provider to draw its own chrome and suppressing Reely's Player.Controls when they do, is the same fix that resolves the double-chrome case on YouTube. Worth adding a YouTube-sourced WithControls story once the fix lands, to close the untested gap the audit found.

Found by the [SIDEPRO-222](#sidepro-222) control-bar audit. See docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-05-control-bar-audit-222-design.md.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Picked up by a Factory Loop Session. Branch: `pedrosousa13/sidepro-223-let-a-consumer-choose-between-reely-drawn-controls-and-the`, cut from `backpack-parity`.

*Pedro Sousa — 2026-08-05*

> *This was generated by AI during triage.*

Landed as https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/pull/166, squashed into `backpack-parity` as `375d889`.

**The open design question is settled**, per the maintainer: `controls` gets a Reely-level home — a `controls` prop on `Player.Root`, fanned out to whichever provider the source resolves to — rather than a key in each per-provider bag. Recorded as `docs/adr/0004-cross-provider-options-live-on-root.md`, which also states the boundary between a Reely-level and a per-provider option, and gives [SIDEPRO-210](#sidepro-210)'s `loop` the home it was missing.

The bags omit `controls` at the type level, so there is exactly one home and no precedence rule is needed. `Player.Media` omits it too — without that, `<Player.Media controls={false} />` typechecked and was then silently swallowed by the prop spread.

Acceptance criteria, all met:

- Vimeo and YouTube both draw their own controls through `Player.Root`.
- With provider controls on, the Backpack wrapper renders no `Player.Controls`, no toggle button and no play icon. That closes the double-chrome defect the [SIDEPRO-222](#sidepro-222) audit found on YouTube; a new YouTube-sourced `WithControls` story covers the combination no story had ever rendered.
- Unset and `false` behave exactly as before.
- `providerOptionsEqual` gained the `vimeo` line the brief flagged as the trap, pinned by a test that fails without it.
- The affordance table comment matches the new behavior. It has four rows rather than three: the brief's own table contradicted the play-icon gate the same brief mandated, so the contradicting row was split rather than shipping a table that lies.
- `docs/backpack-parity.md`'s `WithControls` row is re-verdicted. **It stays `partial`, for a new reason** — Backpack draws its own play icon over the provider's chrome before playback and the wrapper now draws none. By the file's own rubric that is `partial`, not `full`.

One correction to the brief, for the record: the "reversed divergence entry" the acceptance criteria asked to rewrite or remove **does not exist** in `docs/backpack-parity.md`. That claim lived only in the matrix row and a story's doc comment. Both were rewritten, with the corrected claim recorded in place per the file's own convention.

Two findings worth their own issues, neither caused by this work and neither fixed here:

1. `packages/react/src/index.tsx` re-exports `WistiaProviderOptions` and `YouTubeProviderOptions` but not `VimeoProviderOptions`, so the public API is asymmetric for a consumer typing a `providerOptions={{ vimeo: … }}` value.
2. Around 30 `backpack-video.tsx:NNN` comment references across the backpack suite are roughly 8 lines stale, and this work shifts them further.

Verification: `pnpm test` 1196/1196, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm format:check` clean, `pnpm test:storybook` exactly 137. `pnpm test:e2e` was not run — red on every branch for unrelated reasons ([SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) plus three network-dependent Vimeo failures).

<a id="sidepro-257"></a>

## SIDEPRO-257 — Land the library work from backpack-parity on main, without the Backpack parity surface

- **State:** Done
- **Labels:** `infra`, `ready-for-agent`, `Improvement`
- **Priority:** High
- **Milestone:** Consumer-ready main
- **Created:** 2026-08-06
- **Completed:** 2026-08-06
- **Assignee:** Pedro Sousa
- **Linear:** https://linear.app/side-projects-p/issue/SIDEPRO-257/land-the-library-work-from-backpack-parity-on-main-without-the

**Relations**

- **Related to:**
  - [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217) — The Vimeo chromeless probe fires on attach whether or not custom controls are wanted
  - [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) — The background-image guard matches its own doc comments, so the e2e suite is red on every branch
  - [SIDEPRO-258](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/213) — The @real Vimeo plan tests poll customControls against stories that never opt into the probe

**Description**

## What to build

`backpack-parity` is 19 commits ahead of `main` and `main` is 0 ahead of it. Those commits mix two kinds of work. One kind ships to a consumer: a Wistia provider, Wistia source detection, cross-provider options on `Root`, external play-state control, a viewport load threshold, an opt-in Vimeo chromeless probe, a YouTube controls option. The other kind exists only to prove the first kind works: a `BackpackVideo` compat wrapper, its stories and contract tests, and a harness that measures those stories against a local clone of Backpack's own Storybook.

Put the first kind on `main` as one squashed commit. Leave the second kind where it is.

Build it by subtraction, not by re-selection. Branch from `main`, bring in `backpack-parity`'s tree wholesale, then delete only the parity surface. Subtraction is the point: a hand-picked keep-list can silently omit a library change, and a pure-delete diff cannot.

**Delete:**

* `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/` — the whole directory. The three wrappers (`BackpackVideo`, `BackpackAutoplayVideo`, `BackpackVideoHoverPreview`), their stories, their support modules, and their contract tests.
* `e2e/parity/` — the whole directory. It reads a Backpack checkout from a hard-coded local path, so it cannot run in CI.
* `playwright.parity.config.ts`, and the `test:parity` script in the root `package.json`.
* `docs/backpack-parity.md` — the parity matrix.
* The `e2e/parity/*.guard.ts` entry in `vitest.config.ts`'s `include`. Keep the `@reely/provider-wistia` alias in the same file.
* The `'Backpack parity'` entry, and the `Real`/`Mock` order nested under it, from the story sort order in `apps/storybook/.storybook/preview.tsx`.
* The `stories/backpack/` paragraphs in `apps/storybook/README.md`.

**Keep everything else**, including the parts whose only current consumer was a parity story. `reportsPlayback` on the mock player stays: `apps/storybook/stories/mock-player.contract.test.ts` covers it and it describes a real provider behaviour. `e2e/wistia-smoke.spec.ts` stays. Both changesets stay. `CONTEXT.md`'s Wistia and Aurora vocabulary stays, as do `docs/third-party-requests.md`, ADR-0003 and ADR-0004.

**Reword, do not delete, the references that point into the deleted surface.** No code imports cross the boundary — a grep for `backpack/` outside that directory finds comments and prose only — so this is six edits, and each one must still read correctly once the target is gone:

* ADR-0003 opens by naming the wrapper's directory and the parity matrix. Rewrite the opening so it attributes the parity work to the `backpack-parity` branch instead of a path on `main`. The decision it records still governs `customControls` and `loadThreshold`, so it stays on `main` and keeps its number.
* ADR-0004's `WithControls` sentence cites `docs/backpack-parity.md`. Point it somewhere that exists, or drop the citation and keep the claim.
* `docs/third-party-requests.md` cites two wrapper files as evidence for a thumbnail origin and a poster request. Restate the evidence without the paths.
* `packages/react/test/activation.test.tsx` carries two comments citing wrapper files as the scenario a test came from.
* `apps/storybook/.storybook/mock-player.tsx` has one comment citing `reporting-provider.ts` as the thing `reportsPlayback` imitates.

Leave `backpack-parity` untouched. It keeps the parity surface and its full history; nothing in this issue rewrites or deletes it.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `main` carries the library work as a single squashed commit, and `git diff` between that commit's tree and `backpack-parity` shows only the deleted parity surface and the six reworded references — no library file differs.
- [ ] `packages/provider-wistia` is on `main` as a workspace package, referenced from the root `tsconfig.json`, aliased in `vitest.config.ts` and in `apps/storybook/.storybook/main.ts`.
- [ ] `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/`, `e2e/parity/`, `playwright.parity.config.ts` and `docs/backpack-parity.md` do not exist on `main`.
- [ ] No file on `main` references a path under `apps/storybook/stories/backpack/`, `e2e/parity/`, or `docs/backpack-parity.md`.
- [ ] `pnpm format:check`, `pnpm lint`, `pnpm typecheck`, `pnpm test`, `pnpm test:docs` and `pnpm docs:check` pass.
- [ ] `pnpm build`, `pnpm test:packages`, `pnpm test:budgets`, `pnpm test:bundle` and `pnpm test:integrations` pass.
- [ ] `pnpm --filter @reely/storybook build` and `pnpm test:storybook` pass.
- [ ] The Storybook sidebar shows no `Backpack parity` section, and every remaining story renders.
- [ ] Packages stay at `0.0.0` and no changeset is consumed. Versioning and publishing are out of scope.

`pnpm test:e2e` is out of scope: e2e is red on every branch, tracked by [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184).

## Blocked by

None - can start immediately.

**Every acceptance criterion met**, plus one thing the brief did not anticipate.

## The brief's premise about e2e was half wrong

The brief put `pnpm test:e2e` out of scope on the grounds that "e2e is red on every branch, tracked by [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184)". But `main` carries branch protection with `enforce_admins: true` and the three `e2e` jobs among its required checks, so e2e was a hard merge gate here regardless of scope.

Tracing the CI history showed the redness is two separate things, not one:

- `poster.spec.ts:157` — the [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) background-image guard. First red at PR #161. **This PR fixes it as a side effect**: the guard scans visual source files, and the files that tripped it were the parity stories this PR deletes. [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184) still stands for `backpack-parity`, where those files remain.
- `vimeo.spec.ts:57`, `:107`, `:125` — **not** network flake, and not [SIDEPRO-228](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/184). First red at PR #162 (`c0ff5a5`, [SIDEPRO-217](#sidepro-217)), which made the Vimeo chromeless probe opt-in via `providerOptions.vimeo.customControls` but did not update the three e2e tests that depend on the probe running. Red on every branch since, and merged past four times because e2e is not a landing gate on `backpack-parity`.

The maintainer chose to fix the second inside this PR rather than park it. `32c330c` threads a `vimeoCustomControls` prop through `PlayerFixture` and opts `VimeoInteraction` and `VimeoUnlistedInteraction` in. The privacy fix `c0ff5a5` made is preserved — the prop defaults to off, nothing else opts in, and `vimeo.spec.ts:57`'s pre-activation "no Vimeo request" assertion still holds.

PR #170 is the first fully-green CI run in this repo since PR #160: all ten checks pass, all three e2e browsers included.

## Filed, not fixed

[SIDEPRO-258](https://github.com/pedrosousa13/reely/issues/213) — the same `c0ff5a5` defect also breaks two `@real`-tagged tests in `e2e/vimeo-smoke.spec.ts` that poll `customControls` against `VimeoFreePlan` / `VimeoPaidPlan`. Dormant, because `@real` is excluded from default runs by `grepInvert`. Out of scope here; filed `needs-triage`.

## One correction to the brief

The brief counted "six edits" of references pointing into the deleted surface. There were nine. Beyond the five files it named, `e2e/tsconfig.json` still listed the deleted `playwright.parity.config.ts` in `include` (typecheck would have failed), `apps/storybook/stories/mock-player.contract.test.ts` cited a deleted story by its Storybook title, and `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-05-control-bar-audit-222-design.md` carried literal `stories/backpack/` and `docs/backpack-parity.md` paths — that last one required by the criterion "No file on `main` references a path under…".

## Verification

Local, before push: `format:check`, `lint`, `typecheck`, `test` (1040), `test:docs`, `docs:check`, `build`, `test:packages`, `test:budgets`, `test:bundle`, `test:integrations`, `@reely/storybook build`, `test:storybook` (90). CI: all ten jobs green.

`/review` ran twice. Spec came back clean. Standards raised two prose findings — `docs/third-party-requests.md` and ADR-0004 still described the deleted wrapper in the present tense — both fixed in `3613fbd` and re-reviewed as ADDRESSED. A third scoped review of the Vimeo fix raised no Critical or Important findings against that commit itself.

Packages stay at `0.0.0`; no changeset consumed.
